# CBT — Roadmap

> Where the dashboard is going. Looser than a handoff. Items here graduate to numbered handoffs in `docs/handoffs/` when they sharpen up. Polish lives in `docs/ui-plan.md`, not here.

## Framing question

**WTF is going on in Congress?**

That's what someone opens this dashboard to answer. The product positioning isn't "track bills" or "summarize legislation." It's "make sense of what's happening." Every theme below earns its place by serving that question.

This started as a personal sense-making project. The author is figuring out what's actually happening in Congress, building the tools they wished existed, then opening those tools to anyone else asking the same question. The dashboard prioritizes comprehension over coverage. Most existing trackers fail on that axis: they show every bill and help with none.

What answering it in 30 seconds looks like, as the home page the dashboard is building toward:

- Three sentences at the top summarizing the week, generated from the underlying data
- Stage funnel showing where bills are pooling right now (the bottleneck of the moment)
- Topic mix showing what's getting worked on versus ignored
- Top 5 movers (advanced this week), top 5 stalls (went quiet), top 5 in the news
- One historical anchor: pace versus the 118th at the same point in the term

The current home page (page 1 of 157 feed rows) buries the answer six clicks deep. Reorienting around this question gives the home-page redesign a concrete target instead of guessing what the dashboard summary surface should hold.

## Lenses on the framing question

Specific questions an analyst would ask. Each is a sub-cut of "wtf is going on." Add and edit freely; the doc gets sharper as this list grows.

### Noise vs signal

Filtering the noise is what makes the framing question answerable in the first place. Sub-questions, all unlocked by ceremonial classification:

1. What's the noise rate, by chamber, by month?
2. Who makes the noise (highest ratio of ceremonial to substantive bills)?
3. Is the 119th noisier than the 118th?
4. Does noise correlate with party, seniority, swing-district status, or seat-up year?
5. Are some topics noisier than others (health awareness weeks, defense recognition resolutions)?

Cuts 1, 2, and 5 ship as analyst views the day `is_ceremonial` lands in the table. Cuts 3 and 4 need historical or member-level data first.

### Other lenses (seeded, to expand)

6. Which sponsors recycled bills from the 118th and how many landed this time?
7. What's the freshman class focused on?
8. Who introduces the most bills that go nowhere?
9. Which committees are graveyards versus paths to floor?
10. Which bills are getting press right now and from whom?
11. What's the shape of activity over time (introductions per week, enactments per month, deaths per quarter)?
12. Which sponsors are up for reelection next cycle, and what's their pass rate?
13. Which coalitions does each member belong to (caucus memberships, union endorsements, advocacy alignments), and how does that align with their voting and sponsorship behavior?

## Site assessment

Snapshot from a fresh look at https://cbt-chi-silk.vercel.app on 2026-05-12.

What's working: six views established (feed, `/sponsors`, `/stale`, `/changes`, `/president`, `/watchlist`). 15,677 bills synced, daily cron landing at 02:48 MT, stable pipeline. Topic taxonomy renders cleanly as compact tags, stage indicators give immediate progress signal. The terminal aesthetic is consistent across pages.

What's noisy or missing:

- **Ceremonial pollution is loud.** Visual count of page 1: ~14 ceremonial rows out of ~100. Mother's Day, Cinco de Mayo, 200 years of Peru diplomacy, "National Day of Reason," AAPI Mental Health Day, Glioblastoma Awareness Day, Tardive Dyskinesia Awareness Week, National Nurses Week, Eliot L. Engel Post Office renaming, Roy A. Rutherford Air Traffic Control Tower naming, Martinsville Missile land speed record recognition, "National Small Business Week," "National Postpartum Awareness Week for Communities of Color," "Arthritis Awareness Month." Every analyst stat downstream is skewed by this.
- **Zero visualizations.** Pure list views. No topic-distribution charts, no time-series, no funnel, no comparison overlays.
- **No landing moment.** Home page is the feed. There's no "what should I know right now" summary; the framing question gets no answer surface.
- **No external context.** Bills exist in isolation. Nothing surfaces which got coverage, which are being whipped, which are tied to active news cycles.
- **No historical comparisons.** Can't see this Congress versus the 118th at a glance.

## Architecture

Lives in `SKILL.md` as the information architecture rule (snapshot → hub → sub-pages). Themes below reference that rule rather than redefining it.

## Themes

### 1. Bill signal: noise classification + similarity

Direct service to the noise lens, prerequisite for the framing question, highest leverage on the list. Without this, every downstream cut is polluted.

Ceremonial classifier: one additional Gemini classification at summarize time, stored as `is_ceremonial` boolean, hidden by default with a toggle. Folds into the existing summarize call so marginal cost is near zero (rough estimate ~$4 to backfill the 15,677 corpus, then cents per month going forward). Four of the five noise sub-questions become analyst views the day this boolean lands.

Similarity clustering: bills with near-identical structure share title templates and action patterns. v1 is rule-based regex on titles ("Resolution honoring the memory of ___", "Resolution designating ___ as National ___ Day", "To rename the ___ Post Office located at ___"). Becomes the "similar bills" sub-page link off the bill hub, plus a standalone duplicate-shaped-bills view that's useful on its own (shows the post-office-renaming champion, the awareness-week king). Embedding-based v2 deferred until regex proves insufficient.

Sequence: ceremonial first (one handoff, ~1 day). Similarity v1 second (one handoff, ~1 day).

### 2. Home page redesign

Promoted from a sequencing step to a theme. The current home is the feed; the future home is the answer surface for the framing question. The 30-second sketch at the top of this doc is the brief.

This blocks reports and graphs. Both want a dashboard surface that doesn't exist yet, and shipping either one before the home page redesign means rework.

Open questions: keep the feed as a sub-route (`/feed`) or push it lower on the new home? Pagination there or full-bleed list? How much LLM-generated prose at the top before it stops feeling honest and starts feeling like a generated newsletter?

### 3. Weekly reports

A report is a packaged answer to the framing question, generated on a cadence and saved as a snapshot. Different from a live view: it's a moment in time, has commentary, and is shareable as a URL or file.

Two cuts on day one:

Bill changes: what's new this week, what died (no action for some chamber-appropriate window), what advanced (stage transitions), what got revived. Aggregated counts plus a list of the most notable individual movements. Most of this is already computable from existing data; `/changes` is a real-time version of the same thing.

Most talked about: which bills got the most external attention (news mentions, social signal, press releases). Depends on the news signal theme below. Without that data, this cut can't ship.

Open scope before sizing: storage (MDX files in the repo versus a `reports` table), trigger (cron-generated Mondays versus on-demand), authoring (pure LLM versus template with LLM commentary versus hand-written with data injected), format (read-on-site versus downloadable). The Substack draft is effectively the manual prototype.

### 4. News signal: breaking news + media attention

External data ingestion. Pull bill mentions from news sources, tag them to specific bill IDs, store with timestamps. Two surfaces:

Breaking news: real-time ticker or banner showing bills that got coverage in the last 24–48 hours. The closest the dashboard gets to a Bloomberg-style headline feed.

Media attention column: per-bill count of recent mentions, shown on the feed row and in the expanded view. Surfaces which bills are quietly getting traction.

Source options: GDELT (free, comprehensive, noisy), NewsAPI (paid, cleaner, tighter limits), or direct RSS from Politico, The Hill, Punchbowl, Roll Call (controllable, fewer surprises, more setup). The matching is the hard part, not the ingestion. Bill IDs are matchable but most coverage references the marketing title or sponsor name. Plan on iteration.

Sequence: pick a source. Build the sync. Run match accuracy against a hand-labeled sample of 100 bills. Ship breaking-news first (lower bar for usefulness). Media attention column second once matching tightens.

### 5. Graphs and visualizations

The dashboard has no visual idiom yet. Adding graphs is a design decision before it's an implementation task. Pick the idiom once, apply it across views.

Candidate first charts, ordered by analyst payoff: stage funnel on the home page (single bar chart, surfaces the bottleneck of the moment), topic distribution faceted by chamber, activity over time stacked by topic, sponsor productivity scatter (volume versus pass rate) on `/sponsors`, 119th versus 118th laws-enacted comparison on reports.

Library question: D3 directly, Recharts, Observable Plot, or hand-rolled SVG. The terminal aesthetic argues for thin styling and tight control, pushing toward Observable Plot or hand-rolled SVG over Recharts' defaults.

Sequence: pick the library. Ship the stage funnel as the first chart on the new home page. Iterate. Apply the same component pattern to later charts.

### 6. Member legislative depth

Applies the architecture pattern (snapshot → hub → sub-pages) to the member entity. Hub is `/sponsors/[bioguideId]`, thesis is "what this person works on in Congress." Voting record, sponsored bills, committee assignments, badges. Race coverage lives at theme 7's surface and connects back via a header tag, not a hub section. Snapshot lives in the row dropdown on `/sponsors` and the sponsor expansion in the bill feed (couple of badges, current top donor, last notable vote, "view full profile" button).

External data, each a sync pipeline of its own: member bio and committees (Congress.gov `/member/{bioguideId}`, free, already in your auth), endorsements and affiliations (caucus rosters mostly public, union and advocacy endorsements scattered across org sites and press releases), donors (FEC bulk or OpenSecrets API), stock trades (Capitol Trades, Unusual Whales; confirm sources), district demographics (Census API).

Don't batch these. Each is roughly the scope of the original Congress.gov sync.

#### Header indicators (links out, not embedded sections)

Two flags belong in the page header next to the name and party, both clickable to theme 7's race surface:

- "Seat up 2026" (or whichever cycle) when applicable
- "Active campaign" when the member is currently in a contested cycle

These do not get sections on the deep page. Race detail lives on `/race/[id]`; the header indicator is the bridge.

#### Endorsements and badges

The smallest piece in this theme but the most legible at a glance. Powers lens 13 and turns member names on the feed into coalition signals ("this is a DSA-endorsed, Squad-aligned Dem" versus "this is a Freedom Caucus Republican").

Sourcing tiers, easiest to hardest:

1. **Caucus memberships.** Mostly public rosters: Progressive Caucus, New Democrat Coalition, Problem Solvers, Republican Study Committee, the identity caucuses (CBC, CHC, CAPAC). Freedom Caucus is semi-secret but tracked by journalists. Hand-enter the initial set if no clean API exists; refresh quarterly.
2. **Union endorsements.** AFL-CIO, Teamsters, SEIU, UAW, IBEW, NEA. Each org publishes their cycle endorsements; sync would scrape or hand-enter per cycle.
3. **Advocacy alignments.** DSA, Justice Democrats, Indivisible (left); Club for Growth, Heritage Action, Susan B. Anthony List (right); Sierra Club, Planned Parenthood, NRA (issue). Most scattered and per-cycle.

UI: badge component sits in the page header next to the name (top 2–3 by priority) and renders the full set in a dedicated row on `/sponsors/[bioguideId]`. A 10-badge member should not crash the feed-row layout.

Schema sketch: separate `affiliations` table keyed by `bioguideId`, with `org`, `category` (caucus, union, advocacy), `source_url`, `last_verified`. Don't fold into the bills schema.

#### Order

Member bio first (free, unblocks `/sponsors/[bioguideId]` detail pages and the seat-up header indicator). Caucus badges second (small, public, immediate visual payoff). Donors third (largest analytical payoff). Stock trades and broader endorsements after that.

### 7. Races

Separate surface from member depth so neither overcrowds the other. The bridge is the header indicator on the member page; the destination is `/race/[id]` (likely a state-district-cycle pattern like `/race/CO-08-2026`).

A race page answers: when is this seat up, who's running, what do the ratings say, what's the fundraising picture, what does the district look like. Electoral context for the legislator, not a competing thesis to "what they're doing in Congress."

External data:

- Race ratings: Cook, Sabato, Inside Elections (likely manual unless one has an API; quarterly refresh)
- Candidate roster: Ballotpedia or FEC filings
- Fundraising: FEC, can layer on the donor pipeline from theme 6
- District demographics: Census API
- Polling: 538 successor, or curated; harder to source cleanly

MVP race page: rating, seat-up year, top three candidates, link back to incumbent's bio. Ship that with mostly-manual data, layer automated sources in later.

Open question: which races warrant pages? All 435 House + 33–34 Senate seats per cycle is a lot. Tiered approach: full pages for competitive races (any rating not "safe"), stubs for safe seats showing just incumbent and cycle.

### 8. CCBT parity

CCBT lags CBT by every feature that shipped after the fork: `/stale`, `/changes`, `/president`, sponsor depth, sort dropdown, fluid layout, header rename. The longer the gap, the harder the port.

Three options: inline porting (every CBT handoff that ships, follow with the CCBT equivalent in the next session), batched porting (quarterly sweep), or selective porting (only features that pass a "this matters at the state level too" filter). Doing nothing is worse than any of these.

### 9. UI polish

Lives in `docs/ui-plan.md`. Not duplicated here. Pull from there when you want a polish session.

## Sequencing

Working order, subject to your priorities:

1. **Ceremonial classifier.** Cheap, immediately visible, unblocks every downstream stat including the home page summary.
2. **Similarity v1.** Builds on ceremonial. Same regex-on-title shape.
3. **Home page redesign.** Scoping conversation first, then handoff. Sketch the layout, decide what blocks live on it, decide what happens to the feed (sub-route or pushed down).
4. **First graph (stage funnel).** Picks the visual idiom. Lives on the new home page as the first concrete answer to the framing question.
5. **Reports format scoping.** Chat, not a handoff. Decide MDX versus DB, cron versus on-demand, authoring style.
6. **First weekly report (bill changes cut).** Ship with the "most talked about" section as a placeholder.
7. **News sync pipeline.** Pick a source, build, validate matching.
8. **Breaking news view + media attention column.**
9. **Most talked about cut goes live on weekly reports.**
10. **Member bio sync.** Unblocks `/sponsors/[bioguideId]` detail pages plus the "seat up" / "active campaign" header indicators.
11. **Caucus membership data + badge component.** Smallest, most public, biggest immediate visual payoff. Powers lens 13. Schema design here determines how union and advocacy endorsements layer on later.
12. **Race surface MVP.** `/race/[id]` stubs with rating, seat-up year, candidate roster, link back to incumbent. Header indicator from step 10 needs a destination; this is it.
13. **One member-depth pipeline (donors or stocks).** Pick by analytical value.
14. **CCBT port batch.**

That's roughly six months of work at a CBT pace if you ship 2–4 handoffs a week. Don't treat it as a plan. Treat it as one defensible order.

## What this doc isn't

- Not a deadline-bound plan. Order is logical, not calendar.
- Not a feature checklist. Items here are themes; handoffs are checklist work.
- Not where polish lives. `docs/ui-plan.md` owns those.
- Not a contract. Cross things off, reorder, kill items as the dashboard tells you what it actually needs.

The "Also (…)" changelog is append-only and the pointer is frozen-per-block. Close an arc by adding a new block — which carries its own `Also notes now run through HO N` — never by editing a prior block's clause or its pointer. Each block freezes its `run through HO N` at append time, so the "running pointer" is simply whichever block is last: every distinct `run through HO N` appears exactly once, monotonic. A doc sweep therefore adds a pointer via its new block and touches no earlier one — a sweep's grep gate expects the previous block's pointer to still read 1, not 0. Mis-bumping a prior block both duplicates a pointer and violates the append-only rule (the HO 518 §1b near-miss; the HO 418/429 precedent). **A CORRECTION block is the one exception and carries NO pointer** — it corrects earlier blocks rather than closing an arc, so there is no new N to run through, and appending one with a pointer duplicates the N the block above already froze (HO 597 did exactly that; the fix was to strike the clause from the correction block, which is legitimate because a block appended in the same session by the same HO is not settled history — the prohibition protects *prior arcs'* blocks). State the decline explicitly (*"No pointer advance — … the pointer stands at HO N from the block above"*) so a later reader can tell a correction block from one that forgot its pointer.

**Also (HO 424–425), the two chamber-ideology surfaces — no bar move.** Two more surfaces off the HO 419 `member_ideology` layer, both live, no new tables. **HO 424 (`127b798`), the dashboard chamber polarization band:** a new `getPolarizationBand()` (all four chamber-party `dim1` medians independent of any member — `getMemberIdeology` is member-chamber-scoped and returns only a pair, so it couldn't be reused) feeds a `PolarizationBand` server component placed full-width between `RacesBoxTabs` and `WeeklyBand` (the battlefield moved inside `RacesBoxTabs`' non-default tab at HO 270/271, so "below the battlefield" resolved to this always-visible slot). It extends the HO 421 positioned-tick rail (**divs + CSS**, not SVG) to chamber-aggregate scale: two chamber rails (HOUSE/SENATE) on a fixed −1..+1 domain (the 2% inset), D and R median ticks, an amber gap line spanning them, the gap magnitude as the hero. **HO 425 (`910f5c4`), the `/members` polarization dotplot strip:** a new `getIdeologyStrip()` (the full scored population, one cache entry) feeds `IdeologyStrip`, a client-island SVG Wilkinson dotplot (`binw 0.02`, party-colored, strict-D/R median ticks pinned to the band's method inline since a client island can't import the server `median()`, empty-center annotation, hover name + dim1 + hub, click-through), ~180px at the top of `/members`, server-scoped to the chamber toggle only. It's the **third live hand-rolled SVG chart** (`BillsTimeSeries`, `CommitteeActivityChart`, `IdeologyStrip`); the over-time chart (below, data-gated) is the fourth, where the shared scatter scaffold gets extracted. **The finding:** both chamber gaps read **0.92** on dim1 (House and Senate) — the "Senate is the calmer chamber" assumption doesn't hold on real data (oddities); cross-checked band-vs-strip, 0 `member_ideology.chamber`-vs-`members.chamber` mismatches across 552 rows, coverage 551 scored / 1 unscored. **Member depth held at 99** — these ride as enabling ideology surface, **100 stays reserved for the member-scorecard arc**; the **over-time chart (ship 3) remains, data-gated** on a per-Congress D/R chamber-medians sync (backlog). **Also notes now run through HO 426.**

**Also (HO 427–428), the polarization-over-time chart — the ideology arc CLOSED.** The third and last cut off the HO 419 `member_ideology` layer, so the three-surface arc (band 424, dotplot 425, over-time 428) is complete. **HO 427 (`e0601dc`)** shipped the data layer: a `polarization_history` table + a manual **`sync:polarization-history`** aggregating Voteview's full `HSall_members.csv` to per-Congress/per-chamber D/R `dim1` medians (caucus, `party_code` 100/200; 190 rows, 95 Congresses/chamber, two-party from the 34th/1855) + a read-only coverage/cross-check diagnostic + a `voteview-source.ts` extension for the full HSall fetch. **HO 428-A (`83f0952`)** extracted the shared SVG scaffold to **`components/svg/`** (`ChartFrame`/`SvgAxisX`/`SvgGridY`/`SvgLegend`) — the standing "extract at the fourth chart" trigger fired — and retrofitted `BillsTimeSeries` + `CommitteeActivityChart` onto it (byte-identical render verified); `IdeologyStrip`, the client-island dotplot, is the deliberate holdout. **HO 428-B (`11f2122`)** shipped `PolarizationOverTime` on `/members` (collapsed by default, above the dotplot strip). Per HO 427's "let the real curve set the narrative": the historical **line runs to the 118th** (caucus) with the **current dot read live from `getPolarizationBand`** (registration) — the two agree to three decimals (0.92/0.92) so the caucus/registration seam is invisible but real (not line-connected across 2023→2025); the **midcentury low is computed per chamber — House 1947, Senate 1943 (gaps 0.50/0.47), not the mockup's shared 1961**; no prose caption. **No theme-% change** — enabling ideology surfaces; **Member depth holds at 99**, 100 reserved for the member-scorecard arc. **Also notes now run through HO 428.**

**Also (HO 430), the ideology-arc tail — correctness/refactor, no theme-% change.** Two small isolated cleanups retiring the last two ideology-arc follow-ups (both had ridden in backlog QUEUED since HO 429), same **correctness-not-new-surface** bucket as the HO 405–413 arc — no bar move. **HO 430a (`6329a0f`), the `median()` extraction:** the four byte-identical inline copies (server `lib/queries`, the `IdeologyStrip` client island, the sync + diagnostic scripts) collapsed into one zero-import leaf module **`lib/median.ts`** — canonical definition, four importers, so nobody re-inlines it; a pure refactor, the 119th history-vs-band cross-check held at 0.925/0.918 delta 0.000. **HO 430b (`d061284`), the ideology diagnostic caucus fix:** `ideology-coverage-419.ts` now applies the **neither-match invariant** (flag only when Voteview's letter matches neither registration nor caucus) instead of a registration-only equality check, suppressing the structural caucus-vs-registration gap while still catching a genuine sync mismatch. **Premise correction:** the backlog claimed King, Sanders, *and* Kiley tripped the diagnostic; the live run showed only Kiley did (King/Sanders code Voteview 328→I and already agree with `members.party='I'`) — the HO 422 caucus-vs-registration distinction confirmed on live data (a new oddities entry records the premise correction — the old "all three flagged" read was wrong). Diagnostic-only; no `members`/`sync` touch (Kiley's `I` is correct, HO 422 NOT A BUG). Docs: HO 431. **Also notes now run through HO 430.**

**Also (HO 433), the cron-cadence pass on Vercel Pro — infrastructure, no theme-% change.** CBT moved Hobby → Pro, retiring the **once/day cron cap** (Hobby rejected any sub-daily schedule at deploy, so the ten hand-staggered 09:00–21:30 UTC slots were a scheduling artifact, not a design). `vercel.json` cadences reset to what each cron actually wants: **`/api/cron/summarize` `0 13`→`*/10`** — continuous drain, which **discharges the HO 404 summarize backlog loop**: summarize ran once/day, so on any day inflow exceeded one 60s drain the queue grew (1,011 → 1,374 at the HO 404 audit); HO 405/406 made a single tick self-heal but daily cadence let it re-grow — at `*/10` the drain is continuous and the loop can't re-form. Also **`/api/cron/news` `0 14`→`*/30`**, **`/api/sync` `0 9`→`0 */6`**, **`/api/cron/committees` `30 11`→`0 */12`**; votes / rating-history / primaries / race-ratings / weekly-report unchanged (correctly daily / weekly / event-driven); **`maxDuration: 60` untouched everywhere** (frequency replaces per-run volume, so no run needs more than 60s). Two logic fixes shipped **ahead of** the cadence flip in history (config must not deploy before them): a **summarize overlap lock** (`lib/cron-lock.ts` — an atomic `dashboard_state` advisory claim, self-healing on a 120s TTL; Vercel Cron is best-effort *at-least-once* and the drain isn't overlap-safe, so two concurrent invocations would double-spend Gemini and double-write the append-only `stage_transitions` log), and **news dedup-before-LLM** (skip the Gemini matcher for any article whose deterministic `obs_id` already exists — `storeObservation` freezes entities on first insert, so re-matching a seen article at a tighter cadence was pure token waste). Dedup **live-verified** on `cbt-csu-j3`: an immediate second news tick dropped `llmCalls` 23→0 with `llm_skipped_seen` 55/55, wall-clock 43s→17s. **Markets + per-seat Kalshi/Polymarket odds deliberately stay on GitHub Actions** (markets-tick 30m, markets-daily 4h, kalshi-tick 2h); the Vercel `30 21` markets floor was a non-firing HO 314 artifact, left as-is. **Migrating those three workflows GitHub → Vercel is flagged as its own follow-up HO** — a *reliability* play (HO 313 measured GitHub dropping ~20% of scheduled slots), **not a cadence change**, needing query-path / FRED-gating / workflow-retirement care to avoid double-writes. **No theme-% change** — cron infrastructure, no user-facing surface. Docs: this handoff renumbered 432 → 433 (HO 432 is the race-news badge/popover). **Also notes now run through HO 433.**

**Also (HO 434–435), the LDA lobbying pillar — data layer shipped, no theme-% change.** The roadmap's "best next pillar" call landed as an additive data layer (source probe 434 → build 435). **Source:** the lda.gov API (the legacy `lda.senate.gov` host is sunsetting ~2026-06/07; built against `lda.gov`). **HO 434** probed it GO on a partial bill-number join; **HO 435** (`39f6e54` data + `72c41ee` cron) built three additive tables — `lda_filings` / `lda_activities` / `lda_activity_bills` — ingesting current-Congress LD-2 quarterly reports via a resumable **DB-frontier** sync (`sync:lda`; resume = `MAX(dt_posted)` per `(filing_year, filing_type)`, no stored cursor) on a **daily-300s** `/api/cron/lda`. **Corpus:** 108,707 filings / 233,499 activities / 173,885 bill-links / 5,165 registrants / 26,920 clients. **Join reality:** 99.3% of parsed IDs resolve, but only **22.8% of activities carry a bill number** and **25.4% of filings have ≥1 joined bill** — so issue-code (BUD/HCR/TAX/DEF top) is the spine, the bill join the overlay. A 40-row residual (0.037%) on the closed 2025 quarters is accepted DRF count-vs-enumerable drift, not a gap (oddities). **No theme-% change** — enabling data with no user-facing surface; the LDA surface is HO 437 (bill-keyed vs issue-code-first + placement, Corey's call). Banked: a Congress.gov title-enrichment sync (to catch named-not-numbered bills — NDAA / appropriations — since `bills` has no `short_title`) and LD-203 contributions for a future money surface. Docs: HO 436. **Also notes now run through HO 435.**

**Also (HO 437), the LDA lobbying surface — issue-code-first, no theme-% change.** The HO 435 data layer's surface (Corey's call: issue-code-first over bill-keyed). Shipped + prod-verified across `2b34058` (query layer + rollup) · `d6c7d2c` (`/lobbying` surface) · `3851b85` (feed OFFSET fix); live SHA `3851b85`. **`/lobbying`** is a new top-level nav item and a **standalone analytical surface** (the `/patterns` class — an aggregate lens, not an entity hub): a stat readout + issue-area **ranked bars** (native LDA `general_issue_code` labels, NOT crosswalked to the 24 CBT topics — lossy) + a **per-issue drill** (top clients / top firms / recent) + a corpus-wide **recent-filings feed**. **The query-layer story is load-bearing:** request-time aggregation is **non-viable** on this Turso tier for these table sizes (whole-table aggregates row-fetch 30–90s+ cold; even a bounded per-code drill >25s — oddities). So stats + issue-bars + the **full per-code drill** are **precomputed** by the daily LDA cron into a `dashboard_state` blob (`lda_lobbying_rollup`) via full-table **sequential** reads + in-memory JS aggregation (~186s, under the 220s cron budget gate), served O(1); only the corpus recent-filings feed is **live** (an `idx_lda_filings_dt_posted` walk clamped to the 1,000 most-recent — `MAX_FEED_PAGES=40` — so a tail-of-table OFFSET can't 500). A **live-per-code drill was tried and abandoned** (>25s cold); a **covering-index detour was tried and fully reverted** (`migrate.ts` net-zero, no prod schema change). **No theme-% change** — an enabling surface on a new pillar. **Banked:** HO 439 (bill-keyed lobbying on `/bill/[id]`, reuses `lda_activity_bills`) + the CBT-topic crosswalk + a corpus-wide top-firms leaderboard + named-not-numbered bill recovery (needs the Congress.gov title-enrichment sync). Docs: HO 438. **Also notes now run through HO 437.**

**Also (HO 439–440), bill-keyed lobbying on `/bill/[id]` — the LDA arc's second fork, no theme-% change.** The other fork of the HO 435 data layer (the HO 437 block banked this as "HO 439"; **439 became the read-only cost probe, 440 the surface** — the probe took its own number, HO 434→435 precedent). **HO 439 (`42ff9c0`), the cost probe:** timed the real per-bill seek+hydrate shape cold against the worst case. **Verdict: live is non-viable for the fat tail** — worst-case bill (`119-hr-1`) **44.5s cold** (4.5× the 10s `getDb` cap), and the cost is entirely the random `lda_filings` row-fetch (seek 461ms, aggregate 30ms — the HO 340/437 shape confirmed one more time). Fan-out is a **fat tail with an extreme outlier**: 9,179 bills linked, p50 6 / p90 27 / p99 159 / max 8,048. Diagnostic committed as `scripts/diagnostic/lda-bill-cost-439.ts` (sibling to `lda-coverage-435.ts`). **HO 440 (`9a540b2` surface · `ab1505a` data+cron · `7b8f0d5` refactor), the hybrid surface:** **precompute the ≥150-filing tail, live-serve the rest.** `computeLdaRollup` refactored to share its sequential three-table read (`readLdaTables` → `computeIssueRollup` [issue rollup, **byte-identical to prod** verified] + `computeBillDrill`), so the per-bill drill piggybacks the pass already running — near-free. Qualifying bills (**96 at ≥150**, 559 KB) written to a **second `dashboard_state` blob** (`lda_bill_drill`) by the same budget-gated cron step; `getBillLobbying(billId)` reads it O(1), **falls through to a live `getDb` drill** for below-threshold/zero-link bills (worst below-threshold bill live 2.3s, 7.7s margin), **try/catch → null** so a freshly-heavy bill can never 500 the page. A LOBBYING section on `/bill/[id]` (after Hearings, before Summary) reuses `FilingRow` + the extracted `LobbyingMiniBars`; zero-link bills omit it. Ranks top firms/clients by **distinct filings** (consistent with the `/lobbying` per-code drill). Prod-verified across all three `getBillLobbying` branches at `42ff9c0` (blob `119-hr-1`, live `119-hr-2794`, zero-link `119-hres-1420` — 200, section absent). **Still banked:** the CBT-topic crosswalk + the corpus-wide top-firms leaderboard (both carried from HO 437; untouched here). **No theme-% change** — a secondary signal on an existing pillar. Docs: HO 441. **Also notes now run through HO 440.**

**Also (HO 442), the corpus-wide top-firms leaderboard — the LDA arc's last banked surface, no theme-% change.** The third and final item banked in the HO 437 block (bill-keyed → HO 439–440; the CBT-topic crosswalk stays banked; this closes top-firms). Shipped + prod-verified across `b64e1c7` (data + cron/CLI) · `69644fd` (surface); live SHA `69644fd`. **No cost probe** — unlike every prior `lda_*` surface (434→435, 439→440), this introduced no new cold query: `computeTopFirms` is a **third consumer of the shared `readLdaTables` pass** (beside `computeIssueRollup` + `computeBillDrill`), one in-memory grouping over the already-loaded `filings` map, so the build/probe split didn't apply — it rode the ~96s pass already running. Written to a **third `dashboard_state` blob** (`lda_top_firms`) in the same budget-gated cron step under the single `revalidateTag("lda")`; the CLI (`npm run lda:rollup`) mirrors it, so local regen writes all three blobs. **Definition:** ranks **registrants** (the lobbying shops — consistent with `topFirms` everywhere else) by **distinct filings** (the HO 435 house rule), name-tiebroken; per firm carries distinct clients, bill-linked filing count, and their single most-cited `general_issue_code`. **Top 25** (one constant `TOP_FIRMS_N`, store == render). **Surface:** a **TOP FIRMS section on `/lobbying`** (new Section 3, between the issue-drill two-column and the recent-filings feed) served O(1) from `getTopFirms()` (null before the first rollup → section cleanly omitted); the separate blob key keeps the 286KB `/lobbying` rollup read lean (the HO 440 rationale). Mobile: the Top-issue + Bill-linked columns collapse below ~640px (Tailwind, no `globals.css` coupling). **Two deliberate omissions, recorded so they aren't re-litigated:** (a) **no dollar ranking** — LD-2 income/expenses are partial + income-vs-expense asymmetric across registrant types, so a summed-dollar leaderboard would mislead; dollars are the banked **LD-203** money surface; (b) **native issue-code labels, NOT crosswalked to the 24 CBT topics** — that crosswalk is the other still-banked item. **The finding:** rank-1 is **Cornerstone Government Affairs**, #2 Brownstein Hyatt — a filing-**volume** ranking is deliberately distinct from a revenue ranking (Cornerstone runs a huge book of small clients → files a mountain of LD-2s without top-grossing), which is exactly the signal this cut adds over the dollar rankings. Invariants at rollup: **25 firms of 5,175 registrants**, 0 `billLinked > filings`, 0 null `topIssueCode`; the Section-1 readout `stats.registrants` and the leaderboard `totalRegistrants` both render **5,175** (structurally guaranteed — same `readLdaTables` filings map — so no masthead incoherence). **No migration, no new cron slot, no new nav item.** **No theme-% change** — an enabling/aggregate surface on the LDA pillar (the `/patterns` class), matching HO 437/439–440. Docs: HO 443. **Also notes now run through HO 442.**

**Also (HO 444), the CBT-topic crosswalk on `/lobbying` — the LDA arc's last banked surface, no theme-% change.** The final item banked in the HO 437 block (bill-keyed → 439–440; top-firms → 442; this closes the crosswalk), so the **HO 437 four-item fork is now fully discharged — nothing from it stays banked.** Shipped + prod-verified across `8f7d7bd` (data) · `549d81e` (surface); live SHA `549d81e`. **Two calls shaped it. Build, not probe:** unlike every prior `lda_*` surface (434→435, 439→440), it introduced no new cold query — `computeTopicCrosswalk` is a **fourth consumer of the shared `readLdaTables` pass** (beside `computeIssueRollup`/`computeBillDrill`/`computeTopFirms`), one in-memory re-bucketing of the already-loaded `uuidsByCode` sets, so the build/probe split didn't apply (the HO 442 precedent). **`/lobbying` section, not a topic hub:** there is no topic entity route (topics are a filter/facet only), so cross-linking lobbying onto a topic page has no home — the self-contained surface is a **BY-CBT-TOPIC section on `/lobbying`** (new Section 3, between the issue two-column and TOP FIRMS; TOP FIRMS → 4, feed → 5), which does **not** reopen the HO 437/442 native-labels decision (that was about not *replacing* the native `general_issue_code` bars; this sits alongside them, disclosing the lossiness by keeping both, and its payoff is expressing lobbying in the same 24-topic vocabulary as the dashboard's bill distribution). **The map** (`lib/lda-issue-topic-map.ts`, a typed `Record<string, Topic>` — not `enums.ts`, not JSON, so the per-code judgment comments type-check): **79 LDA codes → 23 live CBT topics**; `elections` legitimately empty (no lobbying issue code maps there — an honest gap, not forced); **`other` = 21** industry/profession/contested codes (comprehension-over-coverage, the `TOPIC_ALIASES` posture). Two contested calls resolved on review: **CPT (Copyright/Patent) → `technology`** and **FAM (Family/Abortion/Adoption) → `healthcare`** (the closest single home for a single-valued map; the abuse-vs-industry asymmetry — ALC→healthcare as the *abuse* code, TOB→other as the *industry* code — is deliberate, see oddities). **The multi-code property:** a filing carries multiple issue codes, so it lands in multiple topic buckets — topic-filings sum **213,780 > corpus 110,055**, NOT a partition (the same property the native issue bars have; the section header discloses it, never "share of filings"). Invariants: 23 topics, 0 unmapped (structural — `?? "other"` catch-all), the HO 442 `5,175`-registrant readout holds (same `readLdaTables` map). **No cost probe, no migration, no new cron slot, no new nav item** — the fourth blob (`lda_topic_crosswalk`) rides the same budget-gated cron step under the one `revalidateTag("lda")`. **No theme-% change** — an enabling/aggregate surface on the LDA pillar (the `/patterns` class), matching HO 437/439–440/442. Docs: HO 445. **Also notes now run through HO 444.**

**Also (HO 446–448), the amendments pillar — probe → data layer → first surface, no theme-% change.** The roadmap's "cheapest core-deepening move" call (amendments over nominations: dual-attaches to the bill spine via `amendedBill` and the member spine via `sponsors`, reuses the Congress.gov sync wholesale, fills the floor-stage visibility gap; nominations sequenced later on a weaker join). Three HOs, probe-first as ever. **HO 446 (`508656e`), the source probe — GO:** 6,788 amendments in the 119th (SAMDT 6,560 / HAMDT 228 / SUAMDT 0), `amendedBill` present and resolving 100% to tracked bills, rich `latestAction` disposition vocab. The structural finding that killed a speculative table: sub-amendments carry BOTH `amendedBill` AND `amendedAmendment`, and the API resolves the ultimate bill transitively — so lineage is a nullable **self-FK**, not an `amendment_bills` link table, and `amended_bill_id` is a plain resolved column. **HO 447 (`667cc33` data + `cecff19`/`c5575b2`/`08394e6` fixes), the data layer:** an additive `amendments` table (house-style `amendment_type`/`amendment_number`, **loose links not enforced FKs** — `cecff19`, correct for self-referential bulk-load ordering), a resumable DB-frontier sync (`sync:amendments`, `MAX(update_date)` ascending sweep, list→detail hydration, deadline-gated `/api/cron/amendments` at 07:00 UTC, unbounded CLI backfill) mirroring `sync:lda`, and a read-only coverage diagnostic. Two resume corrections landed in-arc: **resume on the list item's `updateDate`, not the detail's** (`c5575b2`), and a **`--repair` mode** (`08394e6`) that enumerates live-vs-stored and fills the handful of holes the unstable-`updateDate`-order pagination skips (the ascending frontier can't refill an interior gap — oddities). Backfill ran whole: `--repair` exits `complete` at 6,788 == live `pagination.count`. **HO 448 (`13670f2`), the first surface — a bill-hub AMENDMENTS section on `/bill/[id]`:** the per-bill floor fight, placed **between Hearings and Lobbying** (committee → floor → outside-influence order). **No cost probe, no precompute blob** — unlike every `lda_*` surface, and deliberately so: amendments is **bills-scale** (~6,800 rows total, smaller than `119-hr-1`'s LDA filing set alone at 8,048), so a per-bill seek behind `idx_amendments_bill` hydrates instantly even for the worst magnet; `getBillAmendments` is a plain live `unstable_cache` query (tag `amendments`, try/catch → null so a bad read hides the section instead of 500-ing the page), no `dashboard_state` involvement. Each row: label, party-colored sponsor (members join, plain-text fallback for committee/manager amendments with no bioguide), purpose, a **display-only disposition dot** (green/red/neutral keyword-scanned from top-level `latest_action_text` via the `--vote-yea`/`--vote-nay` palette — NOT the deferred status model), sub-amendment `↳ amends` note; ordered by `COALESCE(latest_action_date, submitted_date) DESC`, capped at 60 + "N more" for magnets. **The finding:** `latest_action_text` is present on only **8.6%** of amendments (447's "~35%" was the probe's stale frontier read — oddities), because a couple of budget vote-a-rama magnets dominate the filed-not-acted corpus — `119-sconres-7` alone carries **1,131 amendments (17% of all 6,788)**, only 24 with a top-level disposition. That 8.6% is the concrete argument for the deferred actions-walk status model (the vote-a-rama outcomes live in the per-amendment `actions` sub-resource the sync skips). Prod-verified across magnet (`119-sconres-7`, 1,131 / capped 60), small (`119-sjres-98`, 8 full), zero (`119-hconres-1`, section absent), and sub-amendment lineage (`119-s-1318`, `↳ amends SAMDT 5814/5816/5817`). **No theme-% change** — the floor-stage gap is now visible on the bill hub, but the outcome-classification (status model) and the cross-bill aggregate lens (`/amendments`) that a bump would ride are both deferred, so this is a v1 surface on a new pillar, matching the LDA-arc precedent (437 / 439–440 / 442 / 444 all held). **Banked (QUEUED):** the standalone `/amendments` aggregate surface, a member-hub sponsored-amendments section, and the persisted status/disposition model. Docs: HO 449. **Also notes now run through HO 448.**

**Also (HO 450), the member-hub sponsored-amendments section — the second amendments fork, no theme-% change.** One of the three forks banked in the HO 446–448 block (bill-hub → 448; this ships the member-hub; the standalone `/amendments` aggregate + the persisted status model stay QUEUED). Shipped + prod-verified; live SHA `ea686a8`. **The inverse projection of the HO 448 bill-hub section:** `getMemberAmendments(bioguideId)` seeks `idx_amendments_sponsor` (where 448 seeks `idx_amendments_bill`) and keys each row on the **amended bill** — linked to `/bill/[id]` via `formatBillId`, with the bill title — rather than the sponsor, since the sponsor is fixed (it's their page). A new `MemberAmendmentRow` (a bare row the page maps — the member-page idiom, not self-boxing like the bill page's `BillAmendments`); the disposition dot + module-level `deriveDisposition` are reused, no new palette. **No probe, no blob, no cron/migration** — same bills-scale reasoning as 448: a per-member seek hydrates instantly, and the heaviest amender (Bennet `B001267` at **271** amendments) served live, capped at 60 + "N more". Placed as the **authoring pair** — immediately after Sponsored bills, before Committees (bills + amendments = the two forms of member-authored legislative text) — and hidden when empty (most House members sponsor none), the `{scorecard ? …}` conditional-section idiom. Prod-verified across heavy/capped (Bennet, 271 → 60 + 211 more), small/full (Moreno `M001242`, 6), zero/absent (Aderholt `A000055`, section omitted), and dots + sub-amendment lineage (Paul `P000603`, 1 yea / 7 nay / 35 muted, `↳ amends`). **No theme-% change** — the second v1 surface on the amendments pillar; the outcome-classification (status model) + the cross-bill aggregate lens (`/amendments`) that a bump would ride both stay deferred, matching 448 and the LDA-arc precedent. Docs: HO 451. **Also notes now run through HO 450.**

**Also (HO 452), the actions-walk disposition probe — NO-GO, the status-model fork retired, no theme-% change.** The third fork banked in the HO 446–448 block (bill-hub → 448; member-hub → 450; this probes and **closes** the persisted status/disposition model). Probe committed `67ed564`, read-only, no build followed. **The question:** the display-only disposition dot (HO 448/450) colors only the **8.6%** of amendments with a top-level `latest_action_text`; the persisted-enum model would walk each amendment's `/actions` sub-resource to recover dispositions for the silent 91% — but that ~doubles the backfill (~6,800 → ~13,600 requests, ~85 → ~195 min), so it's only worth it if `/actions` actually carries those dispositions. **The finding: it doesn't — 0% recovery.** A stratified 155-amendment walk (`scripts/diagnostic/amendments-actions-probe-452.ts`, 0 errors / 0 throttles) found that no-top-level-action == **genuinely filed-never-acted**: both the general silent-91% stratum and the decisive `119-sconres-7` vote-a-rama stratum returned **100% submit/propose-only** `/actions` lists (0% terminal disposition), with an **empty residual** (the starter classifier left zero terminal actions unmatched — the 0% is a real absence, not a classifier miss). A vote-a-rama files ~1,131 amendments and votes on ~24; those ~24 already carry a top-level action, and the rest are never called up, so there's nothing in `/actions` to recover. **The top-level scan is the disposition ceiling** — a persisted enum would only persist what render already computes, for a doubled backfill and no coverage gain (oddities). So the status model closes NO-GO; the display-only dot stays as the complete-for-what's-available surface, not a stopgap. **One capability banked, orthogonal to dispositions:** `/actions` carries **`recordedVotes`** ({chamber, rollNumber, url, date}) — a clean amendment → roll-call-vote link (backlog QUEUED), the source for a future "how did each member vote on this amendment" surface, scoped on its own merits (it'd still pay the `/actions` walk cost, but for vote links, not dispositions). Payload also confirmed structured (`type` / 13 distinct `actionCode`s), though the text is already unambiguous. **No theme-% change** — a probe that retired a fork; no surface. This leaves the amendments pillar at **two live surfaces** (bill-hub, member-hub) with **one QUEUED fork** remaining (the `/amendments` aggregate) plus the new vote-linkage lead. Docs: HO 453. **Also notes now run through HO 452.**

**Also (HO 454–456), the nominations pillar — probe → data layer → surface, GO where amendments' status model was NO-GO, no theme-% change.** The other half of the amendments-vs-nominations fork (picked after amendments closed at 453). Nominations have **no bill spine and no member-sponsor** (the president nominates, the Senate confirms) — the "weaker join" the fork flagged, probe-confirmed: nominees carry no bioguide, so the pillar is standalone. Three HOs, probe-first. **HO 454 (`b27a1d2`), the source probe — GO:** 1,884 PNs in the 119th; both decision cuts came back favorable. **Civilian volume is real** — 833 PNs (judges, cabinet, ambassadors, US Attorneys), 44% by PN count, not drowned by the military bulk (1,051 PNs but ~25k nominees at ~24.5/PN; military dominates the *nominee* axis ~30:1, so it must be **collapsed** — one row per list PN, never exploded). **The disposition contrast — the reason nominations clears where amendments didn't:** `latest_action_text` is **100%** present (vs amendments' 8.6%), clean deterministic vocab along the confirmation pipeline (Received → Referred → Hearings → Reported → Calendar → Confirmed / Returned / Withdrawn) — a **persisted** status model is viable (oddities: the amendments NO-GO does NOT generalize). Two catches a naive build misses: the **`(pn_number, part_number)` composite key** (citations are `PN{n}-{part}`; the base-part detail is a *different* record) and the list-vs-detail `updateDate` divergence (same frontier hazard as amendments). Organization is a clean 84-value agency facet; `recordedVotes` absent (the roll-call is in the action text); committee referral present but the civilian rate unmeasured (a confound — deferred re-measure). **HO 455 (`ebadee3` data + `e175fd7`/`f15608b` gate fixes), the data layer:** an additive **list-only** `nominations` table (every core-surface field is list-level, so no detail fetch — which sidesteps the part-specific-detail wrinkle), keyed on the composite, military collapsed to single rows, with a **persisted `disposition`** computed at sync by `computeNominationDisposition` (the differentiator); `committee_system_code`/`nominee_count` present-but-NULL (deferred, forward-compatible). A resumable frontier sync (`sync:nominations`, `MAX(update_date)` ascending, inclusive boundary, deadline-gated `/api/cron/nominations` at 09:00) mirroring `sync:amendments` but cheaper (list-only, ~34s backfill). **The completion gate got a real fix in-arc:** nominations exposed a latent bug the amendments corpus never hit — `--repair` gated on `stored >= pagination.count`, but `pagination.count` **overcounts** the distinct-enumerable set (5 byte-identical duplicate list entries in the feed, 0.27% — the LDA-40 drift), so a correctly-deduping store can never reach it; the gate now keys on **full-enumeration `missing.length === 0`** (`e175fd7`), the diagnostic's landed-clean line likewise (`f15608b`). Corpus: **1,879 distinct**, provably whole. **HO 456 (`754cf0e` disposition tune + `febaaec` surface), the surface — standalone `/nominations`:** civilian-focused (830), leading with the **disposition strip** (60.5% confirmed — the status-model headline) + the 84-agency facet (State/Justice/The Judiciary/…), a filtered list with chips + pagination. **Live aggregation, no rollup blob** — 1,879 rows (smaller than bills), so `getNominationsSummary`/`getNominations` `GROUP BY` live (the bills-scale call, not the `lda_*` precompute one); no `MAX_FEED_PAGES` clamp at 830 rows. Military excluded and **disclosed** in the count line (the LDA-style honesty). Nav item `◈ Nominations` (standalone key, no sub-nav, mirrors Lobbying). The disposition tune (`754cf0e`) fixed the one residual — a reconsider-tabled action reading `received` is a finalized **confirmation** — and surfaced another gotcha (oddities): **`--backfill` resets offset, not frontier**, so a persisted derived column can't be recomputed by re-running the sync on a populated DB (it only re-touches the `MAX(update_date)` delta); the corpus-wide recompute needed **truncate + re-backfill** (safe here only because the table is list-only and API-reproducible). Prod-verified across the disposition strip, both facets filtering, deep pages (no tail 500), and the disclosure line. **No theme-% change** — a new-pillar v1 surface, matching the LDA/amendments precedent; the deferred committee cross-link + detail hydration are what a bump would ride. **Banked:** the `/committees/[id]` nominations cross-link (+ part-specific detail + civilian referral re-measure) in QUEUED; the military view + `/nomination/[id]` detail in BANKED. Docs: HO 457. **Also notes now run through HO 456.**

**Also (HO 458–459), the nominations committee cross-link — the deferred detail-only join, shipped, no theme-% change.** The one fork the HO 454–456 pillar deferred (list-only `/nominations` shipped; `committee_system_code` sat NULL because committee referral is detail-only). Probe-first as ever. **HO 458 (`a0cab49`), the detail probe — GO:** the cross-link rides a cold query never measured at civilian scale on the *correct* record, and the HO 454 civilian referral number was **confounded** — it read the base `/nomination/{c}/{n}`, which for a multi-part PN is a **stub** (`committees` absent). The probe settled the shape: **multi-part is load-bearing** (786/830 = 94.7% of civilian rows live inside multi-part PNs, not a tail); each part-row's stored `raw_json.url` resolves to a **distinct record with its own per-part committee** (PN730-1 African Development Bank vs -2 Federal Labor Relations Authority); `committees` is a sub-resource `{count, url}` everywhere (a 2nd fetch when `count > 0`). Re-measured civilian-only on the right record: **98% referral presence / 100% resolution to tracked `committees` / 0 unresolved codes** (no `sync:committees` gap), effectively 1:1 (a 6/150 dual-referral tail). Cost ~1.98 fetches/row → ~277–500s for 830 rows, over the 300s route → CLI backfill + a bounded cron delta. **HO 459 (`b783d39` data + `fd5e256` surface), the build:** hydrate `committee_system_code` per civilian part-row by following the stored `url` (2 fetches when referred, storing the **first**/primary committee — the dual-referral secondary dropped, disclosed, no link table). A **`committee_hydrated_at` sentinel + partial index** (`idx_nominations_hydrate ON nominations(pn_number) WHERE is_military = 0 AND committee_hydrated_at IS NULL`, the HO 406 summarize-queue pattern) is the two-phase-populate key: every processed row is stamped (incl. the ~2% with no committee) so it exits the queue and incremental re-runs only touch the NULL delta. A `--hydrate` CLI mode does the one-time 830-row populate; a **bounded cron step** (sync deadline 45s → hydrate 55s, cap 40/tick) trickle-hydrates the delta after. **The list sync needs no change** — `buildNominationStatement`'s `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET` already omits `committee_system_code`, so a bulk-refresh re-upsert preserves the hydration stamp (adding the sentinel to the SET clause would re-null every hydrated row — oddities). Initial populate: **830 processed / 817 withCommittee / 13 no-committee / 0 unresolved / 22 dual-dropped / remaining 0** (one transient 15s fetch-timeout on `PN379-07` left `remaining=1`; a resumable 2nd `--hydrate` pass drained just that row — the sentinel-queue working as designed). **Surface:** `getCommitteeNominations(systemCode)` + a `?committee=` filter on `getNominations` (bidirectional), and a **full-width "Nominations referred" section on `/committee/[systemCode]`** (after Hearings; `NominationRow` reused, disposition-badged, header `{total} referred · {confirmed} confirmed`) — **omitted when total is 0**, so House/joint committees (which confirm nothing) show no section, no "0 referred" noise; a "see all → `/nominations?committee=…`" foot when over the 25-cap. Prod-verified: `/committee/ssfr00` (Foreign Relations, 201 referred / 100 confirmed), `ssju00` (Judiciary, 187 / 118 — the filtered `?committee=ssju00` list count matches exactly, join correct), `hsju00` (House Judiciary, section omitted). **Scope held:** `nominee_count` stays deferred/NULL (military-only, no consumer — folded into the banked military-view item), primary-only referral (no `nomination_committees` table), no `/nomination/[id]`, no new nav/cron. **No theme-% change** — a cross-link deepening an existing pillar (the deferred join it was waiting on is now built, but it's a secondary lens, not a new theme), matching the LDA/amendments precedent. The nominations pillar now sits at **two surfaces + a bidirectional committee link**. Docs: HO 460. **Also notes now run through HO 459.**

**Also (HO 461), the `/amendments` aggregate — the amendments pillar feature-complete, no theme-% change.** The last QUEUED amendments fork (bill-hub → 448; member-hub → 450; the actions-walk status model retired NO-GO → 452; this ships the standalone aggregate), so the **HO 446–448 three-fork pillar is now fully discharged** — nothing from it stays banked. Shipped + prod-verified across `6050b41` (query layer + cost gate + index) · `e96f0ab` (surface + nav); live SHA `e96f0ab`. **`/amendments`** is a new top-level nav item and a **standalone analytical surface** (the `/lobbying` + `/nominations` class — an aggregate lens, not an entity hub): a status hero + most-amended-bills + top-amenders ranked cuts + a filtered feed. **Build, not probe, but with an inline cold-cost gate** — unlike the prior amendments surfaces this carried one genuinely unmeasured cut: `disposition` is **display-only-derived** (HO 452 killed the persisted column), so the corpus disposition breakdown SCANs `latest_action_text` (unindexed), the one non-covered query where the two ranked cuts ride `idx_amendments_bill`/`idx_amendments_sponsor`. So a Step-0 gate (`scripts/diagnostic/amendments-aggregate-cost-461.ts`) measured the three unbounded cuts cold **before** committing live-vs-blob: the disposition scan was **4065ms** (SCAN + `raw_json` bloat), the two GROUP BYs **90ms/138ms** (already index-only, never touched the table). The fix cleared it: a **partial covering index `idx_amendments_acted`** (`ON amendments(latest_action_text) WHERE latest_action_text IS NOT NULL`) took the disposition scan **4065ms → 40ms** (SEARCH USING COVERING INDEX), a 100× cut — so **live confirmed, no rollup blob, bills-scale-live like `/nominations`.** The index is a pure performance fix (the HO 277/340 covering-aggregate class), **NOT** a status model — HO 452 stays closed (it persists nothing new; it just makes the already-computed display-only scan index-only). **The query layer:** `getAmendmentsSummary()` composes `total` / `acted` / `agreed`·`failed`·`otherActed` (the covered disposition scan mapped through the module-level `deriveDisposition`) / `filedOnly` = total − acted, `byChamber` (SAMDT+SUAMDT→senate, HAMDT→house), and `topBills`/`topSponsors` (top-15 aggregate + `IN`-join hydration, re-sorted by count); `getAmendments({type?, disposition?, bill?, page})` is the bounded paginated feed (coarse `acted`/`filed` disposition filter — fine-grained agreed/failed stays on the per-row dot, since the JS `deriveDisposition` keyword logic would drift a SQL LIKE mirror). Both live `unstable_cache`, tag `amendments`, `try/catch → null`. **The surface:** a 5-section `/amendments` (header/blurb → status hero bar → most-amended bills → top amenders → filtered feed with chips + pagination), a bare `AmendmentRow` carrying **both** the bill link and the sponsor (the corpus feed fixes neither), and a `Δ Amendments` nav entry (standalone key, mirrors `$ Lobbying` / `◈ Nominations`). **The finding — the "floor almost never votes" headline:** 6,788 filed · **586 acted (8.6%)** · 6,202 awaiting floor action; **6,560 Senate / 228 House** (the House barely amends on the floor; the Senate does via vote-a-rama); `119-sconres-7` alone = **1,131 (17%)** at the top of most-amended; Bennet (`B001267`) = **271** the top amender — so the status bar's muted `filedOnly` segment dominates, which is the point. Prod-verified across the shape: the hero readout matches the data to the digit, `?bill=119-sconres-7` → 1,131 + pill, `?disposition=acted` → 586, `?type=HAMDT` → 228 / `?type=SAMDT` → 6,560, deep `?page=20` + combined filters HTTP 200 (bounded feed, no tail 500), nav tab lit. **No theme-% change** — a v1 aggregate lens on an existing pillar, matching 448/450 and the LDA-arc precedent (437/439–440/442/444 all held). **Banked (QUEUED):** the fine-grained disposition list filter, the `dispositionColor`/`deriveDisposition` extraction to a shared module (deferred to keep the feature commit off two working components — the two-commit refactor-separate rule), and the HO 452 `recordedVotes` amendment→roll-call-vote lead (orthogonal, still QUEUED). Docs: HO 462. **Also notes now run through HO 461.**

**Also (HO 463), the `/lobbying` topic-crosswalk click-through — the HO 444 crosswalk made interactive, no theme-% change.** The BY-CBT-TOPIC bars (HO 444) were non-interactive in v1; HO 463 (`2bdbe15`) turns each into a `<button>` that expands inline to its constituent LDA issue codes, each chip a `<Link>` into the **existing** Section 2 `?issue=` drill — scrolling it into view via a plain `#lobby-drill` hash (Next 15.5 honors it on soft-nav; no `scroll={false}`/`onClick` workaround needed). **No chip can land on a blank:** `drill[code]` is built uncapped over every corpus code (`computeIssueRollup` loops all of `uuidsByCode`), so every constituent code has a real drill. Page-side grouping of `rollup.issues` keys on the **same `topicForCode`** `computeTopicCrosswalk` uses, so the chips align exactly with the bar totals — no double-count seam. `TopicCrosswalk` became a `"use client"` island (instant expand; client state persists across the `?issue=` soft-nav, so the open topic survives a chip click). **No new data layer / blob / query / cron / migration / nav** — a UI pass over data already in the page. **No theme-% change** — a v2 interaction refinement on the LDA `/lobbying` surface (the `/patterns` class), matching the LDA-arc precedent. Docs: HO 464. **Also notes now run through HO 463.**

**Also (HO 466–468), the chip-family existing-chip migration — the HO 287 deferral closed, no theme-% change.** The normalization HO 287 scope-guarded out (its additive half shipped the `--kalshi`/`--poly` tokens + `SourceTag`/`MicroTag`; the existing id/stage/party chips were "used in many divergent ways" and split to this arc; topic already unified at HO 316). Audit-first as HO 287 mandated: **HO 465** (read-only, no commit) catalogued every ID/stage/party rendering + consumer and framed three decisions; the builds landed one commit per leg. **HO 466 (`2706d23`), the ID leg — Decision 1: keep chamber-tint.** `BillIdRail` (vertical) + `.bill-chip` (horizontal) stay chamber-colored (`--rail-house`/`--rail-senate`) per the HO 125 rationale (chamber-of-origin is a *structural* marker, not partisan — amberizing it would erase the at-a-glance chamber cue across ~11 BillRow surfaces + TOP STALLS). `.v2f-id` already matched the HO 287 tier-1 spec exactly (bordered `--accent-amber`, 11px/600, 2px radius), so it became the source of a shared **`BillIdChip` + `.bill-id-chip`** primitive that absorbs only the **non-chamber** id surfaces: the dashboard feed (`.v2f-id`), weekly band (`.weekly-band-id` green→amber), and hearings list + calendar (`.hearing-bill-line .bill-id`, `.hcal-card-billid`) — id text unified through `formatBillId`. **Two documented exceptions left as amber text** (the "compact multi-id list ≠ single row-anchor" line): `SponsorExpandedPanel`'s 14px card bill-list (an 11px chip would misalign the baseline row) and `.hearing-chip` (the compact "·N more" multi-id glance strip). **One behavior change:** the feed id was a plain `<span>` inside the HO 319 click-to-expand row (clicking it expanded the row); as a `<Link>` it now deep-links to `/bill/[id]` (with `stopPropagation` so the row still expands from anywhere else) — consistent with the id linking on every other surface. **HO 467 (`03efc6e`), the stage leg — Decision 2: `StageIndicator` stays exempt.** `StagePillStrip`/`.stage-pill` → tier-2 spec: **10px** (was 11), active (current) pill border = its stage color at **50% alpha** (`color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 50%, transparent)`), history pill → explicit **`--text-dim` text + `--border-strong` border** (dropped the old `opacity: 0.6` dimmed-stage-color); one shared-component edit flows to every BillRow surface. `StageIndicator` (hearings + `/bill/[id]`) untouched — its `▸/▸▸/▸▸▸▸` arrow-prefix encodes stage progression at a glance, which a flat pill loses. **HO 468 (`9327517`), the party leg — Decision 3: tint stays ON (pure refactor, no visible change).** Party color is the feed's core at-a-glance signal, so the spec's "tint off by default" was declined; the leg is a source-of-truth consolidation only. The ~8-way `partyColor` fork collapsed to **one canonical helper** in `lib/race-colors.ts` (widened to `string` + **inline**-normalized rather than importing `normalizePartyVariant`, which lives in `queries.ts` behind `next/cache` — the HO 425/430 client-leaf boundary, and `race-colors.ts` is imported by client race components); the feed (`.v2f-subline-bracket`) + `/members` (`.mc-brk`) inline brackets route through `PartyTag` (gained a `className` passthrough), `SponsorExpandedPanel`/`SponsorHoverName`/`MemberHeader` color-converged (markup untouched — `MemberHeader`'s dash-joined identity meta line is not the `[P-ST]` idiom, exempt). **The null-case lesson (Corey caught it pre-commit):** widening the helper's domain risked collapsing `null → I` (independent purple); the fix keeps two categories distinct — **`I`/`ID` → independent purple, `null`/any-unrecognized-code → `--text-dim` gray** (verified `partyColor(null) === partyColor("L") === --text-dim`; the sibling purple path verified live on `[I-CA]`/`[I-VT]`). No visible change: `[D-CT]` renders byte-identical everywhere; the null `[?-ST]` gray path is function-proven (no null-party row exists in the corpus to render live). Accepted micro-edge: `SponsorHoverName`'s null fallback shifts `--text-muted → --text-dim` (a ~never-fired path). **The B6 chip-family arc is now complete** — topic (316) + ID (466) + stage (467) + party (468) — closing the HO 287 deferral; nothing from it stays QUEUED. **No theme-% change** — a visual-normalization arc over existing surfaces, no new data/blob/query/cron/nav. Docs: HO 469. **Also notes now run through HO 468.**

**Also (HO 470 / 432), race→news on the dashboard — the NEW badge lit, no theme-% change.** The HO 272 RACES-tab `NEW` badge (wired but dark since the news→race join was absent) is now live off the HO 394 observation layer. Provenance twist worth recording: the feature **shipped as commit `35bf8d5`, git-labeled HO 432** — a parallel session had built the complete implementation from an earlier "badge + drawer" handoff (HO 432) and left it **uncommitted** in the tree; the HO 470 session (a fresh badge-only respec) found it, verified it clean (`7cbcd37` is an ancestor of HEAD, diff purely additive over HO 466/467, tsc/build green), shipped it, then **dropped HO 470's own redundant spec** (a local gitignored file, just `rm`'d). **What lit:** `RacesBoxTabs` gained a `newsSet` registry beside `movedSet` (`newCount = newsSet.size`, replacing the hardcoded `0`); a per-card `RaceNewIndicator` (mirror of `RaceMovedIndicator`) registers when its latest-news date postdates the browser's `cbt:racesLastView` stamp, rendering a red `.rc-new` NEWS pip; opening RACES stamps `now` and clears both badges. **No new query** — the badge reads the existing **`getRaceNews(incumbent, 3)`** (threaded via `hubs[i].news[0].observedAt`), so the HO 414 "fold `getRaceNews`+`getMemberNews` into `getObservationNews` on the 3rd consumer" debt **stays deferred** (this adds callers, not a third projection). **The variant reality (corrects a premise stale across BOTH handoffs):** live `/` renders `<CompetitiveRacesBlock variant="v2" />` — whole-`<Link>` `RaceCard`s with **no popover** — so HO 432's second half (race news in the competitive **popover**: `RaceHubBody preview` + a hover-bridge) renders **only on the default variant = `/dashboard-classic`** (the sunset route), **not** the live dashboard. So the live dashboard's race→news is the **NEW badge only**; the news content lives on the `/race/[id]` hub (HO 398) every card links to. Both HO 432's author and the executing session had asserted "the dashboard has a race-news popover" by reading the popover branch in `CompetitiveRacesStrip` without checking the variant the caller passes — recorded in oddities. **The finding:** at ship, **3 of 4 featured incumbents carry ≥1 person-observation** (Ossoff / Collins / Kean; Nunn 0) → the badge lights **"New 3"** today; CDP-verified the pips + badge render and clear on RACES open (NJ-07 co-renders MOVED+NEWS, IA-03 correctly bare). **This discharges the QUEUED "HO 272 NEW badge + race→news drawer" item in full** — badge lit; the "drawer" resolved as a scope finding (no live drawer exists; popover-news shipped on classic); non-incumbent challengers stay unjoinable (no bioguide — the expected HO 398 coverage edge). **No theme-% change — Races held at 99** (a within-theme dashboard signal, matching the HO 398/414 race/member→news v1s). Docs: HO 471. **Also notes now run through HO 470.**

**Also (HO 475), the markets/kalshi cron migration GitHub Actions → Vercel Pro — infrastructure, no theme-% change.** The follow-up HO 433 flagged ("migrating those three workflows GitHub → Vercel … its own follow-up HO"), now shipped + prod-verified across `1c16646` (recon diagnostic, HO 474) · `f3fb87a` (Phase 1 — Vercel crons armed) · `945486f` (Phase 3 — workflows retired); live SHA `945486f`. **The three GitHub-Actions market crons (`markets-tick` `0,30 13-21 * * 1-5` fmp / `markets-daily` `0 */4` bare full-roster / `kalshi-tick` `15 */2`) are now Vercel Pro crons** in `vercel.json`: the intraday **fmp tick** ported as a query-string entry `/api/cron/markets?source=fmp` (the 7 latency-sensitive equity/index symbols SPX/NDQ/NVDA/AAPL/MSFT/GOOGL/LMT), the **bare full-roster** run **repurposing the dormant HO 314 `30 21` floor** (schedule changed in place `30 21 * * *` → `0 */4 * * *` — the every-4h bare superset IS the markets-daily replacement, so no separate floor remained to reconcile), and **kalshi** ported direct to `/api/cron/kalshi` `15 */2`; the three `.github/workflows/*.yml` deleted. **Probe-first as ever:** the HO 474 recon (read-only, committed diagnostic `scripts/diagnostic/markets-cadence-474.ts`) cleared the cutover — writes are benign under overlap (`market_ticks` is a plain append, the "harmless charting fuel" the daily workflow always tolerated; `kalshi_odds` is `ON CONFLICT(race_id) DO UPDATE`, idempotent), auth is green (`CRON_SECRET` proven in Vercel Production — the existing Vercel crons succeed and the repurposed floor already authorized), bloat is a non-issue (3,773 `market_ticks` rows / 51 days, no retention step) — so it shipped as a **clean single cutover** (arm → 1-day overlap watch → retire), not a zero-gap dance. **Reliability, not cadence** (the HO 433 framing): HO 313 measured GitHub dropping ~20% of scheduled slots, re-measured in the recon (the weekday `0,30 13-21` fmp histogram read **17h:12 / 15h:3 / 16h:3 / 18h:3 / 19h:2** where ~10/hr was due). **The attribution method (oddities):** `cron_runs` can't distinguish a Vercel invocation from the still-firing GitHub twin (it records route/status/payload, not method/host/UA), so all three were confirmed as distinct **Vercel-cron GETs** off the deployment host — fmp 17:00:44 (7 sym, no POLY), kalshi 18:15:29, bare 20:00:26 (18 sym + the POLY-SHUTDOWN ghost) — using **GET-vs-POST** and **200-implies-Bearer** (the route 401s without `CRON_SECRET`, so only Vercel-with-secret gets a 200) as the discriminators; the 20:00 window showed the **fmp + bare split 18s apart** (7 vs 18 symbols), proving the two `/api/cron/markets` entries register as distinct Vercel crons (a query-string cron keys on the full path string). **Deferred:** the FRED once-daily gate (the bare 4h run re-fetches the 4 `source:"fred"` symbols ~6×/day, ~20 redundant same-value append rows/day — negligible, and a real gate needs a route insert-guard, so skipped to keep the cutover pure-config; revisit only on a future cadence bump). **Banked:** the **markets-watchdog** pairing (a `cron_runs` health check asserting markets/kalshi fired in-window and the only chronic failure is the known POLY-SHUTDOWN ghost — now more valuable with the reliable Vercel floor) and a one-time **~200-row dead-symbol prune** (frozen Stooq-era rows — DOW/GOLD/BTC/VIX/DXY/… — inert in `market_ticks`, not in `MARKET_SYMBOLS`; pure cosmetics). **This discharges the HO 433 "migration flagged as its own follow-up HO" and closes the "Intraday-reliable markets upgrade" OPEN LOOP** (the HO 314 floor is now the 4h bare run; the "free hosted cron" the loop wanted is Vercel Pro's native cron). **No theme-% change** — cron infrastructure, no user-facing surface. Docs: HO 476. **Also notes now run through HO 475.**

**Also (HO 477), the cron-health watchdog — infrastructure, no theme-% change.** The banked "Markets watchdog" (from the HO 475 block), generalized and shipped as a fleet-wide health surface; live SHA `92baba0`. **The load-bearing finding:** a cron that silently *stops firing* is **invisible to Vercel's own monitoring** — no invocation means no function error to alert on — so the HO 314→475 floor *bounds* markets staleness but can't *detect* a total trigger failure; the only thing that catches it is an external "is the data fresh?" **pull**. So rather than a markets-only check, `lib/cron-health.ts` is a **14-route `CRON_ROUTES` registry + `computeCronHealth()`** (single source of truth) feeding two consumers: a new **`app/api/health` GET** returning **200 healthy / 503 unhealthy** (the pull surface an external uptime monitor watches — the actual push-alert layer, Corey's out-of-repo step), and the **refreshed HO 135 diagnostic** (its stale 7-route pre-433 list retired, now reading the same registry). **The liveness rule is the subtle part (oddities):** unhealthy = **no `cron_runs` row within the route's window** (the real "stopped firing" signal) OR the most-recent run is `error`/`orphaned` — **`timeout` counts as alive**, because HO 139's soft-timeout-resume means a `timeout` row proves the cron *fired and executed* (the pre-build probe caught this: `/api/sync` reads `timeout` with bills ~1h fresh and last clean success ~37h back — a success-age check would false-alarm on every soft-timeout route). **Markets is the one exception, keyed on data not the row (oddities):** because the route logs one `route="/api/cron/markets"` for both the bare + fmp crons AND carries a permanent POLY-SHUTDOWN chronicErr every bare run, `computeCronHealth` judges markets by **`MAX(market_ticks.ticked_at)`** (the literal "no `market_ticks` write in N hours" the backlog asked for) — unhealthy iff ticks > ~9h stale OR the markets run is error/orphaned; a success-with-ghost is healthy, the chronicErr string never consulted. Thresholds ≈ 2× cadence + grace (`summarize` 30m … `sync` 13h … 26h daily, weekly at a flat 9d `windowOnly`). The endpoint is **`force-dynamic`/`no-store`, unauthed, not `wrapCronRoute`-wrapped** (a monitor, not a cron — it never logs itself into `cron_runs`) and **503s on a check failure** (never a misleading 200). Prod-verified: **200 / `healthy:true` / all 14 routes fresh**, `/api/sync` + `/api/sync-votes` alive via recent-`timeout` rows, markets alive via tick-freshness with the ghost ignored. **This discharges the "Markets watchdog" OPEN LOOP.** **Two refinements banked:** fmp-specific freshness (the shared `route` string means fmp stopping while bare fires isn't row-detectable — needs payload inspection) and window-aware thresholds for the weekly routes (flat 9d catches a total stop; precise next-fire needs cron-expression parsing). **One finding filed as a new OPEN LOOP (not this HO):** `/api/sync` hit the 55s soft cap on ~6 of its last 7 ticks — the sync step commits (bills fresh) but a trailing step (dashboard-lead/trades) overruns; the watchdog correctly reads it alive, but it's the HO 407 / 405–406 step-overruns-the-wall class and wants its own probe. **No theme-% change** — cron infrastructure, no user-facing surface. Docs: HO 478. **Also notes now run through HO 477.**

**Also (HO 480), the `/api/sync` lead-query hint sweep — a correctness/operational fix, no theme-% change.** The `/api/sync trailing-step timeout` OPEN LOOP the HO 477 block filed, now probed (HO 479, read-only) and fixed (HO 480, hint-only); live SHA `b440b82`. **The load-bearing correction:** the overrun was mis-diagnosed by its symptom. HO 479's probe read the prod telemetry — 32 of 40 `/api/sync` ticks timed out at the 55s soft cap (a standing ~80%, not a wobble) — and cold-timed the suspects, refuting its own stated hypothesis (Gemini latency): the trailing cost was the HO 405/407 **cold-DB-stall**, not a slow external call. `gatherLeadData`'s transitions query, lured by the `is_ceremonial=0 OR IS NULL` predicate into a `MULTI-INDEX OR` over the low-selectivity `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` (~the whole 16.8k corpus) + a `TEMP B-TREE` sort, **ignored the perfect `idx_bills_stage_changed_at`** and cold-stalled past the 10s `boundedFetch` wall — retrying to 10+10s and *failing* the (non-fatal) lead step. **The finding worse than the timeout:** because the lead step was *failing* (not merely slow), the dashboard lead had been **silently going stale on every real-delta tick**, not only on the ticks that tripped the 55s cap. HO 480 swept the function 407 missed — `INDEXED BY idx_bills_stage_changed_at` on `transRs`/`topicRs`, `INDEXED BY idx_bills_introduced_date` on `introRs` (all cold-EXPLAIN-verified to a single sorted range, the residual `json_each` GROUP BY/ORDER BY temp-btrees inherent per the 407 Q6 precedent) — collapsing cold `generateDashboardLead()` from the 14–20s stall band to ~1–3s (warm 806ms), no `[db] timeout, retrying`. **The wall math:** `sync (~30, budget-pinned) + lead (~1–3) + trades (2.4–7.5) ≈ 33–40s`, comfortably under the 55s cap — route finalizes `success`, lead stops silently failing. Close criterion met. **Two items filed, not folded:** (a) the `wrapCronRoute` **instrumentation gap** — timeout rows carry no per-step timings because the finalize catch-branch writes `{ok,elapsedMs,error,status}`, not the handler's closure `timings`; real but a fleet-wide 14-cron contract change, and unnecessary once the route stops timing out (its success-row timings record normally) → its own HO; (b) the 4th lead read `queryEnactedThisWeek` mis-plans identically but is a shared helper → new OPEN LOOP (HO 481). **Banked, not today's cause:** FMP `AbortController` / split-trades — FMP `-latest` `402`s past page 0 so `maxPagesPerChamber:3` is a no-op, the GETs never hung, trades' 2.4–7.5s is ~200 sequential `INSERT OR IGNORE` round-trips. **No theme-% change** — an operational cron fix, no user-facing surface (the correctness gain: the lead is regenerated, not silently stale). Docs: HO 480. **Also notes now run through HO 480.**

**Also (HO 481), the `queryEnactedThisWeek` enacted-helper hint — the 5th sibling — no theme-% change.** The `queryEnactedThisWeek cold-stall` OPEN LOOP the HO 480 sweep filed, now closed; live SHA `9c19557`. HO 480 hinted three of `gatherLeadData`'s four reads and flagged the fourth — the shared HO 232 helper `queryEnactedThisWeek` (`lib/enacted-this-week.ts`), consumed by the lead step AND the ENACTED THIS WEEK banner + week-over-week delta — as needing its own scoping (a hint in a shared helper has blast radius). Probe-then-fix, hint-first: cold-EXPLAIN confirmed both callers (`queryEnactedThisWeek` + `queryEnactedPriorWeekCount`) mis-plan identically (`MULTI-INDEX OR` over `idx_bills_dash_stage` + temp-btree), and `INDEXED BY idx_bills_enacted` (Option A) collapsed both to `SCAN bills USING INDEX idx_bills_enacted` — cold 617ms / 106ms, identical rows/count — so the Option B new partial index (`idx_bills_enacted_changed`) was unnecessary, no migration. **The partial index qualifies** because it's `WHERE stage='enacted'` and the query carries `AND stage='enacted'`; forcing it bounds the scan to the enacted subset by construction. **Corrected premise:** HO 335 recorded this helper as riding `idx_bills_enacted` cleanly — but that index keys `(congress, latest_action_date)`, has no `stage_changed_at`, so it can serve the `stage='enacted'` seek but not the window filter or `ORDER BY stage_changed_at DESC`, leaving a residual in-memory sort (trivial over the few-hundred-row enacted set). **This closes the known OR-lure instances in the enacted/lead cluster (the last flagged sibling); the standing guard against new ones stays the HO 332/340 cold-start audit.** **No theme-% change** — a correctness/hygiene fix, no user-facing surface. Docs: HO 481. **Also notes now run through HO 481.**
