Docs: HO 544 (this sweep — SKILL `getRecentFilings` + `/lobbying` fbar, oddities cost-probe lesson, backlog VOLUME WATCH + the fbar-cut QUEUED update). **Also notes now run through HO 544.**

**Also (HO 545), the `/lobbying` expand-panel description line-clamp + SHOW MORE — clears the banked QUEUED line-clamp item, no theme-% change.** The LD-2 free text rode a fixed `max-height:140px` scroll box (buries the long tail: p99≈1.8k / max 24.4k chars, HO 485). HO 545 (`54d2fd3`) clamps it to 4 lines closed with an explicit **SHOW MORE / SHOW LESS** reveal; the open state is a **bounded 420px scroll** so even the 24k monster can't stretch the HO 492/493-tuned page. The toggle rides a dedicated `<button>` in a small client island (`FilingDescription`, the `FillerWatchStrip` HO 348 precedent — the SOLE client boundary out of the server `FilingExpandPanel`), **NOT** `<details>/<summary>`: these are descriptions researchers copy, and select-to-copy toggling the panel is an active regression. CSS additive under `.lob-exp-desc*` only — no `.lob-*`/`.mc-*` layout, `PAGE_SIZE`, rail-bound, or `.lob-sec-scroll` retune (the three HO 492/493 numbers are tuned against each other and stayed put). **The verification find (the load-bearing one, oddities):** the handoff flagged the risk that `white-space: pre-wrap` might *defeat* the clamp — it did NOT (CDP: closed box `clientH:88` = exactly 4 lines, `scrollH:6280`, clipped; the clamp engages with pre-wrap). The REAL risk was the **inverse** — pre-wrap makes the 4-line clamp count *hard newlines* as lines, and CDP-measuring the column at **56 chars/line → 4 lines ≈ 224 chars** proved the handoff's ~280-char threshold + bare-clamped-below-threshold would **silently clip** a below-threshold description (a 224–280-char text, or one with a mid-text break, wrapping past 4 lines with no reveal control). Fix: **below threshold renders UNCLAMPED (the `--open` state), not the bare clamped base** — clip-proof regardless of column width or break placement; short text never reaches the 420px bound, so it renders identically to a short filing today. The char threshold (now 250) is thereby demoted to a purely cosmetic "when to OFFER collapse" line, not a correctness dependency (a hard-newline count >4 also triggers the affordance). **Prod-verified on the cache-hit path (2× per URL, matching the local CDP exactly):** the 31-activity / 21,633-char monster (`cd77f510…`, on volume page 1) renders **27 clamped-with-button + 4 short `--open` boxes** — both branches on one filing; SHOW MORE → a bounded 418px scroll (page grew **+330px, not ~6000**), SHOW LESS restores; per-activity toggles are independent; short descriptions show fully with no button and no clip. The collapsed default `/lobbying` page (no `?expanded=`) is untouched — the CSS lives only inside the expand panel. **No theme-% change — UI polish on the existing LDA pillar (the 437/440/442/444/486/544 precedent); the combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92.** Docs: HO 545 (this sweep — roadmap block + backlog QUEUED→DONE + the pre-wrap/hard-newline clamp oddity). **Also notes now run through HO 545.**

**Also (HO 546–547), the `/lobbying` registrant/client search — the LAST HO 486 fbar cut, FTS probed + rejected, no theme-% change.** The final open fbar control. **HO 546 (`debb9dd`, read-only probe) retired the backlog's premise.** The close criterion assumed search needed an `lda_filings` FTS5 mirror of `bills_fts` — the probe found that **wrong on measurement** (the "a backlog line's stated mechanism is a claim — probe it" rule, HO 512/529 class): LDA names avg **25 chars**, so a `%term%` substring scan of 112k rows is **~700ms** (the `bills_fts` premise was scanning long `title`+`summary`, which doesn't transfer to short names). NULL-id/name share is **0%**, distinct registrants 5,183 / clients 27,115 → FTS would **22×-over-index** the repeated names AND add a delete+reinsert trigger to the `FLUSH_AT=100`-tuned sync write path — **all cost, zero benefit. FTS REJECTED; the fallback if `%term%` ever climbs is a distinct-name lookup table, NOT FTS** (recorded so it isn't re-proposed). **HO 547 (`5c73643`) ships Fork B leanest — a live `LIKE`, no new DB object at all.** Two amendments to the probe's rec, both load-bearing: **(1) substring `%term%`, not prefix** — "Boeing" must match "The Boeing Company" (client names lead with articles, firms with surnames); prefix would read as broken. **(2) a REQUIRED pre-build VOLUME×search measurement** — the probe only priced RECENT; VOLUME is the agg-driven join carrying the HO 544 ~7s WATCH. Measured cold: **VOLUME+search 7,792ms** (a TEMP B-TREE over the whole materialized agg — the LIKE defeats the LIMIT short-circuit, and search is *usually served cold* since `q` makes cache keys unbounded) vs **RECENT+search 34–697ms** (a plain `SCAN` under the forced `idx_lda_filings_dt_posted`, no MULTI-INDEX-OR — the OR-cold-stall class HOs 405/407/479/480 did NOT bite because the name columns are unindexed). So **search FORCES RECENT** — `getRecentFilings` overrides `sort→recent` when `q` is set, and the UI disables the VOLUME segment (a page-local disabled variant; the shared `SegmentedToggle` is not widened). **Build:** `getRecentFilings` gains `q?` (substring, % / _ escaped via `ESCAPE '\'`); the **default path (recent, unfiltered, no q) stays byte-identical**; a searched set has no blob total, so the pager rides a **live `COUNT(*)`** with the same predicate (≤67ms), run in **parallel (`Promise.all`) inside the HO 440/448 guard**; `MAX_FEED_PAGES=40` clamp kept; the fbar carries a `form` GET (`?q=`, server-driven — matches the page's `<Link>` idiom) beside the HO 544 controls, carried across pagination + expand, hidden when scoped, count line shows the term, zero matches → `No filings match "term"`. CSS additive under `.lob-fbar-*`. **Prod cache-hit verified (2×):** default byte-identical; `boeing`→results **via substring**; zero-match + literal `%`/`_`→`No filings match` (**escaped**, matched nothing not everything); search+linked composes; search+`sort=volume`→forced-recent (VOLUME disabled); `?issue=`→box hidden + q ignored; page-999→clamped — all sub-second. **No theme-% change — a v1 filter cut on the existing LDA pillar (437/440/442/444/486/544 precedent); the combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92.** This **fully closes the HO 486 fbar-cuts item** (sort + bill-linked + search all shipped). Docs: HO 547 (this sweep — roadmap block + backlog fbar-entry struck to DONE + FTS-rejected recorded + the search-forces-RECENT / short-names-≠-bills-LIKE oddity + SKILL `getRecentFilings`/`/lobbying`). **Also notes now run through HO 547.**

**Also (HO 548), harness coverage extension — a cache-hit detector + interaction coverage for five post-472 arcs, no theme-% change.** Test-only, two commits (`88f669f` smoke · `b38f530` fit-finish); findings-only, zero fixes — the HO 472 posture. **Two gaps closed.** **(1) The crawl never exercised a cache HIT.** `smoke.spec.ts` did one `goto` per route and `e2e-prod.yml` triggers on `deployment_status`, so every unattended prod crawl ran inside the post-deploy cache-*miss* window — structurally unable to see the HO 533 failure class (a cached `unstable_cache` value that round-trips wrong — a `Map`→`{}` — and 500s only on the hit). Each route is now hit **twice** (fresh `goto`, not `reload`); collectors are marked after hit 1 and the second hit asserted on the slice past the mark (attribution), one log line carrying both (`hit1=200 … | hit2=…`); hit-2 document status is asserted **hard**. `/vote/[id]` (HO 540, previously in NO `ROUTES` list) added. **(2) Five interactive arcs had no coverage since HO 472.** `fit-finish.spec.ts` gained six areas with provenance: **RecordTabs** (509/510 — tablist, mount-all-panels-carry-`hidden`, scroll-reset, open-row-survives-swap, and the opposite empty-state policy the two consumers deliberately disagree on: member **dims** `data-empty`, bill **omits**), **`/vote/[id]`** (540 — reached via the bill VOTES-tab link *and* cold direct-nav), **the no-double-count invariant** (542 — every `/vote/` link unique page-wide; never-voted bill omits the VOTES tab), **`/lobbying` fbar** (544/547 — sort/linked rebase+compose, search-forces-RECENT with VOLUME inert, clear, scoped-hides), **FilingDescription** (545 — SHOW MORE toggle, the 24k-char monster's page growth bounded <600px, per-activity independence), **`/news` rails** (515/517 — single selection spanning the topic + member groups). **Five seeds, picked from prod Turso** (query per seed in the ship report): `VOTE`=`senate-119-2-207` (Senate roll call w/ positions + a bill link — nomination votes rejected since the `/bill/` back-link needs `vote.billId`), `VOTE_BILL`=`119-hr-8800` (2 own + 19 amendment = 21 unique `/vote/`), `NOVOTE_BILL`=`119-hr-3034`, `TABS_MEMBER`=`A000055` (amendments=0 + trades=0 → dim tabs), `FILING_UUID`=`cd77f510…` (31 activities, 27 clamped + 4 short). **STEP 0 falsification (HO 549, nothing committed) — the detector was proven able to fail before any doc recorded it as protection:** re-injecting the exact HO 533 defect (hand-applied on HEAD — `getBillAmendmentVotes` returns a `Map`, `BillAmendments` reads `.get`) and running the smoke crawl against a local `next start` produced **`[bill-detail] hit1=200 … | hit2=500 … bad=1 console=1 pageErr=1`** on `119-hr-8800` — the detector fired; the scratch edits were reverted, tree clean. That is what makes 548's clean sweep an instrument reading rather than a structural green. **Findings: NONE — stated as a real result, not an absence:** all 36 routes swept `hit1===hit2===200` (zero divergence), and every one of the six interaction invariants held. **One design detail worth keeping:** the bill-hub scroll-reset is **guarded-and-logged, not skipped** — its highest-count panel fits the 430px body so there's no scroll to reset (the member hub, whose Bills panel overflows, exercises it). An earlier `expect.soft(scrolled>0)` correctly surfaced the vacuous case but reds a *committed* test permanently, the wrong shape; the guard-plus-`console.log` keeps the non-exercise visible on stdout (not a silent HO-503 skip) while staying green. **No theme-% change — harness work on already-counted surfaces; no bar moves.** Docs: HO 549 (STEP 0 + this sweep — roadmap block + backlog fbar-line-struck + 548 tombstone + doubled-crawl WATCH + the extended cache-hit oddity + SKILL double-hit convention). **Also notes now run through HO 548.**

**Also (HO 550–552), the House amendment-vote mislink fix (P1) + the motion-aware-surface GO probe — a correctness fix + a probe on an existing pillar, no theme-% change.** **HO 550 (`9383b9f`, read-only probe)** priced the banked HO 537 M8 surface (the 64 Senate roll calls that touch an amendment without being its up-or-down vote) across six measurements — and its **M5, run first, found a P1 live on prod**: `lib/amendment-votes-walk.ts` (House, HO 532) links **every** `recordedVote` off a HAMDT's `/actions` with no question filter (the mirror of the Senate materializer's anchored `SENATE_AMDT_Q`), so two amendments — `119-hamdt-40`→`house-119-1-187`, `119-hamdt-175`→`house-119-2-122` — were rendering an **"On Ordering the Previous Question"** procedural tally through the amendment-framed `VoteLine` as the amendment's own outcome, green agreed dot and all from `deriveDisposition("Passed")`. Ordering the previous question ends debate; it decides *whether* the House votes, not what — wrong on the number and wrong on the outcome. Per the handoff, the fix outranked the surface. **HO 551 (`c03f1b7`) fixed it — predicate derived from the population, not the two exceptions.** STEP 0 enumerated **all 92 House-linked `amendment_votes` questions**: 90 decisive (**89** "On Agreeing to the Amendment" + **1** ", as Modified") + **2** procedural — vocabulary CLOSED, so the gate is `HOUSE_AMDT_QUESTION_LIKE = "On Agreeing to the Amendment%"` (the `%` covers the modified/amended suffixes), added to `lib/amendment-vote-key.ts` **next to the Senate one** (the leaf module exists precisely so the two chambers can't drift — and the asymmetry survived *because* the House had no counterpart there). The walk constructs `vote_id` from the payload (no question text in hand), so the filter rides a **JOIN to `votes` at link time** in the INSERT's LIKE — one SQL authority, no code-side copy. The two live bad rows were deleted one-time, **scoped to the enumerated procedural set** (exactly 2, dry-run confirmed); both offenders keep their real "On Agreeing to the Amendment" vote, and the `119-hr-8800` 19-amendment control stayed byte-identical (the over-filter check). **HO 552 (`20770e3`) closed a self-inflicted edge the 551 gate opened.** The gate collapses "votes row not synced yet" and "synced, wrong question" to the same no-link, but they need OPPOSITE handling: a wrong-question miss is *settled*, an ABSENT votes row is *pending* — and 551's own header comment falsely claimed the absent case "self-heals on the update_date re-walk," which the queue predicate (`update_date > amendment_vote_walked_at`) **cannot deliver** (a just-voted HAMDT already spent its `update_date` bump getting queued this run, so stamping it drops the decisive link **permanently**). Fix: an existence `SELECT id FROM votes WHERE id IN (…)` before the link loop; if any constructed `voteId` is absent the HAMDT is **left UNSTAMPED to retry** (`deferred++`, `continue`) — the same non-stamp discipline `fetchErrors` already used — and the comment was corrected. Existence-check chosen over the handoff-suggested `SELECT id+question` deliberately, to keep decisiveness the single SQL LIKE (no second predicate copy). **STEP 0 falsification (HO 553, nothing committed):** the `deferred` branch had never executed (both 552 verify runs saw `deferred=0`), so — the HO 549 discipline — it was proven able to fire before any doc recorded it as protection. A scratch perturbation of the constructed `voteId` produced `deferred=1`, HAMDT unstamped, `remaining=1` **pinned across 3 runs** (~175ms / one `/actions` GET each — the permanent-gap signature); restoring the code, the **retry completed** (`walked=1`, stamped, decisive `house-119-1-188` link restored). Scratch reverted, tree clean, nothing committed. **Call: permanent retry is acceptable, no bound** — ~228 HAMDTs, one GET per stuck row per run is negligible, and the only alternative (stamp after N attempts) reintroduces the exact permanent-drop this fix removed; the pinned `deferred` counter makes a stuck row visible instead of silent. **The probe's motion-aware-surface verdict is GO (banked, not built here):** vocabulary CLOSED at 4 classes (table 9 / cloture 4 / chair 1 / waiver 50, **tail 0**), **100% number resolution** (64/64; the waiver form's `Amdt. No. N` confirmed the SAME Senate `S.Amdt` namespace by 42/43 sponsor-name match, not a parallel numbering), **~55 fate-bearing lines** after setting aside the 4 already-anchored (M3: **60** would gain a line they don't have today) and 5 orthogonal cloture/chair, **polarity validated 54/56 (96%)** — and against **FULL coverage** (64/64 residual amendments carry `latest_action_text`, because a procedural floor vote always generates an action, NOT the corpus-wide ~8.6% that would make a high rate on tiny N meaningless), the 2 misses both "Motion to Table Failed" edge cases (`samdt-3946/3947`, "fell when SA … fell"). Cold ~162ms (351ms at probe time; remote Turso varies). **One unreconciled arithmetic smell recorded for the build handoff, not papered over:** M4's exclusion math is `64 − 5 orthogonal − 2 withdrawn = 57` expected clean pairs but N came back **56** — an off-by-one that doesn't move the 96% verdict but a build shouldn't inherit it silently. The surface stays a separate scoped build (invert table/waiver polarity, render cloture/chair as orthogonal not a fate, suppress the 4 already-anchored). **No theme-% change — a correctness fix plus a probe on the existing amendments pillar (the 437/439–440/442/444/448/450/461/537/540/542 precedent); the combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92.** Docs: HO 553 (this sweep — roadmap block + backlog P1 tombstone + motion-aware BANKED→QUEUED + the `deferred` transient-vs-pinned WATCH + oddities the stamp-on-exit-converts-transient-to-permanent rule + the HO 542 chamber-asymmetry M5 extension + SKILL the walk gate / `deferred` / non-stamp discipline). **Also notes now run through HO 552.**

**Also (HO 554–555), the motion-model STEP 0 + the `deriveDisposition` kill-vocabulary widening — a probe + a correctness fix on the amendments pillar, no theme-% change.** The motion-aware surface (GO'd HO 550, QUEUED) got its STEP 0 — and the probe's biggest find wasn't about the surface: it falsified the framing that the disposition dot was already correct on the procedural residual, spawning HO 555 as a prerequisite fix that reshapes what the build still owes. **HO 554 (`be0fc4d`, read-only probe), four measurements.** M1 resolved the HO 550 off-by-one the 553 block said the build shouldn't inherit silently: the 57-expected-vs-56-measured clean-pair gap is `119-samdt-3849`, whose actual-side text "rendered moot" sits outside the probe's actualFate validation vocabulary — a **validation-coverage gap, not a render gap** (polarity holds; the row simply couldn't be scored). M2: motion-vs-actual DISAGREE=0 (no polarity halt) — but **DOT-OTHER=53/64**: on 83% of residual rows the motion implies a clean fate the muted prod `other` dot was missing, falsifying the §0 "the dot is already correct, the surface adds the vote line" framing. M3: motions-per-amendment MAX=1 — no multi-motion render ambiguity. M4: the residual select rides `idx_votes_chamber_date` (31ms/64 rows; the split fetch 253ms/297 rows) — trivially live-servable. **HO 555 (probe `c180054` + fix `2ce1d6c`), the widening — measured, not guessed.** The probe clustered the FULL `other` bucket (115/625 acted rows, top-30 normalized shapes hand-tagged) rather than patching the 3 examples M2 surfaced: the candidate rescues **80** — "ruled out of order by the chair" (47; the point-of-order kill after a failed budget waiver; anchored to the full phrase, not bare "out of order") + word-boundaried "fell" (fell with its parent / when cloture was invoked / when tabled). **Deliberately NOT added, still `other`:** withdrawn (the amendment survived the vote and was pulled later — genuinely neither), rendered moot (describes the cloture motion's fate, not the amendment's — the M1 row), cloture-not-invoked (orthogonal procedure); "stricken" is not a kill token. M2 measured **zero collision** with the 4-string `votes.result` vocabulary, so **one shared function holds** across both input families — no split. **The zero-flip gate HALTed on exactly one row, and the flip was accepted as a correction:** `119-samdt-3963` moves agreed→failed — the OLD dot was WRONG there (its text is "SA 3963 fell when …" with a trailing "agreed to" belonging to the subordinate clause, which the old classifier read), so the failed-family-FIRST ordering is now load-bearing for the subordinate-clause case too, not just "not agreed to" (oddities). **Corpus dots move: agreed 337→336 · failed 173→254 · otherActed 115→35** (corpus other 6465→6385); **the voted block is untouched (0 flips)** — the `?disposition=` coarse filter and the voted EXISTS bypass `deriveDisposition` entirely (M4 grep), so the fix moves dots + summary buckets only, never filter semantics. An **M5 watch instrument** was added to the probe post-fix — agreed-classified rows containing `when` — and reads **0**: SA 3963 was alone, no residual mis-read family (→ WATCH). **The verification produced its own lesson (oddities — the HO 533 rule sharpened):** the Vercel Data Cache survives deploys (HO 312), so a shipped VALUE change does not land on the first prod read — the first hit serves the STALE pre-deploy value and only triggers the SWR regen. "Hit 2–3 times" is a serialization-shape test; for a value change the rule is **confirm the served SHA first (`/api/version`), then hit until the value CONVERGES to the expected number and holds** — at 555 the lighter per-bill dot converged before the heavier `/amendments` summary. Local cache-hit double-hit first (hero 336/254/35 + 42/145; ruled-out-of-order rows red on `/bill` and `/members`; control `119-hr-8800` unchanged), then prod convergence. **The motion-aware build's value proposition is RESHAPED, not retired (QUEUED updated):** the dot now already carries the fate on the rescued rows, so what the build still owes is the **motion-framed vote line** (the tally + inverted polarity + verb — a tabling-agreed tally rendered as the kill it is), cloture/chair rendered as ORTHOGONAL not a fate, and suppression of the 4 already-anchored — the vote-line rendering was always the surface; the dot-correction half is done. **No theme-% change — a probe + a correctness fix on the existing amendments pillar (the 550–552 precedent); the combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92.** **Numbering — the 552 → 554 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 553 was the doc sweep for 550–552 (docs-only sweeps are owed no roadmap block — the HO 506/536 precedent). Docs: HO 556 (this sweep — roadmap block + backlog QUEUED-reshape/DONE/WATCH + oddities ×2 + SKILL verification-convention amendment + `deriveDisposition` vocabulary note). **Also notes now run through HO 555.**

**Also (HO 557), the motion-aware Senate amendment-outcome surface — the HO 537 M8 banked surface, shipped with its model corrected mid-build; no theme-% change.** The 64 Senate roll calls that touch an amendment without being its up-or-down vote (Motion to Table · Cloture · Decision of the Chair · budget waiver) now render a **motion line** — the motion, its tally, the party split, a drill link to `/vote/[id]`, and where the motion decides, the amendment's fate. Live at `bf820bd`; **60 amendments gain a line they didn't have** — 55 of them carrying a "→ amendment killed" clause, the rest tally-only (undecided or orthogonal), with the 4 anchored suppressed. **Anchored-among-killed is 0, and structurally so:** an amendment killed by a tabling motion or a failed waiver dies at that moment and never gets its own up-or-down vote, so every anchored amendment is one the motion left pending or only touched orthogonally. Suppression therefore never hides a fate clause — it only ever drops a tally-only line. **The arc's real content is that the STEP 0 gate fired and the model was wrong.** HO 554's M2 tripwire (dot vs implied fate, DISAGREE=0) had been measured against the **pre-555** classifier, and the probe pins a verbatim copy of `deriveDisposition` — so HO 557's first act (`7798997`, discharging the HO 556 WATCH on its first opportunity) was reconciling that copy to the shipped `2ce1d6c` version and re-running. **DISAGREE went 0→2**, both rows the exact pair HO 550's polarity validation had missed (`119-samdt-3946`/`3947`, "Motion to Table Failed" → "SA N fell when SA N-1 fell"): the tabling motion failed, so the model said *survived*; the amendment later fell with its parent, so the HO 555-widened dot said *killed*. **Both readings were individually true — the model was answering a different question than the dot.** `impliedFate` described the amendment's status **at the motion**; the dot describes its **outcome**. Those coincide in one direction only, and the arc had been reading a label coincidence ("survived" ≡ "agreed") as agreement. **The fix narrowed the model rather than patching the two rows** (`d213cb7`): `impliedFate` → **`motionFate(cls, outcome): "killed" | "undecided" | "orthogonal"`** — there is **no positive fate**. Tabling **agreed** → killed; waiver **failed** → killed; the opposite outcome of each is **undecided** (not tabled ≠ adopted; cleared the point of order ≠ agreed to), and cloture/chair are orthogonal always. Both fate-bearing classes are **kill mechanisms that decide in one direction only**. The rejected alternative is worth recording: suppressing the fate clause whenever it disagreed with the dot would have coupled the motion model to `deriveDisposition`'s vocabulary — so a future dot widening would silently change motion-line behavior — and would have hidden the modelling error instead of fixing it. **The re-gate is the contradiction shape that remains possible:** HALT if any row has `dot=agreed AND fate=killed`; measured **0** (`killed=55 · undecided=4 · orthogonal=5 · dot-agreed-in-killed=0`), and undecided/orthogonal rows assert nothing so they can't contradict by construction. **The build, three commits.** `568d58d` — a new leaf **`lib/amendment-motion-key.ts`** beside `amendment-vote-key.ts` (untouched), carrying the four anchored classifiers verbatim from the probe (table 9 / cloture 4 / chair 1 / waiver 50, **tail 0**, `null` for the tail so it's droppable by type), the number parse (waiver's `Amdt. No. N` = the same Senate `S.Amdt` namespace, HO 550's 42/43 sponsor match), and `motionFate`. **The rename is a type-safety device, not cosmetics:** `"agreed" | "failed"` is structurally assignable to `AmendmentVote["disposition"]`, so tsc would have allowed an implied fate into `dispositionColor`/`voteVerb` and printed "Agreed" on an amendment that merely wasn't tabled. `7027d10` — **`getSenateAmendmentMotions()`**, a corpus-wide **argument-less** cached sibling (64 rows total, so one cache key serves both consumers), two statements only, both HO 554 M4-measured: the residual select on `idx_votes_chamber_date` (**31ms/64**, no new index, no hint) and the `member_votes ⋈ members` split (**253ms/297**). It drops the probe's 64 per-row existence lookups (an unresolvable key is simply never read) and keys on **`votes.congress` per row**, not a `MAX(congress)` — multi-congress safe by construction. Distinct `AmendmentMotion` type, plain `Record` (HO 533), `try/catch → {}`, tags `["amendments","votes"]` (the `amendments` tag is consumer-coherence, not a FROM clause — noted in comment). `b40b462` — **`components/AmendmentMotionLine.tsx`**, a new sibling of `AmendmentVoteLine` (not a widened `VoteLine`, whose verbs are amendment-framed and wrong here), reusing the shared `partyColor` and the **HO 533 all-zero split suppression**; wired into `/bill/[id]` (the inner row in `BillAmendments.tsx` — *not* `AmendmentRow.tsx`, the HO 530 pathspec note) and the `/amendments` feed **page-scoped in `app/amendments/page.tsx`**, so `getAmendments` is untouched. **Suppression costs nothing and makes the two lines mutually exclusive:** the motion line renders only when the amendment has no anchored decisive vote, a check both sites already hold in hand — so `VoteLine` and `MotionLine` can never stack, and there is no ordering question. **No `+N earlier` counter** — M3 measured 1:1, so it would be structurally unreachable code documented as protection (the HO 549 discipline); the render maps the list instead, so a future second motion appears as a second line rather than hiding. **The dot never moves** — HO 555 already fixed it for these rows, and this HO adds evidence, not classification. **One measured over-claim, shipped deliberately (oddities + WATCH):** `motionFate(waiver, failed) = killed`, but M6 (`70a23ae`, added mid-build on the ruling) split the class **48 "ruled out of order" / 1 "withdrawn" (`119-samdt-5806`) / 0 neither** — so 5806 renders "→ amendment killed" against an action text saying it was *withdrawn*, contradicting HO 555's deliberate decision that withdrawn is neither. A 1-row over-claim against 48 correct ones doesn't justify narrowing, and the obvious fix (a token check on action text) is the dot-coupling the gate already rejected. **Numbering — the 555 → 557 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 556 was the doc sweep for 554–555. **No theme-% change — a v1 line on the existing amendments pillar (the 437/439–440/442/444/448/450/461/537/540/542/550–552 precedent); the combined `Lobbying / amendments / nominations` bar holds at 92.** This **discharges the HO 537 M8 / HO 550 QUEUED surface in full**. Docs: HO 558. **Also notes now run through HO 557.**

**Also (HO 559–561), the primaries arc — a banked pair closed on measurement (neither built) + the SC special-primary ingestion path; no theme-% change.** *Nothing here was built to the spec that opened it:* the banked display item targeted code HO 333 had already orphaned, its re-poll twin described a mechanism that already existed, and the real hazard — a scrape silently substituting one contest's results for another's — was found by reading the sync path, not by either measurement. **HO 559 (`6db8fd0`, read-only probe) took the two-year banked pair and closed both without a build.** *Display half — moot, not built:* `buildPrimariesCartogram`, `PrimaryDistrictCard`, `PrimaryRow`, `primariesFill`, `PrimariesLegend` are all ORPHAN; `PrimaryMapCard` and the `variant === "primaries"` branches live only inside `CartogramShell`'s primaries fork, which never executes because the sole `<CartogramShell>` (`ElectoralBoard.tsx:103`) passes `variant="races"`. The item's build target was unreachable code, and `PrimaryTimeline` (`:122`, `c.date <= todayISO`) already ships the date-driven model the item asked for. M3 confirmed **no user-visible wrongness survived HO 333**: `/race/[id]` renders `getRunoffsForRace` (runoff-round only), the member chip is date-only. *Re-poll half — already satisfied:* the cursor wraps forever and each unit delete-rebuilds, so "never re-polled" was false — M5(a) measured all **431** resulted contests rewritten after their date (1–7d=5 · 8–30d=129 · 31–90d=147 · 90+d=150, none ≤0d). **The measured cycle is 44.6 days** (471 units, mean 10.56 advanced/tick, 14/45 budget-stops), which HO 560 C3 halved rather than answering with a priority queue. **The population number was decomposing two different bugs.** Past-by-date 482→**612**, voted-by-results 407→**431**, gap 75→**181** — but **143** of that is zero-candidate (roster never populated) and only **38** is the item's actual target, has-roster-no-shares, none older than 90 days; the 143 is now its own backlog entry. **HO 560 — the hazard the probe didn't look for.** `syncSenateCandidates` delete-rebuilds `senate-{ST}-2026-{D,R,open}` without reading which contest the scraped votebox describes, and `syncCalendar` writes only 270toWin's state-uniform date — so Ballotpedia rebuilding a seat's page around a later contest would have produced **a row dated June 9 carrying the August field, June's results gone, no error anywhere**. Three commits: `87a4366` seeds `senate-SC-2026-special-{D,R}` (Aug 11, runoff Aug 25, `race_id=S-SC-2026`, **`election_round='primary'`** so it flows to `getPrimaryCalendar` → `PrimaryTimeline` as a new Aug 11 tick rather than needing a third round vocabulary every consumer would learn); `aa9093a` freezes **settled** rows (past-dated AND already carrying a share) against the rewrite — narrow on purpose so past-dated-but-shareless rows stay open (the exact mid-count population the re-poll item existed for), with `settledSkipped` surfaced and `reingest-primary-slate.ts` as the correction escape hatch; `65d0692` takes the cron to twice daily. **Freeze blast radius: 431 rows** (senate 42 / house 389). *Amendment before land:* the seed loader's empty-roster path was fixed to leave a roster **untouched** rather than clobber it (oddities). **HO 561 — the design that couldn't fire, and what replaced it.** The obvious route keyed off which URL served, since `scrapeSenateCandidates` already falls back to `senateSpecialPageUrl` and the success path preserves the winner on `result.url` — **but it never fires for SC**: the standard SC page returns 200 with the June regular, so the fallback is never reached, and S2 then found **0 of 35** standard pages missing (including FL and OH, whose specials that fallback was written for). So the special page is fetched **in addition**, gated on a seeded `-special-` row existing for that state, which closes the FL/OH regression surface by construction rather than by care. `5122a79` adds `scrapeSenateSpecialCandidates` to the scrape module (additive; the FL/OH fallback untouched) + the opt-in pass, with `buildSenateMatcher` and `isSettled` extracted so base and special writes make the identical call; `d521145` adds the dated trigger — seeded specials within **±7 days** of primary or runoff date and carrying no shares get scraped ahead of the cursor, cap 3 states/tick, cursor never advanced, budget shared from `DEADLINE_MS` (a cursor position cannot serve a fixed election date; at twice-daily ~14 attempts across each window). *Amendment before land:* the special fetch was moved into the scrape module so one copy of the Ballotpedia URL/UA/timeout semantics exists by construction (oddities). **It ships un-run:** Ballotpedia's SC special page was 404 at both 560 and 561, so C1's hit branch is unexercised and the path has only ever run its miss branch — WATCH-tracked with a scheduled proof date (~Aug 13). **No theme-% change — a correctness arc plus an ingestion path on the existing electoral pillar; Races holds at 99.** **Numbering — the 557 → 561 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 558 was the doc sweep for 557, HO 562 is this sweep (the HO 506/536/553 precedent). Docs: HO 562. **Also notes now run through HO 561.**

**Also (HO 563–564), the House roster-erasure arc — a live P1 found by a queued investigation, then fixed and repaired; no theme-% change.** *The queued "143 zero-candidate rows" investigation found a live data-loss loop, and the two backlog items it was meant to separate turned out to be one bug seen from both ends.* **HO 563 (`921e752`, read-only probe)** was sent to split three candidate causes for the 143 and came back with a fourth thing that outranked the question. **M1:** 143 rows (senate 1 / house 142) across 96 districts — whole-state failures (OH 15/15 · AZ 9/9 · NE 3/3 · ME 2/2 · ND/SD) alongside scattered ones (NY 24 of 26 · NC 8 of 14 · CA 8 of 52 · AL 6 of 7), with `updated_at` well after `primary_date`, so **not un-visited**. **M2, the deciding measurement:** a live scrape of all 96 → **ok=66 · no_section=13 · no_candidates=17 · no_page=0 · special=0** — not one bucket but two: two-thirds parse fine live while sitting empty in the database, the signature of a roster deleted and not yet rewritten, and the zero `no_page`/`special` **refuted the handoff's own §1 premise** that those statuses made these rows (neither reaches the write block). **M3, the finding that reordered everything:** of 40 sampled resulted House districts, **12 (30%) return `no_section` live** (AL-01 · AR-02 · CA-09 · GA-01 · NV-01 · NY-01 · OK-01 · OR-01 · PA-01 · KY-02 · MD-02 · MS-02), every one holding results, every one one cursor visit from the fall-through's delete-then-insert-nothing. **M4:** `.cache/` is uncommitted, so `readCachedHtml` returns null in production and M2/M3 are live Ballotpedia, not a local artifact. **M5:** cursor mid-sweep at house offset 288 of 435, the M1 rows written 2026-06-18 … 07-21 — visited recently and empty anyway. **The mechanism:** `syncHouseDistricts` sent `special` and `no_page` to `continue`, but `no_section` and `no_candidates` **fell through into the write block**, whose per-contest delete-then-insert then ran with an empty roster — a district whose page stopped parsing had its roster, and its results, deleted and replaced with nothing. **HO 560's guard could not have caught it:** that predicate refuses a *rewrite* on a settled row (substitution); this is *erasure*, and a wiped row has no share left to protect — which is why the queued House guard was the wrong shape before the probe ran. **The cadence interaction, as measured rather than assumed — a correction to this block's own first draft.** It originally asserted HO 560 C3 doubled the erasure rate and ran that way for about a day; **that was wrong, and the correction is the more useful record.** C3 (`65d0692`) landed **2026-07-29T18:14:38Z**, after that day's only 12:00 tick, so the first 00:00 slot was not due until 2026-07-30T00:00; before it arrived the cron was paused from the Vercel dashboard for the classifier outage, and the 564 fix went live roughly 71 minutes ahead of that slot. **Hour-00 ticks since C3 = 2, both post-fix** (the scheduled 00:00:47Z tick, which timed out, plus the 00:38:47Z manual confirmation tick) — and **0 ever ran the erasure code**. So **C3 was a latent amplification closed before it took effect**, and the erasure ran at the original once-daily rate for its entire life — which reframes the repair: **+124 rostered contests and +21 carrying a share reflect erosion across many daily cycles, not one accelerated day.** The methodological half is the reusable one: the doubling was asserted from a schedule *string* without checking whether the schedule had ever *run*, and a roadmap block is append-only, so the error would have been permanent — the claim-verification rule applied to the planning layer's own framing. The dashboard pause is worth recording on its own merits (no `vercel.json` change in either direction): these losses are the durable kind, because a row is erased *precisely because* its page fails to parse and the repair works by re-scraping, so the rows destroyed are exactly the ones re-scraping cannot recover. The cadence **stayed at twice daily** after the fix — halving the rate of a fixed bug buys nothing. **HO 564 (`154d779` C1 · `95fdd10` C2).** The rule shipped as *a sync must never convert absence of evidence into deletion*: `no_section` joins `special`/`no_page` and skips the write block (a missing anchor after three retries is a parse failure, not evidence a contest exists), `no_candidates` stays on the row-creating path (that page did parse), and the per-contest delete runs **only when the incoming roster is non-empty**, on both chambers. **Senate was widened in on structure, not measurement** — its status fork already skipped non-`ok`, but the empty-per-contest-roster path on a good parse was the same edge, sized by STEP 0 S3 at **28 rows** outside the HO 560 freeze. `rosterDeletesRefused` surfaces on both summaries and into `cron_runs.payload`, so the guard is visible rather than silent. **Falsified before it was recorded as protection** (the HO 549/553 discipline), on MD-05 with 27 resulted candidates: forced `no_section` left the roster byte-identical 27→27 with the skip logged; forced empty-`ok` returned `rosterDeletesRefused` naming both contests. Scratch reverted, tree clean, nothing committed. **Repair, measured:** House candidate rows **1,911 → 2,294** (+383), contests with a roster **594 → 718** (+124), contests carrying a share **389 → 410** (+21 — result sets recovered, not just rosters), zero-candidate **143 → 73**; refusals during the repair run NE 44 · S 34 · MW 1 · W 7. **The residual 73 is decomposed, not stuck:** 40 rows are a legitimately uncontested party whose sibling contest is rostered, and 32 rows across 16 districts are genuinely non-parsing (AL-02 · AR-01/03 · ME-01/02 · NC-02 · NY-02/05/08/16/18/20/22/26 · UT-04); the handoff's "~30" estimate mapped to the second group only, and the fix repopulated everything that parses while fabricating nothing that doesn't. **The confirmed post-re-enable tick** is `cron_runs` id 6042 (2026-07-30T00:38:47.600Z, success, unit house, cursor 332→344): `rosterDeletesRefused: []` — the field is **live in the prod payload**, which is what confirms the *wiring*, and its empty value means all 12 districts on that pass parsed cleanly so the guard had nothing to refuse. **An empty counter is not evidence the guard works** — the MD-05 falsification above is that evidence, and the two must not be conflated; likewise the presence-of-field discriminator is invalid on `timeout` rows (id 6035 has no finalized payload), so the pre/post-fix attribution rests on deploy timing, not on the field's absence. **No theme-% change — a correctness fix on the existing electoral pillar; Races holds at 99.** **Numbering — the 561 → 564 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 562 was the sweep for 559–561, HO 565 is this sweep (the HO 506/536/553/558 precedent). Docs: HO 565. **Also notes now run through HO 564.**

**Also (HO 566–568), the delete-rebuild erosion arc — the deliberate version of the 563/564 accident; no theme-% change.** The erosion at 563/564 was found by a probe aimed at something else, so the next idle slot aimed one on purpose: a grep of the delete-rebuild shape across sync code picked three targets, and **a read-only probe measured all of them before anything was scoped.** The map it returned inverted the fear — the STANDING wipe (committees, every roster revisited every 12h) had **zero victims**, the real damage sat frozen on the WINDOWED paths, and the derived recompute held its contract exactly. **HO 566 (`0c62a81`, read-only probe), five measurements.** **M1:** 236 committees (53 main / 183 sub), 10 zero-member — all select/joint/commission bodies **ABSENT from the roster source entirely**, so they never enter the write loop; source parsed 230, **0** source-rostered-but-DB-empty, **0** trailing-by-≥3, uniform 1.7h roster age. **M2:** 1,516 votes, **4** with a positive tally and ZERO `member_votes` (`house-119-2-253/254/269` · `senate-119-2-146`), the **1–4 partial bucket EMPTY in both chambers**, surpluses 0 — **the deficit shape did the diagnosing**: benign per-member skips produce partials, an inflow race produces all-or-nothing zeros. **M3:** both vote syncs **WINDOWED** (House watermark `MAX(vote_date)`, L354/L388; Senate `MAX(roll_call)` per session, L361/L373) — thin/empty risk is first-write-only, then frozen forever. **M4:** Senate `amendment_votes` computed 97 == materialized 97, drift 0/0, unmatched 0, residual 64 = the pinned anchors — the HO 537 pure-recompute contract's first external check. **M5** on the record: 2,259/2,624 zero-bill meetings = expected sparsity. Classification: **committees STANDING-zero-victims · member_votes WINDOWED with 4 frozen first-writes · amendment_votes INERT.** **HO 567 (diag `26879b5` · C1 `547d358` · C2 `f6aab1b`), the member_votes self-heal.** STEP 0 gated the cause before any build code: all four returned **populated rosters upstream with tallies matching the stored ones exactly** — a transient-publication race (the tally publishes before the member positions), not permanently memberless rolls. The design fork was decided for the **bounded self-heal pass over holding the vote row**: tallies stay live, permanent loss becomes bounded lag, and repair + fix are one mechanism (the pass's first run drained the 4 — no backfill script). Predicate is **zero-roster only** (`tally > 0 AND NOT EXISTS member_votes`, newest-first, `HEAL_CAP=10`/chamber): partials measured 0, a structural partial (a Senate resolver miss) would spin under a deficit predicate, and the zero-roster predicate is **self-limiting by construction** — a vote that heals even partially exits the set. It reuses the per-vote write unchanged; permanent retry per the HO 553 posture; `memberVotesHealed` / `memberVotesHealPending` into `cron_runs.payload` (live at id 6056). **Both branches proven** (the 549/553 discipline): a scratch-forced empty roster on `269` landed `pending=[269]` with the row unchanged while `253`/`254` healed live around it, scratch reverted clean, re-run healed it. Drain: all four to full tally, zero residual; the 566 probe re-ran as the **regression instrument** — M2(a) 0/0 (senate 871→873 = two new fully-populated votes synced during the drain). **The participation distortion, per the §0.4 read (not from memory):** a zero-roster vote **deflates the 119th participation denominator** — `getParticipationStrip` / `participationAggCte` / `getChamberParticipationContext` all compute `not_voting / COUNT(*)` over the member's OWN `member_votes` rows, so a vote with no rows is absent from every member's count and the missed-rate is measured over fewer votes than occurred; it does NOT inflate any individual MISSED (no member has a row to count as a miss). The career path (`member_career_votes`, Voteview cast-codes) is a different source and unaffected. **Render payoff:** `senate-119-2-146` is the **Kim Amdt No. 5545 budget waiver** — its HO 557 motion-line party split was all-zero → HO 533-suppressed; it now renders **44 D + 2 I yea / 53 R nay / 1 D nv**, and the waiver-failed → amendment-killed clause carries a real split. **HO 568 (`38ea8b0`), the committees delete gate.** The 564 rule enforced on the standing wipe — this time **the analogy WAS measured before porting** (566 M1 sized the same shape). The DELETE is pushed only when the **insertable count** (bioguide-bearing entries, NOT raw array length — the B falsification's point) is > 0; a refusal is **reported only when it protects existing rows** (the rostered/unrostered fork keeps `rosterDeletesRefused` signal-bearing at `[]`; with the 10 empties source-absent, the silent-skip branch is purely defensive today). The one-`db.batch` shape is preserved (HO 143). Falsified both variants on `hsag00` (53 rows byte-identical under an empty array AND insertless entries, code in `rosterDeletesRefused`; control `hsju00` rewrote normally; natural run restored, `[]`). Dissolution trade accepted: a genuinely emptied roster lingers at last membership until a manual one-off. **Two files, adjudicated:** the committees route **hand-picks** payload fields (unlike the primaries route's whole-result pass-through), so surfacing the field took one line in `app/api/cron/committees/route.ts` — guard #1's "one file" was inconsistent with the handoff's own §5.2 payload gate, so Code resolved toward the gate and flagged it; accepted, and the forwarding-shape difference is now a SKILL note. Payload live at id 6059. **No theme-% change across the arc** — a probe plus hardening on existing pillars; no bar moved. **Numbering — the 564 → 568 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 565 was the sweep for 563–564, HO 569 is this sweep (the HO 506/536/553/558/562 precedent). Docs: HO 569. **Also notes now run through HO 568.**

**Also (HO 570), the cleanup arc — six deferred "next-touch" cleanups discharged in one pass, six clean-revert commits; no theme-% change. The block's content is not the deletion count — it's that STEP 0 fired and the target list was wrong.** Each item had been deferred to "the next touch" of a file nobody was touching; this was that touch. **STEP 0 saved two live surfaces the backlog had marked dead.** The backlog named `.v2f-sponsor*` dead — but `.v2f-sponsor-last` / `--plain` are **live** (`V2FeedList.tsx:123,130`), and the `.v2f-sc-*` sponsor-card block sitting *immediately beside* the "these are dead" comment is **live**, rendered by `BillExpandPanel.tsx:224` and revealed by `.bxp-sponsor:hover .v2f-sc-card` (`globals.css:4456`). A prefix sweep would have silently killed the sponsor hover card HO 472 verified; the comment naming the dead set was itself the vector, so **C1 rewrote it rather than deleting under it**. **C1 (`ef36547`)** — 20 dead selectors to zero, `app/globals.css` **9,713 → 9,269 (−444)**, braces balanced 1468/1468: the HO 317 private-Expand orphans, the HO 306 `.hill-band*` family, the HO 366 `/hearings` chrome, the HO 306 list-view filter bar, the HO 367 `.mc-fbar-title`. **Flagged deviation:** C1 also dropped `.hearings-filter-count` + `.hearings-filter-opt.is-active*` (0 consumers) beyond the handoff's literal list, and said so. **C2 (`29a7e66`)** — `getMembersRankedCount` + 8 `lib/hearings` exports (+ the internal `HEARING_TYPES_SET`). Two kept, **and the handoff was wrong about one:** `HEARING_BADGES` produces the live `HearingBadge` type, and **`weekdayOfKey` is live via `mondayOfKey` inside the module** — the planning grep excluded `lib/hearings.ts` from its own consumer search and missed it (the same class of error as the glob; Code caught it and refused the delete). **C3 (`95cf48e`)** — the dup key was **`:96 key={id}` over `billIds`**, not the `:82` issue-code map the backlog named: **82 filings in the scoped drill blob carry duplicate bill IDs because the precomputed `readLdaTables` path has no `SELECT DISTINCT` while the live `hydrateFilings` path does — and the HO 440 distinct-filings ranking is unaffected**, because `computeBillDrill` inverts `billsByUuid` through a `Set<string>` (`lda-rollup.ts:459–464`) so a bill repeated inside one filing contributes its uuid once; the duplication only ever escaped through the `FilingSummary.billIds` display field (`:482`), which is where it was fixed (deduped both arrays). Retired the allowlist entry; `MissingSecret` kept. **C4 (`c229145`)** — `verify-deploy.ts` `process.exit` → `process.exitCode` (live-proven: `verify:deploy` exited 0), the `sync-fec.ts:4` stale "1000/hr" comment corrected to the real 60/hr tier, the dead `getSponsorStates` probe that had produced the HO 404 audit's **false SLOW: 34,656ms**. **C5 (`33dfb3e`)** — the amendments repair gate moved from `stored >= pagination.count` to full enumeration (`missing.length === 0`), matching the nominations fix at `e175fd7`; `RepairResult` now carries `distinctLive` + `duplicates`. **Falsified, so recorded as proven protection not a WATCH:** a scratch fetch monkeypatch served the real 6,975-id stored set plus one duplicate with `pagination.count=6976`; the real `repairAmendments` returned `duplicates:1, complete:true` in **pass 1**, where the old gate (`6975 >= 6976`) would have looped to `maxPasses` with `complete:false`. Harness deleted, tree clean, nothing committed from it. **C6 (`af8378e`)** — the president predicate-wait (`waitForURL(/[?&]stage=president/)` replacing the 1.5s fixed wait; the `toContain` assertion kept; the crawl settle at `:162-163` and the sites at `:275`/`:308` untouched) plus an explicit `/races` → `/electoral` landing assertion. **Code's correction, carried:** the "HO 502 pattern" the HO 570 handoff told it to extend **does not exist in the file** — `/news` and `/president` became real routes, so there was no per-route `redirectedFrom()` assertion to copy (the claim came from the backlog). **The convention problem, stated as a finding:** the ledger's header rule (a backlog line's mechanism is a claim) notes two wrong in three HOs and says a third makes it a convention problem — this arc produced **two more genuine wrong-mechanism claims** (the `.v2f-sponsor*` glob, the HO 502 redirect pattern) plus stale line numbers in three places, so it is past the threshold; promoted to a real backlog item rather than another tally mark. **No theme-% change** — cleanup on existing surfaces, no bar moves, no new surface, no migration, no cron change. **Numbering — the 568 → 570 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 569 was the doc sweep for 566–568, and docs-only sweeps are owed no roadmap block (the HO 506/536/553/558/562/565 precedent). Docs: HO 571. **Also notes now run through HO 570.**

**Also (HO 572), the `#lobby-drill` anchor restored + the schedule-trigger answer — a correctness fix on an existing surface plus a read-only check; no theme-% change.** **The fix (`290e239`, two files).** `TopicCrosswalk`'s code chips link to `/lobbying?issue=…#lobby-drill`, but the HO 486 redesign had removed the target element — so a click dropped the reader at page top. The id is restored on the **stable `.mc-content lob-content` pane container** (`app/lobbying/page.tsx:416`), **not** the scoped `.mc-ctx` block: a well-formed-but-unknown `?issue=` resolves `selectedDrill = null` and falls back to unscoped (`:117`), so the scoped block doesn't render and an id inside it would dangle again through a narrower door. A comment at the id records the placement so nobody "corrects" it onto the scoped block. **Why restore rather than drop the fragment** (the fork HO 571 filed with two options): the crosswalk renders in `.lob-secs` **below** the pane (`:493` vs `:416`, the HO 492 side-by-side layout), so a reader clicking a code chip is at the bottom of the page with ~400px of chrome (masthead + two-row nav + readout + blurb + fbar) between them and the scoped answer they asked for; dropping the fragment would have made the test green **by deleting the behavior instead of restoring it** — the same locate-the-reader's-selection principle already applied at HO 492 (`scrollIntoView({block:"nearest"})` when a bounded rail re-rendered with its selection out of sight) and HO 517 (the rail pin when a deep-linked member landed on a rail with nothing lit). **The assertion promoted soft → hard** (`e2e/fit-finish.spec.ts:470`, the HO 473 precedent: soft while a finding is open, hard once fixed). The STEP 0 diagnostic is the interesting half: the failure was **not** a soft red — `drill.evaluate(...)` ran against a zero-element locator and rejected *before* `expect.soft` was reached, which is why it surfaced as a hard failure and why the `codeChip.count()` guard above it was passing (chip present, target missing). Suite **1 failed → 0 failed (41 passed, 1 skipped)**. **Verified:** `inView` true at 1280×720 and the motivating short viewport; `?issue=ZZZZ#lobby-drill` → 200 with the scoped block absent, the anchor present, zero console errors, confirmed on prod; unscoped `/lobbying` unchanged (~1,380px against the HO 492/493 ~1,369 figure — data-dependent, an id adds no layout). **STEP 0(a) — the `schedule` trigger fires.** Two runs — 2026-07-29 (failure) and 2026-07-30 (success) — both landing **~85 minutes after the `0 15 * * *` slot** (recorded so a future reader doesn't treat a 16:2x run as anomalous). Latest green → the **Jul-23 open loop closes, and with it the whole Phase-2 Playwright-in-CI loop**, whose only surviving thread this was. Breakdown over the last 30: `deployment_status` 14 success / 12 skipped / 2 failure; `schedule` 1 success / 1 failure. **The finding — UNCHARACTERIZED.** An intermittent `pageErr=1` (uncaught page error) on **`/`** and **`/?stage=other_chamber`** in the `smoke.spec.ts` route crawl accounts for the 07-29 schedule red and both `deployment_status` reds; it sometimes recovers on retry #1 and sometimes fails both hits. **Ruled out:** the HO 473 multi-child SVG `<title>` hydration class — all four `<title>` sites (`PrimaryTimeline:145`, `BillsTimeSeries:123`, `CommitteeActivityChart:145`, `RaceDistrictModal:140`) carry a single template-literal child at `290e239`, so that fix held; and the global-`concurrency` cancellation WATCH (these are `failure`, not `cancelled`). **Untested hypothesis, flagged as such:** the two failing URLs differ only by a filter that changes the feed, so the shared dashboard chrome is the suspect surface, and its time-derived render inputs (the HO 183 cycling stamp, relative ages, the hearings three-hour `watchState` window, the tape) are the shape that mismatches only when a render straddles a boundary — **not to be written up as "probably the known #418 class"** (the grep above falsifies that specific mechanism, and scoping a probe on it would repeat the wrong-premise failure HO 570/571 spent two sweeps documenting). Its own probe HO. **No theme-% change** — a correctness fix on an existing surface plus a read-only check, no bar moves. **Numbering — the 570 → 572 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 571 was the doc sweep for 570 (docs-only sweeps are owed no roadmap block, the HO 506/536/553/558/562/565/569 precedent). Docs: HO 573. **Also notes now run through HO 572.**

**Also (HO 574), the `pageErr` probe — the artifact answered it, the design paid, and the failing set is still moving; no theme-% change.** **The design nearly didn't pay.** The probe was built **artifact-first** against a **14-day retention clock**: all four failed-run report artifacts were still live, and the captured `pageErr` message was **identical across all four runs** — a single cause. It is **React #418, `args[]=HTML`** — the hydration text-content mismatch variant. The message had been captured all along (`smoke.spec.ts:129` pushes `err.message`) but only the **count** was ever printed (`:200`), so the diagnostic had existed for weeks yet was reachable only through an expiring artifact. **Route/hit breakdown:** run `30506188415` (`/?stage=committee`, `/?stage=other_chamber`, hit-2); `30510430349` (`/`, `/?stage=other_chamber`, hit-1 + hit-2); `30470412500` (the 07-29 schedule — `/`, four `?stage=` variants, `/president`, both hits); `30572418753` (five `?stage=` variants + `/members`, hit-2). **It fires on hit-1 as well as hit-2, which rules out the HO 533 cache-serialization class.** **H1 falsified.** `/president` and `/members` fired, and both are non-tape inner pages (HO 323/326 stripped the tape; no `MarketsTape` in `HeaderBar`), so the HO 572 "tape-bearing set" observation does not hold — killed on the artifact route pattern rather than a local test, the stronger evidence. **The block's real lesson — the firing set has been re-characterised twice, from growing samples.** Two runs supported "tape-bearing"; four supported "the relative-age feed surfaces," after two non-tape pages fired. **Neither characterization was wrong on its data; both were premature** — the tell is that the set *grew* with sampling (2 routes → 6 → 6 including a non-tape inner page) rather than converging. **The current characterization is also provisional, and the fix HO must not scope around the observed route set until it stops growing.** The confound is still live: those pages are also the heaviest client-island pages, so "relative-age" and "most islands" remain perfectly confounded. **H2 supported, component unidentified.** Time-derived hydration is the shape that fits (#418 by message, both hits, sometimes recovering on retry), but it is a **new** cause, not the HO 473 `<title>` mechanism — re-confirmed falsified, all four `<title>` sites single-child at `290e239`; nearest prior art is the HO 489/490 relative-age `nowMs` arc, a candidate the fix HO *starts* from, not a conclusion this one reached. **Not reproducible locally** — two full local smoke crawls, route-crawl `pageErr=0` throughout; recorded as a **result, not a gap** (the handoff pre-authorised it — an unreproduced intermittent with the instrument shipped is a complete outcome). **C1 (`57395b9`, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` only):** a second log line, emitted only when non-empty, carrying the actual messages whitespace-collapsed + truncated to 200 chars each, `pageErr` first. **Falsified before recorded as working** (the HO 549/553 rule): a scratch client-only throw in `CyclingTimestamp` produced a real `pageerror` through the unchanged capture and the new line printed it, console messages cut at 200; scratch reverted, nothing committed. **Handoff premise corrected (a doc error, not a code one):** the HO 574 handoff said `attachCollectors`/`logClean`/`assertClean`/`settle` are shared between the specs — **they are not.** `fit-finish.spec.ts:56` defines its own `attachCollectors` (and its own `GATE_COOKIE` `beforeEach`); it imports only `isKnownNoise` from `console-noise.ts`. The claim came from SKILL `:1913` (corrected in the HO 575 SKILL commit), so C1 is smoke-only and fit-finish is provably unaffected (re-confirmed 41/0). **No theme-% change** — a read-only probe plus test instrumentation, no user-facing surface (the HO 379 / 382–386 / 503–504 / 548 harness precedent). **Numbering — the 572 → 574 pointer jump skips no block:** HO 573 was the doc sweep for 572. Docs: HO 575. **Also notes now run through HO 574.**

**Also (HO 576–577), the primaries data arc — the member-hub primary chip corrected + the house-date self-referential clobber fixed and repaired; no theme-% change.** Two code arcs on one subject; 577 came directly out of 576's STEP 0. **HO 576 — `getPrimaryForRace` over structured columns (`e3cf396`).** The member-hub chip reconstructed the id `senate-${state}-2026-${party}` literally, which **structurally could not match a `-special-` row**, so SC's Aug 11 senate special was invisible and the chip showed a stale June 9; replaced with a query over structured columns. **STEP 0 earned itself twice.** M3a **proved the handoff's own proposed query wrong** — with no `district` restriction it leaked per-district house rows and would have "fixed" 127 nulls with arbitrary district dates. And it surfaced the design question the backlog line hid: the caller passes `district = null` for House **and** Senate members by design, so "prefer the soonest future contest" hands a House member a Senate-only special that isn't theirs. **The approval was self-contradictory, and Code named the contradiction rather than picking one and proceeding:** special-exclusion, a delta matching M3b's 2 keys, and no caller change could not all hold simultaneously for a chamber-blind function. The resolution was a **`memberChamber` argument** — named that way, not `chamber` (which reads as row-selection) or `includeSpecial` (which would put the policy in the caller) — with **no `p.chamber = ?` in the WHERE**, so no House chip was emptied. The rule, as written into the code comment: **a Senate special is a Senate-only contest, so it is never a valid proxy for a House member's primary in any state** — the senate-row-as-proxy convention works because regular Senate and House primaries share a date, and a special is exactly the case where that coincidence breaks. Derivable, not tuned to SC. Special-test is the id substring (no column marks it), isolated in one named predicate flagged as the single site to change if an `is_special` column ever lands. **Verified delta: SC's two senators only** — 532 unchanged, 0 newly populated, 0 lost chips.

**HO 577 — the house-date clobber. The defect class, not the symptom: a table must not derive its writes from an unfiltered aggregate over itself.** `calByState` was `MAX(primary_date) GROUP BY state` with no filters, so it computed "the latest date on any row this state owns," not "this state's primary date." Specials and runoffs won by construction — and once a house row absorbed the wrong date it was `election_round='primary'` and **won MAX itself on the next tick: the loop was already self-sustaining.** Measured (M1a–M1c): **3 states — LA, GA, SC — 48 house rows**, cause tally **special=2 · runoff=3 · house=48** — the house bucket dominating is the finding, the special/runoff were the injection sources but no longer the active cause. Excluding only special and runoff was therefore **provably insufficient**: **`district IS NULL` is load-bearing because it severs the self-referential class rather than narrowing it** — every row that can feed back carries a district. **M7 forced the shape:** 15 states own house rows but have no clean statewide senate row (AZ CA CT HI IN MD MO ND NV NY PA UT VT WA WI) — exactly HO 209's 35-state gate, with no stale pre-gate rows surviving — so a bare `district IS NULL` filter would have nulled their dates, and the write instead **COALESCEs to preserve** the existing value. **The consequence is recorded, not buried:** those 15 states' house dates are now **frozen with no refresh path** — strictly better than circular and the right call, but new latent staleness, which gets its own WATCH. Part 1 resolved **without calendar-threading** — the cron walks a cursor and the House slice already reads the statewide date from the DB's senate rows (`syncCalendar` seeds them from 270toWin), so the fix lives entirely inside the House sync and the scrape stays out of the per-tick path. **LA is a different failure mode entirely and its facts are recorded here because nothing in the repo can rederive them:** Governor Landry's **EO 26-038 (2026-04-30)** suspended Louisiana's U.S. House primaries only, following SCOTUS in *Louisiana v. Callais* striking the congressional map as a racial gerrymander; per the FEC, **neither the May 16 Senate primary nor the June 27 Senate runoff was affected**, so `senate-LA-2026-*` remains correct and a valid `calByState` source. The order ran "until July 15, 2026 or such time as determined by the Legislature" — **that date has passed**, the Legislature approved a new map in late May, and LA may have reverted to the jungle primary for U.S. House — so **no date is correct for the LA house rows, and "suspended" is itself a claim with an expiry.** LA modeled **postponed, not cancelled**: `primary_date`/`runoff_date` NULLed via a `HOUSE_PRIMARY_SUSPENDED={LA}` carve-out, **`primary_type` left alone** — that column is a *type* (open/closed/jungle), not a status; encoding `suspended` there would break precisely when LA needs `jungle`, and it's the same column where SC's `open` is correct. **The repair (Stage 2, `--apply`):** date-only, **GA 28 → 05-19, SC 14 → 06-09, LA 6 → NULL**, correct dates **derived from the clean statewide row, not hardcoded**, both externally verified against the public calendar — GA's clobber was the June 16 runoff, SC's was the Aug 11 special. Date-only because GA's 28 rows are settled and the tick's roster delete-rebuild would churn decided results; verified **48/48 converged, GA roster `80→80` / `28→28` untouched**. **The review caught two amendments before land:** every SELECT and UPDATE carries `id NOT LIKE '%-special-%'` so a house special can't be swept, and the GA roster check captures a fingerprint **before and after** rather than an after-only count that reads the same either way. **Ordering was the whole risk.** GA's 28 rows are settled, so a guard landing before the repair would have frozen them at the wrong date — **Part 1 → repair → falsification → guard, across two deploys because they cannot ship together.** The House `isSettled` guard (parity with the Senate path ~`:707`) is **untriggered the moment it lands**, and an untriggered guard reads identically to a broken one — so the falsification drove the **real** `syncHouseDistricts` against a settled GA-01 and proved it fires: `settledSkipped` names both ids, `primary_date` and the roster's `updated_at` both unchanged (the delete-rebuild never ran). **The interlude:** prod went down mid-arc on an **account-wide Turso rows-read block (~3h, no code fix)** — STEP 0 could not run and was committed **UNRUN** rather than validated against fixtures (fixtures would have produced numbers that looked like findings and weren't); attribution afterward put the burn on **CBT, not psephos** (psephos peaked ~1.7M and was declining; CBT stepped ~10× around Jul 25 and plateaued near 85–90M/day). Live at **`3391850`** (Part 1 `2e8cbbe` · repair `eb88959` · guard `3391850`), two-stage fast-forward through a review ref. **No theme-% change** — a correctness fix and a data repair on existing surfaces. **Numbering — the 574 → 577 jump skips no block:** HO 575 was the doc sweep for 574, and 576–577 are covered by this one block. Docs: HO 578. **Also notes now run through HO 577.**

**Also (HO 579–580), two cron-hygiene fixes on the trades + LDA paths — no theme-% change (a log-noise correction and a cron-topology fix, no user-facing surface).** **HO 579 — the FMP `page` 402 is a plan boundary, not an error (`54f0590`).** `/api/sync` had logged `[sync] trades error: FMP house-latest page 1 failed: 402` every tick; confirmed by hand against the live endpoint — page 0 returns data, page 1 returns the 402 — because the account is on FMP's **Free plan, which permits `page=0` only**. **It's an entitlement gate, not a rate limit:** the FMP usage meter read **0/250 calls and 0 MB/512 MB** while the 402s fired, so nothing was misconfigured or over quota. `lib/fmp.ts` gained `FmpPlanBoundaryError` + `isFmpPlanBoundary`, keyed on **402 AND the page-parameter text** (not 402 alone, so a payment failure on any other parameter stays a real error — proven both ways: a synthetic limit-parameter body returns false, and a forced 404 still lands in `errors` with `isFmpPlanBoundary=false`); `lib/trades-ingest.ts` records `planCappedAtPage` on the result instead of pushing to `errors`, and `/api/sync` carries `tradesPlanCap` per chamber. **The design call worth recording:** `maxPagesPerChamber` was deliberately **not** capped at the call sites (3 cron / 20 manual) — hardcoding `1` would work today and quietly cap a future paid plan, whereas detecting the boundary means an upgrade needs no code change. Nothing is lost operationally (the `-latest` endpoints return newest-first, so page 0 still captures every new disclosure; only per-tick backfill depth is forgone). **The honest gap:** the post-deploy prod tick was warm (`inserted=0` → short-circuit before page 1), so it exercised the clean stop but not the `planCappedAtPage=1` recording — proven locally against the real 402 and self-closing into a log line on the next tick that reaches page 1 (WATCH). **HO 580 — the LDA rollup and sync were competing for one budget (`e459fe9`).** The rollup started only if `msLeft >= ROLLUP_RESERVE_MS (220_000)` against a 300s ceiling, so **the sync had to finish within 80s**; the first authenticated run made **259 `lda.gov` calls at ~1s each** (~259s), starving the rollup by more than 3× the margin. **The inversion is the finding:** the rollup ran only when the sync had nothing to do and was starved precisely when new filings arrived — `route.ts:45-48` accepted "a day stale" but the real behaviour was stale indefinitely until a hand-run `npm run lda:rollup` (last refresh 114,048ms; 129,105 filings / 277,703 activities). Neither component was wrong; the coupling was. **Change 1:** a dedicated **`/api/cron/lda-rollup` at `0 22 * * *`** (the previously-banked standalone slot, now built), with the rollup block + `ROLLUP_RESERVE_MS` + `ROUTE_CEILING_MS` removed from the sync route; the new route can `revalidateTag("lda")` which `scripts/rollup-lda.ts` cannot (Next-runtime only), so the surface no longer waits on a TTL, and the manual script stays the backfill hatch. **Slot correction recorded:** the handoff proposed `0 10`, which is **taken by `/api/sync-votes`** — the instruction to verify against `vercel.json` rather than trust the handoff is what caught it; `0 22` is clear of every daily cron, markets (`0 */4`, last 20:00), the weekday `?source=fmp` pass (`0,30 13-21`, last 21:30), and kalshi (`15 */2`). **Change 2:** the deadline check at `lda-sync.ts:411` ran *before* `fetchPage` while the 429 retry sleep happened *inside* it — a check passing at 279s then a `retry-after=39` sleep landed at 318s and the function was SIGKILLed instead of exiting with `deadlineHit` (the observed 504). `fetchPage` now returns **`null`** when the sleep would cross `deadlineMs`, and the caller treats it as `deadlineHit`, **not** `fetchError` (different destinations — reporting it as an error would convert a clean pause into recorded failure); `onThrottle` still fires so `throttled429` keeps counting the real 429; backfill passes `deadlineMs: undefined`, leaving the branch inert. **Exactly one `return null` exists in the fetch path**, so no other condition can be misread as a deadline pause. **The falsification was two-sided** (why it's recorded): BAIL (`retry-after=60`, deadline ~35s out) → bailed, `deadlineHit=true`, `fetchErrors=0`, elapsed 31s against the ~91s a real sleep would have produced; CONTROL (`retry-after=2`, deadline far) → slept, proceeded, 16 pages — a guard that always bailed would pass a bail-only test and be badly wrong, and elapsed time is physical evidence rather than a reported flag. First runs after deploy: sync at 21:38 (`pages=19`, no rollup line at all), rollup at 21:39 (`rollup ok in 20219ms`). **Numbering — the 577 → 580 jump skips no block:** HO 578 was the sweep for 576–577, and 579–580 are covered by this one block. Docs: HO 581. **Also notes now run through HO 580.**

**Also (HO 582), the markets tape read burn — a query-cost fix, no theme-% change (a read-budget/infrastructure fix; the only user-facing effect is a latent double-render removed).** `getLatestMarketTicks` (the tape query) had `revalidate: 60` — **equal to `MarketsTapeClient`'s `POLL_MS`** — so the SWR TTL expired every minute and any open tab drove ~1 regeneration/min around the clock (~1,440/day). **STEP 0 (`markets-read-burn-582.ts`, `3baf98c`, read-only, measured-not-reasoned per the read-budget WATCH):** 12,240 rows/regen × ~1,440 = **~17.6M rows/day per open tab, ~20% of the ~88M/day plateau** — NOT small, so the tape is a real contributor and this ships as more than hygiene. **C1 (`cf1516d`) is the ~60× lever** — `revalidate 60 → 86400`, a bounded-staleness backstop with the single tag-flushing writer (the markets cron's `revalidateTag("markets")`) as the sole refresh path; a regen marker (`[markets] regenerating market-ticks-latest`) proves the new cadence, and the false "no per-request DB hit between writes" header comment on `/api/markets/latest` is corrected true. **C2 (`8396068`) ~1.5×** — Q2's spark window `julianday(ticked_at) >= julianday('now','-9 day')` was non-sargable (the column wrap defeated `idx_market_ticks_symbol_time`, reading every row each spark symbol owns: 5,636 read to return ~1,619); replaced with a sargable JS-ISO `ticked_at >= ?` bound (STEP 0 M1 confirmed the stored `new Date().toISOString()` format, M3a proved row-for-row equivalence, EXPLAIN is now a per-symbol range SEARCH). **C3 (`2a86c3f`) was initially SKIPPED as a no-op and that was WRONG — the reasoning error is the record, not just the outcome:** STEP 0 read Q1's `SCAN market_ticks USING COVERING INDEX` and called it "index-only, cheap," but that IS the scan branch of the gate, and index-only is a *latency* property while this HO's currency is *rows read*. After C2 the covering scan was **~6,604 of the ~8,223 residual (~80%)** and **grew with table history, not symbol count** (~100 rows/day trends toward ~43k/regen within a year). C3 replaces the MAX-GROUP-BY join with N per-symbol `WHERE symbol=? ORDER BY ticked_at DESC LIMIT 1` seeks UNION ALL'd (all N SEARCH arms, no temp b-tree, **~19 rows read against the join's ~6,604**, equal ~31ms latency — the ~1,638 figure is the *combined* post-fix per-regen total, Q1 ~19 + Q2 ~1,619, not C3's own read), bounding it at symbol count (oddities — rows-read vs milliseconds are different currencies). **C4 (`d0feb8f`)** visibility-gates the poll (skip when `document.visibilityState==="hidden"`, poll-on-return; `POLL_MS` unchanged) — defense in depth at the source. **Two behavior changes, both strict improvements (STEP 0 M3):** the 13 retired legacy symbols (DOW/GOLD/…) are no longer read (the join returned them, the loop discarded them via `if (!meta) continue`), and **CPI+WTI — which had two identical rows tied to the millisecond on `ticked_at` (a same-run double-write) and which the join therefore rendered TWICE on the tape — dedupe at read time** via `LIMIT 1` (the write-path defect is a separate P3 backlog item with its own instrument; a read-time dedupe removes the *symptom*, oddities). **Verification is PARTLY OPEN — recorded as such, not written up closed:** `/api/version`=`d0feb8f` confirmed, and local + prod `/api/markets/latest` verified the deduped 18-symbol payload with sparks intact; but **freshness-after-the-next-cron-tick, the marker cadence in runtime logs, and the 24h Turso rows-read slope were all pending at sweep time.** **The prediction, on the record before the measurement lands (append-only, so it stands either way):** the plateau was a *step* (~10× ~Jul 25, a ~79M/day jump), the tape has polled since HO 172, and `market_ticks` starts 2026-05-27 — so the tape accounts for **at most ~17.6M of the ~79M step**; C1+C2+C3 should cut the daily slope by **roughly a fifth — a level shift, not a resolution**, and if the slope barely moves the burn model is wrong and *that* is the finding. The read-budget OPEN LOOP is **partly discharged**, not closed — it stays open aimed at summarize / LDA / traffic for the residual ~80% (backlog). **Numbering — the 580 → 582 jump skips no block:** HO 581 was the doc sweep for 579–580 (docs-only sweeps are owed no roadmap block, the HO 506 precedent). Docs: HO 583. **Also notes now run through HO 582.**

**Also (HO 584), the LA House 2026 restore — the "small data fix" that was the HO 577 clobber through the front door; no theme-% change (a correctness fix on an existing surface).** The backlog item read as a one-line unlock ("drop LA from `HOUSE_PRIMARY_SUSPENDED` in the same change that writes a real date") and **it wasn't** — that precondition was **necessary and insufficient.** HO 577's non-suspended branch COALESCEs the House date from `calByState`, LA has a clean statewide **senate** row (`senate-LA-2026-*` @ 2026-05-16), so unlocking alone would have COALESCEd 05-16 onto all six `house-LA-*-open` rows — **the exact HO 577 clobber, re-entered through the mechanism 577 shipped as the fix.** STEP 0 (`la-house-restore-584.ts`, `11f7735`, read-only, non-SELECT-refusing) measured the six rows and what the unlock would write (M2 = 2026-05-16, the §0 argument confirmed). **The root cause is the convention, not Louisiana:** the senate-row-as-proxy holds only while a state's chambers **share a date AND a system**, and **Act 7 of the 2026 Regular Session broke both at once** — the House runs a jungle all-candidate primary on Nov 3 (an "open general" Dec 12 if nobody clears a majority) while the Senate runs closed party primaries on May 16. So this is the **second instance of the HO 576 rule** (*a Senate contest is not a valid proxy for the House when the coincidence breaks*), a **statute standing in for 576's special.** **The fix (`c40c447`) generalizes the mechanism:** `HOUSE_PRIMARY_SUSPENDED` (a force-NULL lock) → a per-state **`HOUSE_PRIMARY_OVERRIDES`** map carrying `{primaryDate, runoffDate, primaryType}`; an override WINS over `calByState` (no COALESCE — it is authoritative), every other state is unchanged, and **suspension is now expressible as a NULL-dated override**, so HO 577's suspended/COALESCE split collapses to one branch keyed on override presence. LA → `{2026-11-03, 2026-12-12, jungle}`. **The override covers `primary_type`, not just the dates, for a non-obvious reason:** `primaryTypeFor` DEFAULTS everything outside top_two/top_four/ranked_choice to `open`, so LA senate's stored `open` is a **fallback, not a determination** — a correct future upstream fix to its true `closed` (Act 1/640, LA's first Senate party primaries since 2010) would otherwise stamp `closed` onto six all-party House rows via the proxy; writing `jungle` is safe against that *correct* fix, not against today's value (oddities, the no-op-that-depends-on-an-upstream-bug). **External facts with their expiry (nothing in the repo can rederive them):** Act 7 + the LA SoS calendar, **verified 2026-07-31**; litigation over the suspension and the new map was still live, so "restored to jungle" is itself a claim with an expiry — re-check before treating a future discrepancy as a bug. **Terminology trap recorded so a future reader doesn't "repair" it:** LA's statute calls the Nov-3 contest the **"open primary"** and Dec 12 the **"open general,"** but CBT's `open` means *party primaries any voter may join* (what SC has) — a structurally different system — so LA is `jungle` and Dec 12 is modeled as `runoff_date`; the SoS wording must not prompt a correction to `open`. **The row shape was already correct (HO 93.5) but the reasoning under it was dead:** the six `-open` rows came from Ballotpedia voteboxes against a handoff premise (LA closed-partisan for 2026, Act 1/640) that *Louisiana v.
