Callais* and Act 7 killed — right answer, dead reasoning — so C2 (`6188ac8`) **re-commented the `NONPARTISAN_HOUSE_STATES` membership around Act 7 rather than re-deriving it, and inverted the tripwire** (under Act 7 there should be no D/R LA House voteboxes, so the structural guard firing now means Act 7 was superseded, not that Ballotpedia drifted). **Verification is COMPLETE here, unlike 582's partly-open close** — the falsification drove the real `syncHouseDistricts` and **all six rows are 2026-11-03 on prod.** But the method itself is the subject of a new discipline rule: **a leg proved the *defect* by writing the 05-16 clobber to the production DB and relying on the fix to undo it, rather than proving branch-and-args untouched** — survivable only because the primaries helpers are uncached (SKILL + oddities). **The caveat, in the same breath:** the fix is write-side only — `getPrimaryForRace` still resolves a LA House member's chip from the statewide **senate** row (05-16), so **the member chip and the six House rows now disagree on prod today**, a live user-visible inconsistency this HO created (the read-side of the convention it fixed write-side, same root as HO 576's `memberChamber` fork; backlog). **Numbering — the 582 → 584 jump skips no block:** HO 583 was the doc sweep for 582 (docs-only sweeps are owed no roadmap block, the HO 506 precedent). Docs: HO 585. **Also notes now run through HO 584.**

**Also (HO 586), the LA/at-large House member-chip read side — the read-side of the convention HO 584 fixed write-side; no theme-% change (a correctness fix on the member-hub primary chip, an existing surface).** Entered as "the LA chip shows May 16" — the live inconsistency HO 584 *created* (write-side corrected the six `house-LA-*-open` rows to Nov 3, but `getPrimaryForRace` still proxied the chip off the statewide `senate-LA-2026-*` row @ 05-16) — and ended as the general **district-first resolution**. **What licensed the promotion from LA-only to general is the M3 full-corpus delta:** every current member's chip computed under the shipped query and the proposed one — **534 members, (c) non-null→null = 0** (no regression, the gate), **senate changed = 0** (the proxy path byte-identical), **every (b) date-change was exactly the LA six** (May-16 proxy → their own Nov-3 jungle row), and the **160 (a) null→non-null fills were precisely the HO 576 top-two / no-2026-Senate-seat carry-forward**, closed here as a side effect (a party-keyed statewide senate proxy structurally can't serve CA/WA's `-open`-only rows or the ~21 no-Senate-seat states; the member's own `house-{ST}-{DD}` row can). **The fix is three touches, not one — the M1 finding was that it couldn't live in `queries.ts` alone:** the query (a district-first branch preferring the member's own `house-{ST}-{DD}-2026-*` row, `p.party = ? OR 'open'`, falling to the statewide proxy only on no-row-found — never on found-but-empty), the caller (`app/members/[bioguideId]/page.tsx` now passes `member.district` instead of a hardcoded null), and the render (the label reads the RESOLVED row's `party` over a three-value column — D→"Dem", R→"Rep", anything else, i.e. `open`/jungle, no party word: the honest all-party "Primary:"). **The at-large contract, made a convention:** `members.district` is NULL for the six at-large states (AK/DE/ND/SD/VT/WY) while `primaries.district` stores `"00"`, mapped at the query boundary (`district == null ? "00"`) — null-means-at-large for a House member; a stray null in a multi-district state finds no `"00"` row and degrades safely to the proxy, never a wrong match; the invariant that keeps that safe is that **no `"00"` row exists outside the six at-large states**. Five of the six at-large members sat invisible in M3's "unchanged" bucket — an **outcome gate can't see a branch that never fired** — and only M6, instrumenting WHICH branch ran, caught them (oddities). **The ORDER BY correction, attributed correctly:** the review spec said "exact party wins" without scoping it to a temporal class, and it shipped as the FIRST sort key — which would let a stale PAST exact-party row outrank a live FUTURE `open` one, defeating the chip's "next primary" semantic; the mandated re-run-after-change caught it, and the record is that the **spec was wrong, not the implementation** (fixed to future-first as the primary key, exact-party-over-`open` a tie-break WITHIN a temporal class, never across it; oddities). **The AK accounting note (so the arithmetic reads right):** the M3 delta split +2 fills / +4 same-date moves rather than the expected +3/+3 because Begich already carried a `senate-AK-2026-R` proxy — so AK is a same-date row-move, not a fill. **The countdown note (written down so it's never filed as a bug):** `primaryDays` (the 30-day amber countdown) now runs for ~430 more members than before this HO — LA countdowns appearing in early October, and CA/WA/AK ones on their own schedules, are expected behavior. **Verification COMPLETE on prod at `a1a97a8`** (the primaries helpers are uncached, so no cache convergence to wait out): four eyeballs through the shipped `getPrimaryForRace` — Carter LA-2 / Fields LA-6 → "Primary: 11-03-26" (no party word), Moran TX-1 → "Primary Rep: 03-03-26", McBride DE (NULL→"00", the at-large branch-1 path) → "Primary Dem: 09-15-26", Cornyn TX senate proxy byte-identical; no review-ref prod lag (~52s promotion). **The 77-of-560 empty-past-house-roster count M5 surfaced is pre-existing data debt** (invisible on the date-only chip), filed P3, not a 586 blocker. **Numbering — the 584 → 586 jump skips no block:** HO 585 was the doc sweep for 584 (docs-only sweeps are owed no roadmap block, the HO 506 precedent). Docs: HO 587. **Also notes now run through HO 586.**

**Also (HO 589–590), the `pageErr` #418 hydration arc — a diagnostic probe + a committed harness + two correctness fixes, and the OPEN LOOP is SHARPER but NOT closed — and explicitly NOT narrowed, since the two-cause partition is retracted below; no theme-% change (hydration-correctness fixes, no user-facing surface change).** The arc that HO 574 left open ("React #418, component unidentified"). **HO 589 (STEP 0, `259babd`) corrected two 574-era claims that had aimed every downstream hypothesis, and one is load-bearing:** (1) `args[]=HTML` is the **structural / markup** variant, **not** "text-content" — `react-dom-client.development.js:5240` fills the first `%s` with `fromText ? "text" : "HTML"` and CI captured `HTML`; a text mismatch reads `args[]=text`. (2) The "identical message ⇒ **single cause**" inference is **withdrawn** — the component-naming diff (second `%s`) is **DEV-only** (prod `react-dom` bundle carries 0 occurrences of the `hydration-mismatch` template/link), so the minified prod string is **byte-identical for every structural mismatch in the app** and message identity carries **zero** cause-count information; the unattended crawl can never self-identify a component, and raising HO 574's 200-char cap buys nothing. **The sample-attribution correction (so a future reader doesn't mis-credit it):** 589 sampled `30724601882 · 30583714867 · 30572418753 · 30595816107`; **HO 574 sampled `30506188415 · 30510430349 · 30470412500 · 30572418753`** — **only `30572418753` overlaps**, so 589's "`30595816107` is a transient Server-Components 500, not #418" applies to **589's own sample**, not to 574's record (writing it as "one of 574's four wasn't #418" would be a false correction to an append-only entry — the HO 587 misattribution class). Three near-disjoint runs is a **second** confirmation the failure is live in-window. **H4 (a static-prerender + `useSearchParams` skew) raised and FALSIFIED:** every prod-firing route builds `ƒ Dynamic`, so `useSearchParams()` gets real params server-side and cannot skew — and 590 M3b corrected the evidence table: **all five routes 589 read as `○ Static` are 3xx redirect stubs** (`/dashboard-v2`→`/`, `/committees`→`/members`, `/members/pass-rate`→`/members?sort=passrate`, `/primaries`+`/races`→`/electoral`), so "`○ Static`" meant "prerendered output" which for a redirect is the stub — **no static route serves tape HTML**, and the static-prerender concern dissolves with H4. **HO 590 rebuilt 589's scratch reproduction rig as a committed, local-only harness** (`scripts/diagnostic/hydration-clock-harness-590.ts`; needs `next dev` for React 19's DEV describeDiff, so it can't ride `e2e-prod.yml`), which manufactures the SSR-vs-hydration clock skew and captures the DEV owner tree + `args[]=HTML`/`text` variant — the guard-correction that a fix whose falsification instrument is *scratch* is protection recorded without being fired. **Two DISTINCT latent bugs found and fixed, kept distinct in the record — both are correctness hardening, NEITHER is proven to be a prod cause, and they are deliberately UNNUMBERED (see the partition retraction below):** **the markets tape (structural, `args[]=HTML`, `626c251`):** `MarketsTapeClient`'s `useState(() => Date.now())` gates the `<span class="markets-tape-arrow">` node via `showSlots`; under a stale/market-boundary skew the arrows collapse (harness: SSR 72 arrows → 0 on hydration; reconciled as `2 halves × reps(2) × 18` vs the post-mount `reps→1`, so `reps` is consistent across hydration and NOT the bug). Named via the DEV owner tree (`TickItem` → the arrow span), fixed by prop-drilling a server `nowMs` (the HO 489/490 shape). **The `lob-age` text drift (`args[]=text`, `601f54a`) — a SEPARATE defect:** `FilingRow.relAge()`'s render-time `Date.now()` drifts the "Nd ago" string across a day boundary; same prop-drill fix, proven by **string equality** (SSR text == hydrated text under skew), not the tape's node-count. **The finding that keeps the OPEN LOOP open (590 C5):** prod `/` is served **fresh per request** (`X-Vercel-Cache: MISS`, `Age: 0`, `no-store`, `Date` advancing), so the render→hydration gap is **sub-second** (harness `control(+0)` — that same gap — **never fires**) and **cannot straddle** a 26h/market boundary; `isMarketOpen` is TZ-explicit (`America/New_York`). So the tape and `lob-age` are real latent bugs but are **NOT the mechanism of prod's ~42 `args[]=HTML` fires**, which **remain unattributed** — a green crawl after these fixes is doubly ambiguous. **The "cause #1 / cause #2" partition is RETRACTED here (HO 591), because C5 destroyed the premise under it:** the two-cause split existed for exactly one reason — `/` carried the tape and the inner pages didn't, so they *had* to be different bugs — and C5 measured the tape out of prod. **The corrected statement: all ~42 prod `args[]=HTML` fires are UNATTRIBUTED — `/`, the six `?stage=` home variants, `/president`, and `/members` alike — not "one fixed, one remaining."** **Whether that is one cause or several is now UNKNOWN**, since message identity carries zero cause-count information (589 M1) and the evidence that once separated the routes is gone. **`/president` is kept named** — it fired in prod (HO 574) and is **tapeless**, and narrowing to "the members surface" would silently drop a route from a firing set whose *growth* is exactly what HO 574's scoping guard warns about. **One lead checked and RULED OUT (HO 591) — a shared client island spanning the firing set:** the repo has **no `SessionProvider` and no `useSession`** (auth resolves **server-side** in `HeaderBar` / `DashboardV2Header`, prop-drilled into `AuthButton`) and **`app/layout.tsx` is bare**. `/` and `/members` have **disjoint** directly-imported client islands (5 vs 9); the **three** shared ones come via their server headers — `AuthButton` (server-prop `user`, `useTransition` only), `CyclingTimestamp` (HO 489-verified safe), `MobileNavDrawer` (`useState(false)` constant seed) — **all deterministic at render.** Stated precisely, because the loose form is false: they **do** share three client components; the finding is that **none of the three can skew.** This is a ruled-out lead, **not** support for either partition. **`/members` remains the sharpest UNEXPLAINED observation** — it fired at `control(+0)` in the 589 sweep and prod fired `committees-redirect`→`/members` — but **37 fresh-context re-tries across 589+590 produced 0 fires** and the single-page-bleed hypothesis 589 floated **failed to reproduce** (590 C2), so those control fires stand unexplained rather than naming a second cause. **Falsification (three-sided, via the harness):** RED at HEAD (`/`@+40d FIRE, arrows 72→0) → GREEN after (no fire, arrows 36, and the full 175-scenario sweep 34+8 fires → 0) → CONTROL (a `+27h fastForward` still flips the tape to stale via the untouched 60s interval, 0 hydration errors — the fix doesn't freeze the staleness signal). **Verification on prod at `601f54a`** (`/api/version` → `601f54a9…`), **and read carefully what it does and does not cover — it covers exactly one thing: the fixes didn't break the tape.** The prop-drill's own risk is a *frozen* clock, which would surface as dead arrows or a stuck stamp, so: prod `/` (SSR, with `ct_seen`) serves **72 `markets-tape-arrow` + 60 `markets-tape-symbol` + 2 AS OF stamps**, prod `/dashboard-classic` **8 + 8**, `/api/markets/latest` **18 symbols**; and on the same SHA locally (prod withholds the *interactive* tape from headless — BotID, so the hover/hydrated read must be local), hydrated `/` settles to **36 arrows / 60 symbols / 36 change cells** — the documented `72 → reps→1` post-mount reconciliation, **not** a collapse — `/dashboard-classic` to **4 / 8**, the **AS OF stamp cycles** (CT→PT; UTC→ET), the untouched 60s poll **fires at ~63s with the arrow count unchanged at 36** (values poll, arrows survive it), and **0 hydration errors** fire on either page. **There is no cache convergence to wait out:** `nowMs` is a **per-request server value**, not a cached one — and prod `/` re-measured `X-Vercel-Cache: MISS` / `Age: 0` / `no-store`, C5's fresh-serve again. **What this expressly does NOT verify is anything in the OPEN LOOP.** Since C5 proved these fixes were **never the prod mechanism**, **a green `e2e-prod.yml` crawl is NOT verification** of this arc — not of the fixes, not of a cause, not of a close; the loop still closes only on a **named cause plus green runs**. **HO 574's block is SUPERSEDED, not retro-edited** — its "single cause" reading was correct on its evidence and wrong on a fact nobody had (the prod message carries no diff). **Numbering — the 586 → 589 gap:** HO 587 was the doc sweep for 586, **HO 588 is the parked absence-watch STEP-0 probe** (`7ce1f0e`, read-only diag) whose roadmap block is owed when that arc ships, and **HO 591 is the sweep for 588–590** (this block + the backlog retraction + oddities + SKILL). Docs: HO 591. **Also notes now run through HO 590.**

**Also (HO 589-595), the pageErr/500 arc — one hypothesis family falsified, one fault split in two, one 500 named and closed, and TWO of this arc's own conclusions retracted on measurement; no theme-% change (correctness + reliability, no user-facing surface change).** **HO 589 (STEP 0, `259babd`) corrected two documented premises that had aimed every downstream hypothesis:** `args[]=HTML` is the **structural / markup** variant, not "text-content" (`react-dom-client.development.js:5240` fills slot 1 with `fromText ? "text" : "HTML"`), and the component-naming diff in slot 2 is **DEV-only**, so the minified prod message is **byte-identical for every structural mismatch in the app** and **message identity carries zero cause-count information**. The HO 574 instrument's 200-char cap is therefore already at its ceiling — raising it buys nothing, because the prod crawl can never self-identify a component. **HO 590 fixed two DISTINCT latent bugs, and NEITHER is proven to be a prod cause:** the markets tape (`MarketsTapeClient`'s `useState(() => Date.now())` gating the arrow node, structural `args[]=HTML`, `626c251`) and `FilingRow.relAge()`'s "Nd ago" drift (`args[]=text`, a **separate** defect, `601f54a`), both fixed by prop-drilling a server `nowMs`. **The load-bearing finding is C5:** prod `/` is served **fresh per request** (`X-Vercel-Cache: MISS`, `Age: 0`, `no-store`), so the render-to-hydration gap is ~1.3s and **cannot straddle** a 26h/market boundary — the harness's `control(+0)` never fires. Both fixes are recorded as **correctness hardening, not closures**. **HO 591** was the sweep plus four fixups, including **retiring the "cause #1 / cause #2" partition** once C5 destroyed its premise (the split existed only because `/` carried the tape and the inner pages didn't). **HO 592 falsified H5** — data-dependent invalid HTML nesting, the strongest remaining *clock-independent* structural mechanism — **in one pass: 0 reparented / dropped / synthesised nodes across all 37 `ROUTES`**, with the detector self-validated before any result was trusted (6 positive fixtures incl. div-in-p, a-in-a, tr-without-tbody, multi-child SVG `<title>`; 2 negative, one pinning `<div>` inside `<a>` as **valid** HTML5) and scanner coverage checked (1460 authored = 1460 parsed on `/`, every element matched by source offset, so the clean result is a real read and not a short one). The **HO 473 SVG `<title>` class is clean at HEAD** both statically (4 sites, all single-child) and at runtime (0 multi-child titles in rendered prod HTML). **H4** (static-prerender + `useSearchParams` skew) is likewise recorded raised-and-falsified: every prod-firing route builds `ƒ Dynamic`, and the five routes 589 read as `○ Static` are 3xx redirect stubs. **HO 593 answered the question that reframed the arc: TWO FAULTS.** The `#418` fires and the intermittent 500 are separate, and the discriminator is the pageErr **message**, which the crawl already logged — no instrument change needed. Over 54 runs / 4,230 route-hit cells: **87 `#418` vs 12 "Server Components render"**, the 12 landing only on `committees-redirect` / `members-pass-rate` / `member-detail` / `stale`, while every `/`-family route **and `/president`** carries `#418` and has never 500'd; the 500 is a whole-route `__next_error__` shell, **not** a segment failing after a flushed shell, so it cannot be producing a structurally different tree. **Three record corrections carried precisely:** (1) run `30595816107` is **RECLASSIFIED** from HO 589's "transient Server-Components 500" to a **site-wide outage** — 216/216 cells non-200, which swamped every aggregate until it was detected and excluded; this is a **new statement here, not a retro-edit** of the 589 record, which was correct on its evidence. (2) The M2 stage table is recorded **WITHOUT a ranking**: the counts are **2/0/0/1/1/1** and will not carry one, because retry masking makes them too small and noisy to order. (3) Route-disjointness is phrased as **"disjoint in the crawl sample"** — M3's digest lists `/stale`, `/` **and** `/members` over 24h, so **`/` is not immune, it was unsampled.** **HO 594 shipped the covering index (`eab2fd2` migration, `8159554` rewire) AND retracted its own STEP 0 recommendation.** Steady state went **20-24s to 140ms-1.5s**, but co-located the cold first touch was still **8.2-14.1s, worst 20.003s to a TimeoutError — margin -4.1s**. The reusable lesson is the *shape* of the error: **a recommendation made on a laptop MEDIAN (208ms) was reversed by a co-located WORST CASE.** Two controls pinned the residual rather than assuming it (`?order=` moved the spike to whichever query touched the table first; `?warm=1` showed a trivial `SELECT 1` does not absorb it), giving the page-residency-under-60s finding that made indexing structurally insufficient. **HO 595 materialized it.** `member_participation` (~554 rows) is rebuilt on the only writer of `member_votes`, and **`member_votes` is now absent from the request path** — the seven remaining references are the bounded ones. Verified **query-level and co-located**, per 594's own finding that route-level counts understate this class: **GREEN worst 6.46s (margin +3.54s) vs RED 14.4s (margin -4.4s) measured in the same requests, 0 query breaches, 0 TimeoutErrors**, route-level 0/13 on cold keys disjoint from both prior runs, and the residual 2-6s proven **positional** (the first-query-in-a-cold-function tax — reordering moved it, and the same read runs 23-137ms when it follows). **Values unchanged:** fingerprints `1eedaa8d` / `21611d72` / `349972d`, row counts 536/549/536, across `/members` (both sorts), `/committee/[code]` and `/members/[bioguideId]`. The **non-destructive refresh path is PROVEN, not assumed** — all three guard branches (empty / shrink / throw) fired against the live 554-row table, which held 554 rows with `refreshed_at` unmoved each time, then a real refresh advanced the stamp with a stable row count. **595's own STEP 0 M2 was ALSO retracted:** it framed materializing as a 1h-to-24h freshness regression and defended it with a 3.02pp drift ceiling; `member_votes` has exactly one writer path (`lib/votes-sync.ts` + `lib/senate-votes-sync.ts`, both only via `/api/sync-votes`, cron `0 10 * * *`), so materializing on the writer is **freshness-NEUTRAL and marginally better** — the value publishes at sync time instead of up to an hour later, and the shipped `revalidate: 3600` was spending ~23 recomputes a day re-deriving values that could not have changed. The justification defended a cost that did not exist. **Verification of 595 on prod at `2297b98`:** `/members?sort=missed` and `?sort=passrate` both 200 rendering 7.5% / 22.5% / 1.9%, `/committee/hlig00` 200, `/members/A000055` 200; `member_participation` 554 rows, stamp `2026-08-04T01:54:03.907Z`. **What is NOT closed:** the `pageErr` #418 OPEN LOOP stands exactly as open and as unattributed as before — this arc removed one hypothesis family and one confound from it, and named nothing. **Numbering — the 586 to 589 gap:** HO 587 was the doc sweep for 586, **HO 588 is the parked absence-watch STEP-0 probe** (`7ce1f0e`) whose roadmap block is still owed when that arc ships, and **HO 596 is the sweep for 591-595** (this block + oddities x6 + SKILL + backlog); **589-590 were already swept at HO 591** and are re-narrated above only because the `30595816107` reclassification and both retractions revise them. Docs: HO 596. **Also notes now run through HO 595.**

**Also (HO 597), the `/lobbying` VOLUME sort — a materialization plus a cache-placement fix, and the arc found a WORSE defect on the way out; no theme-% change (a query-cost + correctness fix; the user-facing effect is that VOLUME returns rows again).** **STEP 0 (`baed5c4`) led with the finding that lowered its own urgency**, which is the part worth keeping: the HO 440/448 guard had fired twice in the 7-day retention window and **neither was a timeout** — both were `BLOCKED: SQL read operations are forbidden`, a Turso read-quota block during one ~93-minute incident that also took the rollup, top-firms and crosswalk. Timeout-shaped fires in real traffic: **0**. The 20s cold breach was real and reproducible on demand but production kept the query warm; that was reported plainly rather than dressed up as justification for work already scoped. **Two defects shipped.** (1) The one the WATCH named: VOLUME ordered by a request-time `COUNT(*) FROM lda_activities GROUP BY filing_uuid`, materialized to `lda_filings.activity_count` with `idx_lda_filings_activity(activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)` forced via `INDEXED BY` — subquery and JOIN gone, a pre-ordered single-table walk (`daf6dcc` migration · `387c56f` writer + resumable backfill · `f2f4915` rewire). HO 544 had tuned the JOIN *direction* here and was right to; the lesson that supersedes it is that **no join direction saves a request-time aggregate over a large table**. (2) Found while fixing (1) and the reason the degradation lasted: **the guard sat INSIDE the `unstable_cache` callback, so its degraded empty feed was the callback's return value and was cached for an hour** — one cold miss poisoned that key for 60 minutes and the successful retry never ran (`82eaa24`; the HO 552 class, oddities). **Falsified four-sided (`418e80c`).** RED co-located: the old shape breaches every VOLUME variant and **does not finish in 55 seconds** — worse than STEP 0 could see, since STEP 0 measured through the 10s+10s double abort and could only learn "20s". GREEN worst-of-cold-runs: volume **564ms (+9.44s)**, page-40 **1.48s (+8.52s)**, +linked **2.97s (+7.03s)**. CONTROL ran the old and new SQL against the **same live state** and diffed them (not a temporal before/after — the corpus grows daily): 1,000 visible rows identical position-for-position, clamp boundary identical in three forms, **0 of 429 zero-activity filings visible** (rank-1000 count = 11), `SUM(activity_count)` == `COUNT(lda_activities)` exactly. The degradation path was **fired**, as a matched RED/GREEN pair: RED served the stored empty in <600ms without touching the DB, GREEN re-queried and recovered. **Two residuals, both filed rather than quietly widened into.** `?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` improved from ">55s, always" to **worst 15.62s, breached 2 of 5** — a large improvement that is **not a clear**, and rounding it to one would be the over-claim HO 594 had to retract; its mechanism is per-row `EXISTS` probing cold pages (the HO 594 residency class), its fix a second materialization beyond this HO's priced scope. And the one that matters more: **`/lobbying?q=` is breaching on prod and rendering a wrong answer** — `?q=boeing` shows "No filings match" with a 200 while 78 filings match — **not caused by this arc** (the search SQL is byte-untouched) and **found only because the new instrument names the failing cache key**. HO 547 priced that path honestly at **≤67ms** for the pager COUNT; at 129,401 filings it is **92.8s**. A cost measurement is a property of the corpus, not the query (oddities). **The instrument is proven in production**, which is what the handoff demanded of any fix here: the guard's log line now carries `sort/linked/q/page`, and it is that line — not the crawl, which is structurally blind to a 200-with-empty-body — that turned an invisible failure into a one-line diagnosis. Also notes now run through HO 597.

**Also (HO 597, record correction to the HO 594 and HO 597 blocks above — appended, not retro-edited, per the append-only rule).** **Every `20.00Xs` figure in the HO 593-597 arc is the instrument's ceiling, not a measurement.** `lib/db.ts` aborts a request at 10s and retries once, so a query slower than 10s reports **~20.00s regardless of whether it takes 21 seconds or five minutes** — the readings are RIGHT-CENSORED at the bound. So the HO 594 block's *"worst 20.003s to a TimeoutError"* should be read as **>20s**; its sub-ceiling readings (8.2-14.1s steady, 140ms-1.5s) are real durations and stand, and the **-4.1s margin stands as a lower bound** on how negative it gets. Same for HO 593's 20.005s and HO 597 STEP 0's three starred rows. **HO 597 is what exposed it:** re-running the same RED query behind a **55s** ceiling instead of a 10s one showed it does not finish in 55 seconds either — so a figure the record carried as "20s" was in truth **>55s**, a 2.75x understatement of the defect that was being argued about. The general form, and the reason this is worth a block of its own: **a timeout is a censoring instrument, and a censored reading is a lower bound, not a value.** When a measurement lands on the bound, re-run it behind a deliberately looser ceiling before quoting it — otherwise the number you defend a design decision with is just the number your timeout was set to. **No pointer advance — this block CORRECTS the HO 594 and HO 597 blocks above and closes no arc; the pointer stands at HO 597 from the block above.**

**Also (HO 598), the `/lobbying?q=` routed search — the wrong answer removed, the loop NARROWED not closed, and the arc reversed itself three times on the way; no theme-% change (correctness + reliability — the only user-facing change is that search returns right answers and fails honestly).** **The reframe that reset the arc, and the most reusable thing in it: the LIKE was never the cost — the walk is.** A `COUNT(*) FROM lda_filings WHERE registrant_name IS NOT NULL` — a name-column read with **no predicate to evaluate and nothing to match** — measured **86.3s co-located**, against the zero-match search's 91.3s. So cost tracks **bytes dragged off the table**, not the predicate and not the match count; every candidate after that was priced in bytes, and the ones that looked clever on the predicate were priced out. **FTS was re-verified and REJECTED AGAIN, on a NEW reason.** HO 546's premises still hold (name columns 25.1 / 27.6 chars; **5,326 registrants + 22,422 clients = 27,748 distinct names across 129,401 filings**); only its *number* expired (~700ms → 91.3s). But the deciding argument is neither of those: FTS5 cannot do infix, so `%oeing%` stops matching "Boeing" — and, decisively, **FTS fixes the sparse half while still needing routing for the dense half, exactly as the name table does.** Both solve the same half; the name table then wins on every remaining axis (already built and maintained, keeps substring semantics, no new index type, no trigger on the `FLUSH_AT=100`-tuned write path). **My earlier framing — "FTS solves a half that isn't broken" — is recorded here as WRONG: sparse IS the broken half.** **The `~700ms → 91.3s` delta is UNATTRIBUTED, and explicitly not a platform regression.** `oddities.md:235` records **no measurement conditions** for the 700ms — no cold/warm, no co-located/laptop, no spread — while the entry immediately beside it, same arc and same surface, records *"a TEMP B-TREE at 7.8s cold"* and even reasons that search is usually served cold. The arc distinguished cold for one number and not for the other, so the gap sits unexplained between corpus growth (112k → 129k, only **1.15×**), measurement conditions, and platform behaviour, and nothing on the record can apportion it. **Two gate failures, and the rewire was withheld both times** (round 1 FAIL, round 2 FAIL after sparse was re-measured at n=8 and routing was priced) before a third round cleared every regime except sparse. **Two retractions, each recorded as a retraction rather than quietly dropped:** the STEP 0 assertion that *"the corpus is NOT the cause"* (nothing measured supported it — the 86.3s bare-column read is what replaced it), and the **12.6s routing price** (leg 2 had been charged for the name scan leg 1 had already done; with ids passed through, leg 2 is **0–1.5s** — the correction is what made the routed path viable at all). **Shipped:** the bounded pager `COUNT` (`1afdd02`, 58.1s → **2.25s** on common terms, rendered `520+`, because any total past the clamp was already being discarded by the code that asked for it) · the honest timeout render (`79d1276` — *"Search timed out — try a narrower term"*, distinct from the genuine *"No filings match"*, count `—` not `0`; the guard always knew the difference and never said so) · `lda_names` + its two id indexes + the sync writer (`4fd9b48`, `e7fdf30`, `85a173f`) · the routed rewire (`e4f09ba` — resolve the term against `lda_names`, **empty → return without touching `lda_filings` at all**, sparse → seek by literal ids, dense → hand back to the `dt_posted` walk) · the completeness instrument (`a4e2c1c`) · the arg-binding fix (`06d03dd`). **The routing threshold `m* = √(k·N) ≈ 1,297` is DERIVED, not tuned** — a formula over `PAGE_SIZE` and the rollup blob's corpus count, so it re-derives as the corpus grows — and it was verified on a **straddling** pair (`insur` 0.86×, `hospital` 1.16×), not on comfortable extremes. **Final measurements: 24 runs, 0 breaches, median 3,291ms, worst 7,735ms.** **Prod-verified at `06d03dd` across all four regimes** — `boeing` 13 rows / count 78 · `insur` 13 / 520+ · `hospital` 13 / 520+ · a nonsense term **0 with "No filings match" in ~400ms** (was 91.3s, and it now never reaches the table). **It shipped over a failed gate, deliberately, with the three conditions stated** — strict dominance (the replaced sparse path breached on **both** `lib/db.ts` attempts at its *best* observed 19.8s; the candidate went 0-for-24), honest failure, and the loop left open — now written into SKILL as a fenced rule, together with the gate statistic it corrected: **gate on the upper tail (`worst − median`), never on the range**, because `max − min` penalises a fast outlier and a 380ms best case had failed sparse on its own good news. **HO 594's retraction was for CLOSING on a thin margin; this improves on one.** **The loop is NARROWED, not closed:** the residual is leg 1 — a ~33,620-row / ~1 MB covering scan ranging **0.3–6.6s** — which is ~1/23 the bytes of the walk this arc started from, fell roughly linearly, and **carries the same 2-15× run-to-run spread at every size**; that is a variance question, not a query-design one, and it is handed by name to the new variance OPEN LOOP. **And a bug shipped and was caught in prod within minutes — by an instrument this arc had just added.** `e4f09ba` left the bounded COUNT binding **2 args against a 17-arg routed predicate**; the guard **degraded to an empty feed instead of 500ing**, and HO 597's failing-key log line named it, fixed at `06d03dd`. What let it through is its own lesson (oddities): the page query and the COUNT run in **parallel inside one guard**, and the equivalence harness executed only the page query — **verifying one branch of a `Promise.all` is not coverage of the pair.** Both safety nets that caught it — the honest-timeout render and the keyed log line — were built in the preceding four HOs, which is the one part of this arc that worked exactly as intended. Docs: HO 599. **Also notes now run through HO 598.**

**Also (HO 600-601), the SC August special primary — a phantom contest deleted, a parser gate that inverted against itself replaced by a router, and TWO of the driving handoffs' own premises retired on measurement; no theme-% change (a correctness fix plus an ingestion path on the existing electoral pillar — Races holds at 99).** **The external facts first, because the repo cannot rederive them.** South Carolina's August 11 contest is a special **primary** under **S.C. Code §7-11-55**, held after the death of the Republican nominee for U.S. Senate, and open to **Republican candidates only** because that nominee had general-election opposition; voters who cast a June *Democratic* primary ballot are barred from it, and the winner goes onto the November 3 general ballot in the deceased nominee's place. Filing closed **2026-07-28**. Verified **2026-08-05** against the SC Election Commission notice (2026-07-13) plus concurring county-board notices and the LWV state guide, and independently corroborated by Ballotpedia, which links an August 11 **Republican** primary page and no Democratic counterpart. It is **not** a special *election*, which is why SC is absent from Ballotpedia's two-item 2026 Senate specials list (FL, OH) — and that distinction is the whole arc. **HO 600 (`36b7731`, STEP 0 `43b7ef6`) deleted a live phantom.** The seeded `senate-SC-2026-special-D` asserted a contest that does not exist by statute. It was **not dormant** — `election_round='primary'` put it into `getPrimaryCalendar` -> `PrimaryTimeline` and into `getDashboardPrimaries`, where it **inflated the Aug 11 tick from 47 to 48 contests**. Its visible footprint was a **count inflation, not a rendered label** (nothing anywhere printed the words "Democratic special primary"), which is the honest severity and is recorded here because the handoff assumed worse. Both halves shipped in one commit — `seed-special-primaries.ts` is a pure `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT` with **no delete pass**, so removing the seed entry alone leaves the row and deleting the row alone gets undone by the next seed run; proven by a full re-seed that upserted only the R row. The delete was one literal id, print-before-delete, dry-run by default, with the zero-candidate authorization gate enforced **at runtime** rather than trusted to memory. **Two HO 600 premises were retired by its own measurements.** `/race/S-SC-2026` was **never in scope** — the race hub reads `getRunoffsForRace`, which filters `election_round='runoff'` and returns **0 rows** for it. And a surface the handoff did not name **is** in scope: `getDashboardPrimaries` runs unconditionally in `CompetitiveRacesBlock` regardless of variant, so the PRIMARIES tab renders on **both** `/` and `/dashboard-classic`. **The defect HO 601 fixed, stated as its class: a container's self-reported type cannot gate its contents.** `parseCandidatesPage` kept a votebox only when the box's specialness **agreed with the page's** (`/special/i.test(h5) !== onSpecialPage -> drop`), where `onSpecialPage` is `isSpecialElectionPage()` — which reads the **`<title>`, and the `<title>` is not the URL**. Ballotpedia serves the FL/OH special-election **article** under the plain `United_States_Senate_election_in_{State},_2026` URL (HTTP 200, `redirected=false`), so those pages self-report as special and their "Special ..." boxes agreed and were kept. SC's page is a genuine regular article that **also** hosts an additional Aug 11 special primary: page-special false, box-special true, so the only box carrying a **published 10-candidate field** was silently dropped and the parser returned `no_candidates`. **Fixing the URL alone would not have worked** — that is the finding that reframed the arc. **HO 601 §1's own rule was WRONG, and its §2 gates caught it before any code shipped.** The handoff specified routing on the `<h5>` alone: any special-marked box -> `senate-{ST}-2026-special-{party}`. Measured across all 35 senate states, that produced **4 unseeded targets** and moved **FL, OH and SC**; FL's and OH's 2026 races **are** special elections, so every primary box on their pages reads "Special ..." while the base ids are their correct and only home — routing on the `<h5>` would have moved all four rosters onto unseeded ids, which the HO 561 gate refuses, leaving the base rows to take an empty roster, HO 564 to correctly refuse the delete, and **both states to freeze silently at stale values**. The shipped rule instead resolves against the registry: **a special-classified box routes to `senate-{ST}-2026-special-{party}` IFF that id is seeded, otherwise to the base id** — because the `<h5>` says a box is a special *contest* but not whether that contest is *ADDITIONAL to a regular one for the same seat*, which is the fact that decides the destination, and the seed registry is the curated encoding of exactly that. Seeded router: **0 unseeded targets, SC the only state changed, FL/OH byte-identical.** **A consequence recorded where it will be read: SEEDING IS NOT ADDITIVE.** Adding a `-special-` seed row now MOVES that state's boxes off the base ids — seed FL and its rosters migrate, the base rows freeze, and nothing errors. That is the same failure the router rejection avoided, **re-entered through a config change instead of a code change**, so the warning lives at the router, in a new `data/special-primary-seeds/README.md`, and in oddities. **Shipped (HO 601):** `5bf1176` pre-flight · `efa858f` code · `0473dc6` docs · `07dd555` the pre-flight baseline relabel · `c4a3c06` the freeze-status residual. The code also carries a **specialness-aware dedup key** (Mark Lynch appears in SC's June R roster at 28.9% **and** in the August field, so a `contest|name` key silently dropped a real second candidacy); the **C2 regular-page fallback** in `scrapeSenateSpecialCandidates`, kept for a reason §0 did not give — the standard pass is **cursor-driven** and HO 600 M2 measured three consecutive ticks stuck on `unit="house"` at cursor **439-462 of 471**, so the senate slice might not reach SC before Aug 11, and a fixed election date cannot be served by a cursor position; and, because that fallback parses a document full of regular voteboxes, the special pass now filters on `isSpecial` so it **writes only its invoked special ids** and cannot become a second writer of base rows on a different schedule. **C3 fixed an instrument that could not read FAIL.** `runSpecialPriorityPass` returned `string[]`, so `scrapeSenateSpecialState`'s `fetchFailure` was computed and then discarded: HO 600 M2 read **`fetchFailures: []` across three ticks that each 404'd**, while the backlog WATCH named that field as its evidence. It now returns `{attemptedIds, populatedIds, fetchFailures, settledSkipped, emptyRosterSkipped, sources}`, forwarded verbatim by the pass-through route. `emptyRosterSkipped` was added **pre-emptively during the fix**, because "attempted, no failure, nothing populated" would otherwise have read exactly like success — the same inversion, caught before it shipped. **Falsified in three legs against prod, twice (the re-run also proving idempotency, 10 -> 10 identical).** Leg 1: **10 rows** on `senate-SC-2026-special-R`, exact name match to the published field, **0 missing / 0 extra**, with `sources[].url` confirming the fallback fired and `candidates: 19` against 10 written confirming only special boxes were taken. Leg 3: FL/OH content identical with **both** branches exercised (FL future-dated so the write path ran and reproduced itself; OH past-dated so `settledSkipped` fired) and **0** `-special-` rows created for either. **Leg 2 is the one to read carefully, and the ship report initially overstated it.** It recorded `isSettled` **consulted and REFUSED** on `senate-SC-2026-R`, byte-identical, `updated_at` untouched. But `isSettled` short-circuits **before the roster is built**, so it refuses every settled row unconditionally and cannot discriminate a substituting roster from any other — and the payload it refused was the **correct June roster**, not a substituting one. So the HO 560 freeze is **exercised on its general case** and is now **STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE for substitution**: the router removes that hazard upstream, so no substituting roster can ever arrive at a settled row for the freeze to observe. HO 600 reported the freeze untested; **the honest successor state is narrower than "exercised," not broader.** The router is the first line, the freeze the second, and it stays live and useful for its general case — leg 3's OH `settledSkipped` is that case firing. The generalizable shape is now in oddities: **a fix upstream of a guard can make it unreachable, and the fix's own success report reads as the guard being exercised** — distinct from HO 549/553, where the guard never fired at all; here it fired on a different input than the one it exists for, which no "did it fire?" check can distinguish. **A third premise fell with the arc.** HO 601 §5 guarded `senateSpecialPageUrl` as "correct for FL/OH." **FL and OH never used it** — they are read from their regular URLs — and SC's special URL 404s, so combined with HO 561 S2's 0-of-35 missing standard pages, **nothing currently reaches Ballotpedia through that URL shape at all.** Filed as a loop with an explicit *do not delete on this evidence*: the measurement shows it is currently unreached, not that the case it defends against cannot occur. **Scope held:** `senateSpecialPageUrl` unmodified, no `-special-` row auto-created, `senate-SC-2026-special-D` absent throughout, no Aug 25 runoff row (filed, dated, conditional). Docs: HO 602.

**Also (HO 602), the platform read and read-budget attribution — four HOs of reasoning replaced by one measurement, and a logged burn figure corrected; docs-only, no theme-% change.** **Provenance, because this is the only record.** Every number here came off the **Turso dashboard for org `csu-j3`, read 2026-08-05**. There is **no script and it is not reproducible from the repo** — the HO 584 statutory-facts class, so the numbers, their source and their date are written together. **The platform facts the variance loop was blocked on.** Plan **Developer** ($5.99/mo, **2.5B rows read/mo**, +$1/B overage). DB primary **`aws-us-west-2`** (`libsql://cbt-csu-j3.aws-us-west-2.turso.io`), single group `default`, **no replicas, no branches**. Vercel functions are pinned **`pdx1`** (`vercel.json:2`), which is us-west-2 — so **store and compute are in the same AWS region**. **Two variance hypotheses die on that**: cross-region latency and replica routing are both out. What remains is that Turso's own Developer-plan launch post describes the tier as running on a **massive multi-tenant server**, which is the shape that leaves a median alone while fanning an upper tail. **There is no purchasable lever** — the plan comparison table carries quota, retention and governance rows and **no performance row at any tier**, so a Scaler upgrade is an unfalsifiable experiment and was **rejected rather than run**. The loop therefore closes by **BOUNDING** (a co-located worst case with the margin as a number), never by explaining, which was always the second half of its criterion. **Read-budget attribution, answered by measurement after four HOs of reasoning about it.** Per-query `rows_read` over 30 days: the `bills_agg` sponsor aggregate **110.0M / 317 calls / 347k per call (26%)** · `getParticipationStrip` **91.3M / 106 / 861k (22%)** · `getChamberParticipationContext` **85.4M / 96 / 890k (20%)** · the pre-582 markets spark window **40.7M / 8,660 (10%)** · the pre-582 markets `MAX`-`GROUP BY` join **35.5M / 8,470 (8%)**. **Top five = 363M of 418M, 87%.** The whole LDA family is **~11M, under 3%**. **None of the four standing suspects was the answer** — the loop carried the e2e crawl, summarize, the LDA precompute and real traffic; it was three aggregates. **The `/members` 500 loop and the read-budget loop were ONE fault, and nothing said so.** HO 593 named the two participation queries as the 500's cause, HO 594 indexed them, and **HO 595 materialized them into `member_participation` and removed `member_votes` from the request path — closing 42% of the read budget as a side effect**. HO 582 had taken the markets **18%** two days earlier. HO 595's tombstone says *"Not claimed: this does not touch the `pageErr` #418 OPEN LOOP"* and is **silent on the loop it did close**. Recorded here as a **new statement, not a retro-edit** — 595's block was correct on its evidence. **Append-only correction to the HO 577 burn figure.** The record says CBT "stepped ~10x around Jul 25 and plateaued near **85-90M/day**." The daily series shows a baseline of **3-5M/day** through ~Jul 22, a ramp from **Jul 23-24** steepening **Jul 27-30**, and a peak near **38M**. **The timing survives; the magnitude is roughly 2.4x overstated**, and three HOs reasoned against the higher number. **The ramp has a named cause, and it is not the one first proposed here.** A 24-hour Top Queries read showed LDA on top and was mis-read as the ramp — **a 24-hour attribution window shows what is LEFT after the fixes, not what caused the rise**. The 30-day window names the participation aggregates, which shipped **into** the ramp: `getChamberParticipationContext` HO 523 (`2b8d62b`, Jul 25), `getParticipationStrip` HO 527 (`7fa729c`, Jul 26), `participationAggCte` into the roster HO 535 (`b565c13`, Jul 27). Dates verified against `git log`. **The HO 597 prediction is OWED AND UNSCORABLE, not failed.** HO 594/595/597/598 all landed Aug 3-5 — the same 48 hours as the `activity_count` backfill (129,000 UPDATEs) and the `lda_names` back-catalogue sweep — so the fixes and their one-time cost are **confounded**, and the day-matched quiet day the prediction requires does not exist yet. Direction is not in doubt: **~60% of the burn was removed across Aug 1-4** against a logged estimate of *"roughly a fifth, a level shift not a resolution."* **Re-read Aug 8-9 on a quiet day** and score it against the stated constraints rather than letting it age out. **Cap risk is low**: 418M against 2.5B over 30 days; even sustained at the 38M peak that is ~1.1B/month. **The lesson that outlives the numbers** (oddities): **rows read and milliseconds are different currencies**, and this is the strongest instance yet — a covering-index scan documented in-code as *"index-only, 96ms"* (`lib/queries.ts:6015`) is the **single largest read-budget consumer on the database**, invisible to every latency instrument, visible only to per-query `rows_read`. Docs: HO 602. **Also notes now run through HO 602.**

**Also (HO 603–604), the layout arc's first phase — three orphaned arcs given a home, then the viewport type scale shipped in three commits that can each be reverted alone; no theme-% change (a global type-plumbing change plus doc custody — no surface gained or lost a feature).** **HO 603 was custody work and has no block above this one, which is why the range starts at 603.** Part A filed three arcs that existed only in conversation into `docs/backlog.md` — Absence Watch (its R2/R3 fork recorded UNRESOLVED, and its cost half explicitly EXPIRED because HO 594/595 rewrote the participation path it was priced against), the HO 590 dashboard declutter (whose mock **does not exist on disk**, so the backlog entry *is* the artifact), and the C1–C8 layout audit. Plus the display-orphan sweep including the dead variant fork (`1adf056`) and the `isSettled` router comment (`3bc619e`). **The lesson that entry carries: a backlog entry that points at a file is only as tracked as the file** (oddities). **HO 604 then shipped C2, the type scale, as C0/C1/C2 + a rider.** **C0 (`c943e07`)** landed `docs/design/dashboard-layout-target.html` (recovered verbatim from `~/Downloads`, SHA-256 checked) and `layout-conventions-arc.md`, the plan of record — **but its second artifact, `layout-conventions-c1-c8.html`, did not land** — absent from disk at sweep time; a copy survives in the design chat and lands as its own `docs(design)` commit if relayed. The second mock in this arc to go missing this way. The arc doc's §0 pointer to it is knowingly dangling. **C1 (`4185325`) is the load-bearing idea: substitute at IDENTITY first.** Six `:root` tokens `--fs-9..--fs-14` declared equal to the literals they replace, so the 714-site substitution — 353 in `globals.css`, 17 in `landing.module.css`, 344 across 82 TSX files — renders byte-identical and the ladder becomes a one-file change that reverts alone. Eleven regexes, not 714 edits, because **714 of ~827 type sites are six values**. Two traps were caught only because the numbers were taken twice: `landing.module.css` writes `font-size:11px` with **no space**, so a regex tuned to the `globals.css` style would have skipped all 17 of its sites and reported clean (the HO 549/553 shape — a pattern that structurally cannot match reads identical to nothing to match); and a **coverage grep must be run BEFORE as well as after** — it read **714 → 0**, and a grep pointed at the wrong path returns 0 both times and looks like a clean sweep. **C2 (`fd069b9`)** is a flat **+1px per stop at 1600/2000/2400**, chosen over the multiplicative alternative because it keeps every token an integer at every stop while still walking the mock's 12→13→14→15. **Coverage, and why the C1 gate could not have produced it: 0 missed sites, calibrated against the pre-C1 build where the SAME instrument reports 496 missed / 0 scaled — the same 496 signatures.** A computed-size diff across an identity change **cannot detect a missed site** (a site left as literal `11px` renders 11px, exactly what `--fs-11` renders), so it is a *breakage* gate that reads zero on a no-op; only the 1440→2560 delta reads differently depending on whether the work happened. At 1440 the rendered output is byte-identical to pre-arc across seven routes. **The rider (`5b9c34c`) repo-ignores `docs/handoffs/`** — Tailwind v4 honours a repo `.gitignore` but **not** `core.excludesFile`, so local builds were compiling class names out of handoff *prose* into five phantom rules that the deploy never had; the defect is **build-input parity**, not shipped dead CSS. **Prod-verified at `5b9c34c`**: three ladder stops live, `--fs-11` 11px→14px between 1440 and 2560, no horizontal overflow, three identical passes. **Deferred and named, not dropped:** C2's other half (row padding + fixed-size glyph strips stepping at the same breakpoints) — the ~68 CSS one-offs and 34 SVG `fontSize` attributes don't scale, so the hierarchy compresses at wide widths; that is recorded in the ladder comment as bounded and real, not a bug. Docs: HO 605. **Also notes now run through HO 604.**

**Also (HO 605–606), the layout arc's doc sweep and then the audit — an instrument that refused its own first crawl; docs plus one read-only diagnostic, no theme-% change (nothing user-facing moved).** **HO 605 (`de08df2` + `bbe062b`, via review ref)** swept 603–604: the roadmap block above, the `backlog.md:130` rewrite (both its claims had gone false — `NOTHING WAS BUILT`, and the `layout-audit-591.ts` filename), four oddities, the arc-md renumber, and a ride-along correction to the HO 603 oddity whose own 784 figure was short for precisely the reason that entry is about. It also split the deferred **padding half of C2** into its own backlog entry — remediation, not measurement — and caught a live self-contradiction: SKILL's Layout grid paragraph prescribed `max-w-[1200px]` on a sparse column at 2400px+, which is **exactly what C8 rejects**, so landing C1–C8 beside it would have put SKILL in contradiction with itself; it now defers to C8 and records the supersession. **HO 606 built the audit (`e794194`) and then fixed it (`94f6543`), because the falsification leg REFUSED THE FIRST CRAWL — the single most useful thing this arc has done.** Pointed at `/` before any zero was trusted, M1 reported a **10px** interior gap on the breaking-headline row whose headline visibly ends ~1,100px from its timestamp, and M2 read **zero**. **The cause, made twice: layout boxes were measured instead of ink.** A `flex: 1` child stretches its box across exactly the whitespace C1 is about, so the box-edge gap is ~0 while the visible gap is enormous; M2 had the same defect one level down, where a stretched last child hides its panel's slack. Fix: measure to the **deepest ink** — the painted box when an element has one (a bordered chip *is* its box), else its text client rects. Same row, same threshold: **10px → 1,092px**; M2 on `/` **0 → 3**. **Thresholds were never touched — only the measurement definition**, which is the distinction between correcting an instrument and tuning one. A third bug hid in the diagnostic written to *explain* an M2 zero: it evaluated on a **fresh blank page** and printed `[]`, indistinguishable from "this route has no such element" — the fourth same-as-success instance this arc has produced, and the one that landed in the place whose entire job is explaining a zero. **The numbers, with the correction the run itself forced: 27 distinct pages, not 30.** `/races` and `/primaries` 308 to `/electoral` and `/members/pass-rate` 307s to `/members?sort=passrate` — redirect aliases §3 missed when it excluded `/committees` and `/dashboard-v2` for the same reason — so site-wide M1 is **1,329 deduped**, not the 1,902 the raw table shows. Over **6,082 candidate rows**: M1a 362 raw · M1b 2,299 raw; **M2 29**; **M4 1,170 elements holding 102,450px of reserved vertical space**. Raw counts inflate because one visual gap is counted once per ancestor wrapper (2,661 → 1,902 on an innermost-of-chain dedup). **The gap distribution is bimodal with a gulf at 60–120px** — 1% of 72,913 measured gaps — so the 120px threshold sits in empty space and is **defensible rather than assumed**, which is the thing a threshold usually cannot say for itself. **M4's seed vocabulary caught 14 of ~1,170 hits**; the candidate-emitter found the rest, which is the vocabulary closing on measurement exactly as designed rather than on the `NO MEETINGS`/`—`/`NO DATA`/`NONE` guess. **The table's top three are three different problems, which is why they are worth taking together:** `/members` (565 M1b singleton wide rows, and the worst narrow-width surface by an order of magnitude — C1/C8, wide rows that should be panes), `/changes` (M3 **14.34** against a 2–5 norm, plus 42,255px reserved — C3/C4, not C1 at all), and `/lobbying` (78 of 88 are **M1a** repeated-row anchors — the canonical C1 defect and the cleanest test of whether the fix generalises). **Burn on the record: 80 page loads, 131.4s, 0 retries, 30/30 HTTP 200, zero routes unmeasurable.** Output was pasted and never committed; full per-route detail sits in the repo-ignored `docs/handoffs/606-artifacts/`. Docs: HO 607. **Also notes now run through HO 606.**

**Also (HO 608–610), P3 of the layout arc — the dashboard rebuilt against the committed mock in three slices, then the instrument that scored it rebuilt too; no theme-% change (every surface kept its features; only their arrangement moved).** **The slice shape, and the numbers each slice actually moved on `/` at 2560, all under instrument v1 so they compare (the 49 is the 606 crawl artifact's own figure; the 31 and 22 are from the rebuilt same-day crawl chain, since this HO's re-run had destroyed that artifact — in-session prints at 608 and 609 read 50 and 32, and the ±1–2 is live-data churn, not transcription: `f031673` itself measured 32 and then 31 forty minutes apart in one session):** 606 baseline **M1 49 · M2 3 · M3 8.41 · M4 11 elements / 1,190px** → **HO 608** (`2a1bd90` audit touch-up · `1d9c339` dashboard-classic removal · `5fa764b` the two-region shell) + **HO 609** (`938f0ca` compact two-line rows · `f031673` internal feed scroll + viewport-anchored popover) together took it to **M1 31 · M2 3 · M3 6.06 · M4 3 / 758px** → **HO 610** (`72d63a0` hearings interior · `730b792` packing pass · `2ca2c96` panel collapse · `a620e20` cleanup) closed at **M1 22 · M2 0 · M3 5.18 · M4 1 / 42px**. **The site-wide delta is exactly the two dashboard routes: 24 of 26 routes byte-identical across the full pre/post crawl** — M1 2,152→2,131, M2 23→17, M4 93,568→92,136px, every unit of which is `/` and `/?stage=committee`. That flatness was the control and it held perfectly. **Two of 608's §6 expectations missed, and both diagnoses are worth more than the predictions were.** It predicted **M2 3 → 0–1** from `align-items:start`; M2 stayed **3** through both 608 and 609, because the three hits were never the grid — they were the hearings week grid's empty day columns, which no shell change could reach and which only died at 610. And it set **nav one row at ≥1200px**, which the 608 HALT diagnosed as the **8×54=432** arithmetic: the nav had just moved *into* `.dash-left`, and the feed column takes ~432px, so the target was computed against a container the element no longer lives in (retracted in backlog with that shape). **HO 610's own three slices.** **The hearings interior (C4)** replaced the five-column week grid with the mock's `.hbar` / `.rib` / `.sched-wrap`: a day bar that falls through to the next day with meetings rather than ever showing an empty one, a five-cell ribbon where an empty day costs **one dash** instead of a 358px "No meetings" box, and the shown day's schedule. That single change is where **M2 3→0** and **M4 758→42px** came from, the 42px residue being the HEARINGS tab label. **It also reversed HO 282's height pin** — the box was pinned to the RACES footprint so a tab switch never resized it, but the day schedule is about a third of that height, so pinning would hold ~240px of dead space open under the DEFAULT tab, which is what C7 and C4 forbid; the cost, stated because it undoes a deliberate decision, is that a HEARINGS↔RACES click now shifts the week strip below it. `RacesBoxTabs.tsx` was untouched (CSS only), and the tab's TYPE/CHAMBER filter bar went with the grid — a glance surface, with the filters one click away at `/hearings` via `→ ALL`. `/hearings` itself is unchanged and still shares the per-meeting detail card, extracted to `HearingDetailCard.tsx` in the same commit so the two cannot drift. **The packing pass (C1)** put breaking rows, the masthead and the week strip on the mock's packed-left flex, and **deviated from the mock on the masthead**: the mock's `.mast` pins SIGN IN right with a `.sp{flex:1}` spacer, which IS the C1 pattern (SIGN IN sat ~700px right of LAST SYNC at 2560), and **SKILL owns the conventions, so the mock loses that one**. It also fixed `.race-grid`'s pre-existing 1200–1399 overflow — **not with another breakpoint, because a breakpoint cannot express it**: the feed column appears at 1400 and takes 400px, so the columns the container holds run **2 → 3 → 2 → 4** across viewports 1200/1300/1440/2560, non-monotonic in viewport and monotonic only in CONTAINER width, which is what `auto-fit` reads. Swept 700→2560: scrollWidth ≤ viewport everywhere (was +120px at 1200, +20px at 1300, and a 327px grid-over-container spill at 1440 that no document scroll revealed). **The panel collapse** gave `BillExpandPanel` a container query on its own width: inside `/`'s 400px column the panel is 372px and the 1.35fr/1fr body resolved to **99px/273px — 71px of prose, 1.4 words per line over 48 lines** — and the fr ratio was not what decided it (the metabox's ~273px min-content is a floor the 1fr track cannot go under, so the whole squeeze lands on the summary). Container, not viewport: `/bills` renders the same component at 1108–2228px and never crosses the threshold, so no route branch exists. After: **6.9 words/line over 10 lines**. **A live CSS bug surfaced on the way in and had been shipping since HO 570.** The comment above `.bxp` listed swept classes with glob stars, and the star-slash pair inside `-cmte-*` **closed the comment**; the parser then discarded the `.bxp` rule that follows, so `padding: 14px 16px 16px` was **dead in every built stylesheet for forty handoffs** — the panel measured 372px wide with a 372px body — and the only trace was one `Unexpected token Delim` warning in the build log that nobody read. It is also what silently dropped `container-type` on the first attempt at the collapse. **Two premise corrections the arc forced.** The feed rows are **`V2FeedList`, not `BillRowList compact`** — they have been since the HO 311 swap, and the handoff chain had been carrying the older name. And **HO 300's `overflow-visible` on `home-feed-panel` was a stale claim on main, in both halves**: the card it named is `SponsorHoverName`, which renders on `/bills`' `BillRow` and **has never rendered in that column**, and its "safe because the feed has no max-height" clause expired when HO 609 capped the column. Removed and re-reproduced at 1440 on a feed scrolled to its end with a row expanded — the title popover renders at the identical rect either way (it is `position:fixed` since 609) and the expanded panel's own `.v2f-sc-card` is bounded by `.v2f`, an INNER ancestor the rule never governed. **Then the instrument, because the fix broke the thing that scored it.** Removing `/`'s last stretched panel made the M2 falsification leg — whose anchor was "M2 must fire on `/`" — **refuse the crawl that would have scored the removal**: a successful remediation rendered indistinguishable from a dead detector (`2469404` re-probes fallback routes and names where it fired). **The 22 residual M1 rows then decomposed into 14 design / 5 instrument / 3 predicate**, and only the first was a real finding: 14 were stage-funnel rows, where the space between a short bar and its value **is the encoding**; 5 were packed rows that v1 scored anyway because it bucketed items on `round(centreY/4px)`, and a baseline-aligned 13px id and 17px headline fall in different buckets, so it measured id→age **with the headline sitting invisibly between them** — 873px of gap on a row with none; 3 were `.home-shell`/`.dash-page`/`.dash-left`, whose "gap" is the two-region gutter C8 asks for. **So instrument v2 (`e6bbc08`) changed four things and no threshold:** vertical-interval clustering replaces centre bucketing (under a six-token type ladder, mixed sizes on one baseline are the NORM, so v1 could not read any real row correctly); an M1b candidate with a child taller than 60px is a container, not a row; rows under `[data-viz-row]` leave M1 and are **counted** on their own M1x line, because an uncounted exemption grows silently; and the artifact is SHA-stamped and never overwritten — v1 wrote a fixed filename, and **this HO's own re-run destroyed the 606 baseline it was meant to be compared against**, which had to be rebuilt by checking out `f031673` and crawling again. **v2 was falsified with its anchors INVERTED**, because HO 610 packed the rows v1's anchor was built on: the known-GOOD rows must now read SMALL (masthead **12px**, breaking **24–50px**, against v1's 75px and 873px for the same rows — wrong in BOTH directions), the viz exemption must be VISIBLE (13 exempted, 0 funnel rows scored), and a known-BAD control must SURVIVE (`/members` holds **548**). The bound on the good rows is **derived, not tuned to pass**: the id column is a fixed 5.8em (93px at 2560), so a short id contributes basis-minus-text of alignment slack before the 10px gap — 93−53+10 = 50 for `S 5201`, 93−79+10 = 24 for `SJRES 181`. **Legs A and B both assert that v2 reports LESS than v1; alone they are satisfied by an instrument that reports nothing, and leg C is the only thing separating a correction from a silencing.** **Under v2, `/` reads M1 0 · M2 0 · M4 42px, with 13 viz-exempted** — a true zero net of a counted exemption, on the route the whole phase was about. `/?stage=committee` keeps **one** row: `.active-filter-strip` at 1,347px, conditional chrome that only renders when a filter is active and was therefore invisible to every unfiltered pass. **The v2 full crawl IS the P4 baseline, and it is a different instrument from every number above it** — site M1 **1,634** (M1a 327 · M1b 1,307), **M1x 26**, M2 17, M4 968 / 92,136px — ranking **`/members` 548 · `/changes` 471 · `/stale` 153 · `/bills` 78 · `/lobbying` 69 · `/news` 62 · `/trades` 55**. **P4's order is ruled and it revises 606's:** phase order holds, `changes`/`stale` stay P6 and lead it as a pair; within P4 **`hearings` FIRST** (the 610 hearings interior transplants nearly whole, and `/hearings` now carries **M2 9**, the largest on the site), then **`members`** (the C8 panes work), then `bills`/`news`/`trades`, with **`lobbying` last now that its generalisation-test role is spent**. **Narrow widths moved slightly the wrong way and are recorded rather than buried:** on `/`, @720 overflow 82→89 and extra-wrap +1 at both 900 and 720 — the ribbon and the now-longer masthead line wrap. P3 is a wide-viewport phase; the M5 debt is a backlog entry carrying those numbers. **Prod-verified twice** (`a620e20` for the four code slices, `e6bbc08` for v2), each `/api/version` to the served SHA plus a converged read. Docs: HO 610 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 610.** *(superseded by the HO 611-615 block below.)*

**Also (HO 611–615), P4 of the layout arc — the feed routes packed, then the phase closed by rebuilding the instrument that scored them and moving its anchor off the product entirely; no theme-% change (every surface kept its features; only their arrangement moved).**

**READ THE ERA LABEL ON EVERY NUMBER BELOW. v1 / v2 / v3 are three rulers and they do not compare.** The P4 baseline is v2 (`e6bbc08`); P4's own before/after is v2→v2 across two DOMs; the phase-close crawl is v3 (`f40768f`) and is reported twice — once against the same DOM under v2, so the instrument delta is isolable, and once as the P5/P6 baseline. **A route whose number fell because v3 stopped lying about it is an INSTRUMENT NOTE, not a remediation claim, and this block never launders one as the other.**

**P4's remediation, v2 throughout so the routes compare.** Site M1 **1,634 → 312** (M1a 327→108 · M1b 1,307→204), M2 **17 → 8**, M4 **92,136 → 65,278px**. Per route: **`/members` 548→21** (HO 612 `c531177` — one grid line, the `.mc-row` name track `1fr`→`minmax(0,240px)`; plus `3a2b3f1`'s HeaderBar titlebar, −2 on every route, and `8a23ff9`'s ≥1750px second roster pane) · **`/lobbying` 69→64** (HO 614 `13203eb`) · **`/trades` 55→52** (HO 613 `5e272d8`) · **`/news` 62→0** and **`/bills` 78→1** (HO 613 `3ba8c29`/`5d63ab4`, HO 614 `718d2ab`) · **`/hearings` →0 with M2 9→0** (HO 611 `f2a28dc`, the week ribbon + agenda replacing the five-column grid) · plus `c42ddb1`, `ActiveFilterStrip`, the conditional chrome every unfiltered pass had been blind to. **The two largest movers were never worked: `/changes` 471→1 and `/stale` 153→51, entirely by shared-component inheritance from the feed-row packing** — the phase's best single argument that C1 is a convention defect and not a per-route one, and why `/changes` enters P6 already M1-clean.

**THE ANCHOR-EXPIRY TRILOGY, and it is the arc's most reusable lesson because it happened three times in four handoffs while the rule for it was already written down.** HO 610's oddity said a falsification anchor pinned to a specific known defect has a shelf life exactly as long as the defect, and a remediation phase is in the business of ending them. **612 planned for it** — leg C was re-anchored from `/members` to `/changes` in its own commit (`63c7cd2`) because 612 was about to take `/members` to ~20 by design, at which point a silenced instrument and a successful fix read identically. **613 was saved by the fallback list rather than by foresight** — leg C walks a list and passes on the first route that fires, so when `/changes` collapsed to 1 the leg fell through to `/stale` (51) and the crawl proceeded. **614 exhausted it**: its §2 preamble had to grep the components it touched against `/stale`'s render tree *before the first commit*, because the fallback list had no depth left. **615 ended the pattern instead of extending the list.** Leg C is now `scripts/diagnostic/fixtures/layout-legc.html` — committed, loaded over `file://`, versioned with the instrument, reachable by no route and by no remediation. Four positives, four negatives (each a preserved false positive of an earlier instrument version), one calibration block asserting M1x = 3 exactly. **Positives catch silencing, negatives catch over-firing, and both directions now hold permanently in one leg.** `/stale` retires as an anchor with thanks; the M2 product-fallback probe retires with it, since the fixture's POS-2 / NEG-4 pair proves the panel detector directly for three fewer page loads.

**THE MULTI-RECT SAGA, whose shape is that REMEDIATION PRODUCED THE ARTIFACT.** v2 pushed every client rect as its own measurement item, so a cell that wrapped onto two lines became two items and its short last line could be measured against the next cell **with its own long first line sitting between them, invisibly**. C1 packing is precisely what lets a long cell wrap instead of being pushed out of view — so the artifact grew with every fix the phase shipped. It was hit at 613 (`/bills`' filter bar, a 223px residual proven to be the search input's painted box sitting in the next band), three times inside 613, and then at 614, whose commit body records it as *"the first the wrap fix itself caused."* **v3 (`f40768f`) measures INK SPANS**: one item per child node, extent = the union bbox of its rects, present in every band it overlaps, so no gap is ever measured across it. The measurement then forced a second rule the plan had not anticipated — **the continuation-band cut**: a band whose member set is a SUBSET of another band's is not a line, holds no item not already measured somewhere richer, and can only ever report a gap across items the fuller band contains. The live case was a `/trades` row whose second band held exactly `{date, amount}` and reported the 625px between them — an area the reader sees occupied by five single-line cells. Membership, never position or count: a genuine two-line row keeps distinct items on line 2 and is still measured.

**THE REMEDIATION KIT, as it actually accreted — eight moves, each earned by a route that resisted the previous one.** **Decompose before designing** (614's 67 split into four families of which one was the commit's; 613's `/bills` bar carried TWO pins). **Derive, don't tune** — the cap is arithmetic or it is a curve, never a guess. **The knee** (`/members` 240px = 16 names clipped to buy 11 rows; `/trades` 280px, just above p75, = 2 of 50 live) — with its precondition, that **when the label-ink SPREAD exceeds the 120px threshold no cap satisfies both "no truncation" and "under threshold" at once**, which is why both routes bought their last rows with truncations and filed the trade rather than taking it. **The two-ended derivation** (614: `cap = threshold + min-ink − column-gap = 120 + 239 − 14 = 345`, taken at 340 — derived against the SHORT end, because a cap sized off the widest label leaves the shortest one `cap − ink` of slack; HO 612 measured that directly, where a widest-name+2ch cap of 340px left the shortest name 231px of gap and took 548 to 527, not to ~0). **The wrap-cap** (614's rc cell is two names joined by an arrow and can wrap, so its cap cost zero truncation where a member name or a ticker description could not). **Wrap, don't pin** — the standing narrow-width guard. **Dependency-grep the anchor** (614 §2: prove leg C's route shares nothing with the work by dependency, not by route name). **Re-anchor rides the expiring commit** (612 `63c7cd2`).

**The chip rule got its reference row, and it demonstrates both halves at once.** `FilingRow` on `/lobbying` (HO 614 `13203eb`): **31 issue codes became plain dim text with 0 borders** because they are labels, and **181 bill chips kept their `1px solid rgb(217,119,6)`** because they are links with hover. The C3 rule since HO 609 — a border survives only on an interactive affordance — now has one row that shows the line rather than describing it.

**THE PHASE-CLOSE COMMITS (HO 615).** **`f40768f` instrument v3** (above) · **`8a05ad4` the exemption extension** — `FirmsLeaderboard` and `TopicCrosswalk` marked `data-viz-row`, `/lobbying` M1 **28→1**, both curves measured before either attribute was written: TopicCrosswalk's void is **bar-end → value** (37–648px, and capping the label track clips all 23 labels at every cap tried while leaving 18–21 rows over), FirmsLeaderboard's is **name → the bar column's fixed origin** (476–699px on 25 of 25, and cap 300px clips 0 while leaving 25 over) · **`294b51a` the rail** · **`9244cb9` `HearingsList` removed** with its 8 selectors, per-selector against a positive control that fired first. **The attribute went on the TABLE and not the row list**, because a column header's geometry is not independently choosable — it has to sit over the columns it labels, and both headers were over threshold (742px, 1051px) for exactly that reason.

**Three step-0 premise corrections, all MEASURED rather than reasoned, which is the only reason they were found.** (1) **`/trades`' 52 are not multi-rect artifacts.** They were expected to drop to ~0 under ink spans; the artifact inflated the MAGNITUDE (1137→248px) while the count fell only 52→32, because the row's FIRST band reads 129–248px of real column slack against the amounts axis and is over threshold either way — a genuine C1 residual, not an instrument one. (2) **`/lobbying`'s "11 artifact rows" is a subset, not a route total** — v2 reads 64 on that DOM, decomposing to ≈48 axis + ~11 artifact + ~5 other. (3) **`/committee/[id]` has no `.mc-pane` at all**, so "possibly, check its roster embed" is answered negative rather than left open. Each of the three had a plausible reasoned answer; none of the three reasoned answers was right.

**THE 883 WAS A JOINT CONSTRAINT AND THE FILING NAMED ONLY ONE HALF OF IT.** The HO 612 entry recorded "a 312px rail that never collapses" plus "a hard 883px minimum," which reads as one cause with a restatement. Measured: at 720 the pane is 688 and the content column 374, and the element setting the document width is a `.mc-r-num` cell whose row grid needs ~546px. **Neither the rail nor the row is the defect alone** — the rail is what leaves 374 for something needing 546, and relieving either one clears it. The same filing listed `/lobbying` `/bills` `/news` as sharing the class, which is a **class-presence claim, not a failure claim**: measured at 720, `/members` 883 (+163) and all three others exactly 720. So the fix hooks a new `.mb-pane` class rather than bare `.mc-pane` — widening it would restyle three already-closed routes to fix a fourth.

**THE RAIL REVERSAL, and it is the eyeball clause earning its place in a handoff.** §3 specified the rail stacking BELOW the content, C8's inverse, with "no disclosure widget unless stacking proves unusable in the eyeball." Built as specified, **every number was green and the rail landed at y = 19,643** — the member list is unpaginated at `LIST_LIMIT` 600, so the committee filter became unreachable on precisely the screens the rule exists for. Doc-width passed, bucket (b) passed, the wide layout was byte-identical, and the feature was useless: **no metric in this audit can see "off-screen."** Above and unbounded is no better (236 committees would bury the answer), so the rail is capped at 420px with internal scroll — not a new widget but the HO 492 `.lob-rail-scroll` convention, shipped one route over for the same reason. Acceptance: `/members` doc-width @720 **883→720**, bucket (b) **0/0/0** at 1024/900/720, 1024+ byte-identical (pane 992 / rail 312 / content 678 either side, and the media query cannot reach above 900 by construction).

**"The instrument was right; the fixture was wrong."** Leg C's NEG-2 — the wrapped-cell negative — was authored with a right-aligned trailing cell, which put a genuine 135px far-right anchor inside the row that exists to isolate the phantom. v3 reported it and the leg failed. **The correct response to a failing negative control is to check whether the control is clean, not to relax the assertion**: the phantom itself was already gone (590→61px once the second defect came out). A negative must carry exactly one property — the thing it denies. The same pass caught a first `/trades` leg-A bound of 200px **sized off one sampled row's 129**, re-derived to 400, which sits in the empty space between the two measured populations (real slack 129–248, artifact 625–1137), so the gate asks whether any artifact reading survived rather than whether the number got smaller.

**Comments are hypotheses, and this phase produced a base rate for it.** Three orphan claims tested across four handoffs — **one true-but-already-actioned, one false, one stale**: `FilingRow.tsx:5`'s claim that `IssueDrill` was orphaned was TRUE, but the file had already been deleted at `0765aaf`, so the `git rm` branch could not fire and HO 614 corrected the comment instead (`cc1b32f`). HO 611 §3's claim that the hearings embedded mode still existed was FALSE, and commit 2's own falsification is what surfaced it (`42efcee`). HO 610 found HO 300's `overflow-visible` note stale in both halves. **One in three of this arc's load-bearing comments was wrong about the code beside it.**

**THE P4 GAP, owned here rather than discovered by whoever opens P5.** P4's scope list in `layout-conventions-arc.md` §1 is eight routes: `/bills` `/news` `/amendments` `/nominations` `/hearings` `/members` `/lobbying` `/trades`. The HO 610 order ruling named *"hearings FIRST, then members, then bills/news/trades, with lobbying LAST"* — six — and **`/amendments` (v3 20) and `/nominations` (v3 15) fell out of the phase without ever being declined.** No handoff dropped them; an ORDER RULING was read as a SCOPE LIST, and each subsequent HO took its route from the order. **The reusable shape: an order ruling is a permutation of a scope list and can silently be a subset of one, so a phase closes by diffing against its SCOPE LIST, not against the order it was worked in.** Neither is a P5 or P6 route; both are carried into a P4-residual HO in the backlog.
