**The v3 phase-close crawl — 26 routes, 72 page loads, 77.4s, 0 retries — reported both ways so the instrument change is isolable.** **Same DOM (`cc1b32f`), two rulers:** v2 312 → v3 180, i.e. **−132, of which only 27 is product work** (the exemption). The remaining 105 is the instrument no longer lying, and it is concentrated exactly where the wrap artifact lived: `/reports` 16→1, `/trades` 52→32, `/patterns` 11→3, `/welcome` 8→1, and `/lobbying` 64→28 before the exemption took it to 1. **M2 (8) and M4 (709 elements / 65,278px) did not move at all** — a clean control that v3's changes were confined to M1 and M5. **The v3 P5/P6 baseline:** site **M1 180** (M1a 47 · M1b 133) · **M1x 151** · **M2 8** · **M4 709 / 65,278px**, ranking **`/stale` 47 · `/trades` 32 · `/committee-detail` 28 · `/amendments` 20 · `/members` 16 · `/nominations` 15 · `/member-detail` 4 · `/bill-detail` 4 · `/patterns` 3 · `/vote` 3 · `/electoral` 2**, with **`/` `/bills` `/news` `/hearings` `/changes` `/search` `/president` `/race-detail` at 0**.

**M1x IS A CANDIDATE COUNT, NOT A ROW COUNT, and the exemption extension is the first marking large enough to make that visible.** `/lobbying` reads 125: itemised, **50 are actual rows** (25 firm rows + 1 header + 23 topic buttons + 1 header) and **75 are wrappers and tracks** that pass a width-based candidate gate — a 1255px bar track and a firm-name span stretched by its `1fr` column are both ≥600px wide. The same inflation has always been inside the funnel's 13. The handoff predicted "~48 into M1x" and "M1x ≈ 13 + the new count"; neither maps, because M1 counts over-threshold rows and M1x counts candidates. **The actionable number is the M1 drop.**

**M5 gained its (a)/(b) split and immediately retired a worry this arc had been carrying for four handoffs.** M5x = the element's OWN style declares `text-overflow: ellipsis` or a line clamp — authored, permanent, not actionable; bucket (b) = real. **`/members`' "worst narrow-width surface on the site," 721/217/1301 overflowing elements at 1024/900/720, is 100% M5x.** Every one is an authored `truncate`. There was never any real overflow there. **The honest caveat, recorded rather than buried:** M5x captures ellipsis and clamp only, so `/`'s bucket (b) of 86–88 is **the markets marquee counted at six nesting levels** (`markets-tape--scroll` 7519/976, `-track` 7542/7538, `-half`, `-set`, `-item`…) plus the three ancestors carrying its +85px document overflow at 720, and `/bill-detail`'s single (b) at 720 is `pre.bdp-rawpre` (710/686). Neither is narrow-width breakage. A scroll-container-by-design third category is filed in the backlog and deliberately NOT taken here.

**Prod-verified converged** at `9244cb9` — `/api/version` to the SHA, then the served stylesheet read directly: `.mb-pane` present with its `max-height:420px` inside `max-width:900`, `hearings-band-head` and `hearings-empty` at 0, and `.hearing-row` at 7 as the positive control that the grep can still see a live rule. Docs: HO 615 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 615.**

**Also (HO 616–620), the layout arc's close — P4-residual, P5, P6a and P6b worked to their scope lists, then the instrument rebuilt one last time so the terminal crawl could be trusted as the maintenance baseline; no theme-% change (every surface kept its features, only their arrangement moved).** Five HOs, twelve route commits, two corrective docs commits and two instrument commits, and the arc ends on an **arithmetic identity rather than a target**: site M1 equals the named residue register, site M1x equals the exemption registry, and nothing is unexplained. The terminal crawl (`62c25e4`, 26 routes, 72 page loads, 76.3s, 0 retries) is what every future layout HO reads first.

**READ THE ERA LABEL ON EVERY NUMBER BELOW. v1 / v2 / v3 / v4 are four rulers and they do not compare — and this block never launders an instrument note as a remediation claim.** The full chain, every arrow attributed: **v1 2,152 → 2,131** (product — P3, and 24 of 26 routes were byte-identical across it) **→ v2 1,634** (instrument) **→ 312** (product — P4) **→ v3 180** (−132 on the same DOM, **of which 27 is product** and 105 is the instrument no longer lying) **→ 27** (product — P4-residual at HO 616, P5 at 617, P6a at 618) **→ 17** (product — P6b at 619) **→ v4 17** (instrument, and this arrow is **flat by construction**). **The bridge control, which is why the run was done twice on one DOM:** v3 and v4 read `62c25e4` and **M1 17 · M1a 0 · M1b 17 · M1x 332 · M2 3 are byte-identical across the two rulers**, because v4 touched only M3, M4 and M5. **The M3 and M4 arrows are instrument, all of them** — v3's M4 601 / 57,458px partitions exactly into v4's **M4-true 14 / 853px + M4f 587 / 56,605px**, an identity, not a re-measurement, and no route's M3 movement between the two columns is a product claim.

**HO 616 — P4-residual, and its whole value is that four routes with the same-looking defect got three different answers.** The axis question — *does a numeric column on a shared x qualify for the `data-viz-row` exemption?* — was ruled route by route, by applied curve, never by pattern. **`/trades` 32 → 1: the attribute was BARRED and the fix was structural** (`7eba4be`). A right-aligned money column is clause 1's named non-qualifier; the reorder moved the variable-width asset name **last**, converting per-row interior slack into trailing gap, which is the target state and unthresholded — and HO 613's 280px cap **retired** rather than being re-tuned, clipping going 10 rows → 0 at 2560. **The reorder alone fixed almost nothing, which is the finding:** the row's real gap was type→amount at 129px, two fixed tracks' slack, and the repair was three column widths derived from both closed vocabularies rendered in the real cell across 1,209 trades. Two of them were invisible defects, not gaps: the **amount** track at 110px was narrower than *every* value in its vocabulary, so the money column had been wrapping to two lines on every row — M1 skips a second band holding one item and M4's 12-char limit excludes a 16-char cell, so **neither instrument could see it**; and the **date** track at 64px, verified by HO 613 at 1024/900/720, overlapped the chamber chip by 3px at 2560 because the `--fs` ladder steps every token +2px by the widest stop — *a fixed track checked only at narrow widths is unchecked at wide ones, because the type in it is not the same size there.* Fixing the wide row then **caused a narrow-width regression** (bucket (b) 0 → 105 at 720, document over by 40px) which `minmax(0,N)` and an 80px-earlier compact breakpoint closed — *a fixed track is a pin in disguise: it cannot give width back.* **`/amendments` 20 → 0 and `/nominations` 15 → 0: the attribute was SPENT, on curves** (`780da6d`). Both render `RankRow`, which the handoff called identical and which reading both files falsified — one was a local function at `1.6fr`, the other inline at `1.4fr` with an active state, i.e. **near-identical, which is worse than identical, because it is drift that already happened**; extracted to `components/RankRow.tsx` with `labelFr` deliberately left a prop. The applied curves say mark: `/nominations` live worst 1,347px and **cap 200 → 2,071px**, `/amendments` live 652px and cap 200 → 917px — every cap worse, because the bar sits in a fractional `2fr` track that absorbs whatever the label gives up, so the dominant void is bar-end → count, *which is the encoding itself: a short bar with a long run to its number IS the small number.* **`/members` 16 → 13: decomposed to accepted residue** (`c400450`), after the **seventh application of one pattern** — a `flex: 1` spacer element pinning the three strip heads' trailing items at 1,843 / 1,671 / 1,534px. **Heads only: the HO 612 fence lifted for chrome and stayed shut on the plots**, and the reason that class stayed invisible for three handoffs is worth the record — *these were carried by a scope fence, not missed by an instrument, which is a different failure mode and a slower one, because a fence looks like a decision every time it is re-read.*

**And HO 616 corrected the arc's own registry rather than defending it** (`a2ca42e` + `1b9d334`). HO 615's `FirmsLeaderboard` entry read *"cap 260px clips 2 names and leaves 25 of 25 over threshold; cap 300px clips 0 and leaves 25 over — a cap cannot reach it."* **That claim was false, and it was false because it was MODELLED** — computed from track arithmetic that subtracts each label's own excess over the cap, which reports *no change* for any cap wider than the longest label and therefore cannot see that capping a `1fr` label track **moves the fixed bar track left**. Applied in the page and re-read: **cap 180px → worst 114px, 0 of 25 over, 10 of 25 names clipped.** A cap *can* reach it. **The exemption stands on the corrected number and not the wrong one** — a reachable cap that clips 40% of firm names is not an acceptable knee beside this arc's others (3% on `/members`, 0.33% on `/trades`) — and **the false claim is quoted in place rather than deleted, because a registry whose corrections are invisible is a registry nobody re-checks.** `TopicCrosswalk` was re-measured the same way and **confirmed with its mechanism attached**: every cap makes it worse (180 → 873px), the modelled curve having landed on the right answer for the wrong reason. The rule went to SKILL as one sentence: **the curve is APPLIED in the page and re-read, never computed from track arithmetic** — *a marking with no curve is a silenced defect, and a marking with a modelled curve is a silenced defect with a number attached.* One further correction the arithmetic forced on the way: the funnel — the **oldest** marking — had only ever filed one unit, and a first draft of the site total asserted 113 visual rows from a guessed row count; measured, it is 13 candidates / 6 rows. **The unit caveat was written at HO 615 and the oldest entry had not been brought up to it.** Site M1x 151 → 310. **P4 then closed on its scope list** — eight routes, every disposition in a permitted form (worked-to-zero / exempted-with-curve / accepted-residue-decomposed), the first enforcement of the SKILL rule HO 615 wrote after `/amendments` and `/nominations` fell out of the phase silently.

**HO 617 — P5, and the two positioned axes got OPPOSITE answers from the same applied method, which is the clearest thing the registry has produced.** `/committee-detail` 28 → 0 · M2 2 → 0 · M4 11 elements / 662px → 1 / 42px (`a30f063`); `/member-detail` 4 → 0 · `/bill-detail` 4 → 0 · `/vote` 3 → 2 · `/race-detail` 0 verify-only (`cd8c3d7`). **Decomposition first, and both of the handoff's "likely" families were wrong** — not the `BillRow`/`NominationRow` lists (zero contribution) and not the two charts (SVG, never in M1 at all). What it actually was: 27 roster rows on a `grid-cols-[1fr_auto]` pin, one `ml-auto` header link at 1,803px, two grid wrappers stretching — one of them **"Recent activity" holding a single line of empty-state text stretched to 1,042px beside a 27-member roster, 954px of it blank, the largest single stretched panel left on the site** — and ten `WatchCell` spans that rendered `<span className="hearing-watch" aria-hidden />` for meetings with no video, 62px each, **620px of the route's 662px**. **The axis pair.** `.ideology-axis` was **MARKED**: applied curve (axis width → worst gap / scale / party-median spread) 2490 → 2299px / 1195px per DW-NOMINATE unit / 1878px · 1400 → 1209 / 672 / 1055 · 1000 → 809 / 480 / 753 · 700 → 509 / 336 / 527 · 500 → 309 / 240 / 376 — reaching the 120px threshold needs a ~420px axis, **a 6× loss of resolution on the one distance the rail exists to show**. `.v2f-bar` was **CAPPED**: live 2486 → 379px worst · 1200 → 165 · 1000 → 132 · 800 → 98 · 640 → 72 — a cap reaches it, and **position here encodes SEQUENCE, not magnitude**, so compressing costs the reader nothing. Same method, opposite verdicts, and the discriminator is what the position *means* — **magnitude is marked, sequence is capped**, scoped to `.bdp` because `.v2f-bar` is the shared `BillStageBar` and SKILL's HO 507 rule forbids editing it for one page's benefit. **`/vote` produced the plateau curve and the residual was filed rather than chased:** `columns-2 sm:columns-3 lg:columns-4` topped out at 4 columns ~630px wide holding 120–235px names, so a row read 511px across the gutter; `columns-[240px]` gives 9 columns at 2560, and **240/16 is the ELBOW of the applied curve, not a target** — (260,24) → 196px · (240,16) → 160 · (228,12) → 132 · (220,12) → 132 · (200,12) → **1192**. It plateaus at 132 because below ~240 the binding term stops being the column width and becomes the ragged fill of the final column, and 200 breaks it outright. **No width reaches zero, so 2 rows are filed decomposed.** And the HO 617 HALT produced a finding that bounded its own arithmetic: `/member-detail`'s 2,312px of M4 was **not** the reserved empty-state panel §2 predicted — itemised, it is 27 populated elements (a photo, 2 stat tiles, 4 tab buttons, 20 feed-row parts at 99px each because the feed row is 99px tall). **M4 counts SHORT-TEXT elements, not empty ones**, which on any feed-bearing route inflates hugely, and P6a's scope had been about to be set off M4 pixels.

**HO 618 — P6a, where the defect was C4 wearing C1's clothes and the signature was the tell.** `/stale` M1 47 → 0 (`1c1058f`). §1's live question — do the momentum rows carry bars on a shared origin, i.e. is this the registry question again? — **was answered by measuring, not by pattern**: they do not, so no exemption was spent. **All 47 rows read EXACTLY 121px, one pixel over threshold, and a uniform gap is not a content-driven stretch — it is a fixed reservation.** Across all 50 rows: 45 no-HEARD rows at 121px, 4 HEARD at 57px, 1 at 65px; inside a no-HEARD cell, `.row-support` 40px reading "—" then `.row-hslot` **52px reading nothing**. HEARD shows on 5 of 50 rows and the badge right-aligns against the age column either way, so the reservation bought nothing. **M4 could not see it, and that qualifies HO 617's conclusion rather than contradicting it: M4 keys on HEIGHT, and this reservation is 52px WIDE and content-height tall — a horizontally-reserved slot is invisible to M4 by construction.** M1 caught it only because the slot happened to sit between two other cells. **`/stale` M3 13.62 → 6.55, and the party-red age heat died at source** (`65d5eee`), splitting **the same helper by what its two modes MEAN**: `daysSinceColor` painted both staleness and desk-time overrun with `--party-republican` — the oddity filed at HO 610, forbidden by the SKILL party rule since, *because a party token carries party, and a red number reads Republican before it reads old.* **Staleness is not an alarm**: on the route whose entire premise is staleness, per-row red is noise and the number plus the furthest-first sort already carry it, so it returns no colour and inherits the row. **Desk-time is a real threshold** — past 10 days a bill has outrun the constitutional veto clock — so it keeps a hot end, moved to amber-bright, *this app's urgency colour and nobody's party.* §2 predicted `/bills` would tick down with it and **it didn't, for an exact reason**: `daysSinceColor` runs only when `daysSinceMode` is set, and plain `/bills` has no days-since column, so it never consumed the party-red and had nothing to inherit — a convention correction with zero metric movement anywhere, stated rather than left as an unexplained flat number. **The quiet pass is structural, not a list of dimmer colours, and that distinction is what actually moved M3**: M3 counts descendants whose *own* style differs from their parent's, so repainting a label a quieter shade still competes and still scores; the row's base ink moved down to dim and every label below dropped its declaration and **inherits**. **The target was ≤5 and it lands at 6.55, and the last 1.5 was not taken** — all nine survivors are exactly what C3 says keeps colour (title, stage pill, prior stage, chamber rail, sponsor link, watch button, party bracket, momentum signal, heard badge), and the handoff's own guard said hitting 2 would mean something functional had been deleted. **`/changes` closed by verification, in its own words: worked to zero by shared-component inheritance from the HO 613 `BillRow` pack** — the route that proved C1 was a *convention* defect rather than a per-route one, closing without a commit of its own. **Site M1a → 0** with `/stale`'s 47, and it stays 0 through the terminal crawl. A third commit the plan didn't have then closed a real document overflow the guard surfaced (`913bb8b`): `TopicFilter` rendered 24 chips in a `flex` row with **no `flex-wrap`** inside a `.filter-chips` section that *does* wrap, so below ~1,020px both P6a routes scrolled sideways — pre-existing, fixed at source, **one word, and it is the kit's own guard**: *a fixed-width row inside a wrapping parent is a pin wearing the parent's clothes.*

**HO 619 — P6b, the batch, and the convergence the instrument built to score it could not see.** Three commits. **The rails size to content** (`67767f1`): `/members` was paying 2,618px of dead panel and `/bills` 1,807px, and `align-items: start` capped both — **but the scoping was measured, not assumed.** All four `.mc-pane` consumers stretch their rail by default; only two waste anything by it. `/lobbying` reads **−1,958px** and `/news` **−14px** — their rails are bounded scroll containers whose ink *overflows* the box, so capping would shorten a rail that has more to show, **which is the negative-dead case and it was left alone.** And **the seam was load-bearing and could not ride the rail**: `.mc-rail`'s `border-right` is the only thing marking the split on a 2,528px pane, so it moved to the **container** (a 1px full-height background at `--mc-seam-x`) — *because either child can be the shorter one*, and a filtered `/bills` inverts which column is tall. **The chip rule converged by function, measured in the DOM rather than read out of JSX** (`8937235`): 796 topic chips across nine `BillRow` surfaces, and **zero of them are a link, a button, or a filter** — every one is a `<span>` inside a Tooltip. So both of the ruling's open questions were answered *the rule, not the pass*: `/stale`'s chips were **not** links, so HO 618's quiet pass removed no affordance and the border stays gone; `/changes`' chips do **not** filter, so the divergence was **not principled — it was the rule not yet applied, to 428 chips on one route.** Two surfaces keep their border for the opposite reason (the `/changes` and `/stale` filter rows are real click-to-filter links, hrefs measured), and `/changes` now shows both halves on one screen: **24 bordered controls above 132 rows of plain labels.** A separate finding from the same audit: HO 618's `plain` prop **never reached `TopicChips`' responsive MOBILE branch**, so `/stale` still shipped one bordered chip per row to a phone — 50 of 140 — *a prop that styles by function has to reach every branch that renders it*, and that is one of the two citations behind this sweep's second SKILL rule. **And the commit reported honestly that M3 could not score it:** M3 sampled the M1a groups (`kids >= 3`), the chip-bearing rows carry two visible element children, so **the 796-chip convergence was invisible to the instrument built to score row noise** — filed for 620 rather than presented as a null result. **The batch** (`d56608e`) took nine routes, and **seven of the eleven residual rows were ONE family**: a section head with its trailing element pinned to the far right of a 1,400–2,500px bar, by `margin-left: auto`, `justify-content: space-between`, or a `flex: 1` middle cell — *the same three spellings this phase has now removed eight times.* `/patterns` 3 → 0 · `/electoral` 2 → 0 · `/reports` `/trends` `/watchlist` `/report-detail` `/welcome` 1 → 0 each, with `/lobbying` 1 and `/trades` 1 **accepted, priced at HO 615**. `/patterns`' bars header is the arc's last new exemption and ships with an applied curve — void 781px live and **worse at every cap** (180 → 1,166px) while clipping 0 of 5 labels, the `TopicCrosswalk` shape and not the `FirmsLeaderboard` one; M1x **18 candidates / 6 rows** (5 bars + 1 header), site M1x 314 → 332. `/welcome`'s live panel is a **static replica of the pre-609 dashboard row** — which is why it held the site's worst M3 at 8.86, still carrying a boxed id, an underlined non-link sponsor and bordered chips — and the quiet pass mirrored the live reference row on `/` rather than inventing a second treatment: **8.86 → 5.00 with all five survivors named, colour-only, zero borders, zero backgrounds.** One rule fell out of it: **M3 counts a colour difference in EITHER direction, so dimming an element to quiet it scores exactly like brightening one** — the first pass left three survivors scoring only because they were *quieter* than a bright parent, and moving the row's base ink down is the honest fix rather than the metric one. **Two formal acceptances, decomposed rather than inherited:** `/electoral`'s M2 2 × 52px hero-stat band (reopening costs the align-start machinery plus two moved seams to reclaim 98px and buys three ragged-bottom cells where a stat band's job is to read as one aligned row) and `/welcome`'s 775px aside, which is **not a stretched sibling at all** — `.split` is `min-height: 100vh`, so both columns are viewport-sized and `align-items: start` would collapse the landing to a 915px band inside a 1,440px viewport. **Site M1 27 → 17, M2 5 → 3, and the 17 IS the whole of the accepted-residue register.**

**HO 620 — instrument v4, and it shipped rather than being filed because the terminal crawl is the baseline every future HO reads and v3 would have handed it two known lies** (`59a45cd`). Four measurement changes, **no threshold moved**, falsified on the fixture before any route. **(1) M4 splits into M4-true and M4f (furniture)** — the emitter (≥40px tall, ≤12 chars) cannot tell reserved emptiness from a short label doing its job in a tall row, and furniture is *inside a repeated-sibling row AND carrying non-empty text*, **both clauses**, because a genuinely empty box in a repeated row is the `/stale` reservation and must stay in M4-true. The HO 617 hand-split became the instrument's own output, on its own line, the M1x pattern. **(2) M4w — width-reserved slots**, because M4 keys on height and `/stale`'s 52px `.row-hslot` was the whole of that route's M1 while M4 read nothing. **(3) M5s — scroll-by-design**, per the backlog entry's shape, counted **once at the outermost of its chain**, since six levels of one marquee was the whole distortion; the definition deviates from that entry's literal phrasing and says so — *"…with an animated or explicitly-scrollable CHILD"* misses `pre.bdp-rawpre`, one of its own two worked examples, because a `<pre>`'s only child is a text node. **(4) M3 samples every repeated-sibling group with ≥2 element children**, retiring the `kids >= 3` gate it had inherited from M1a.

**`/changes` is the worked example of both lies at once, and it is why v4 shipped.** Its **M3 3.00 was 10 nav items at 3.00 apiece** — the route's feed rows never entered the sample at all, so a number the whole phase had read as "this route is quiet" was measuring the navigation and wearing the route's name; under v4 it reads **11.23**, and **that movement is instrument, not product**. Its **M4 267 elements / 26,523px** — the largest single M4 figure on the site, and the number that would have set P6's scope — is **267 furniture and ZERO M4-true**: 267 short labels in repeated rows. *The headline number was entirely not the thing it is named after.* M1a is untouched by the M3 change, so this is a second, wider group pass and M1 cannot move with it.

**The M4w near-miss is kept in the record because the fixture comment had already named the failure mode and the predicate committed it anyway.** M4w's first draft carried **no negative control**, passed a green fixture, and then fired **359 times on `/members`** — 201 committee-activity bars, 153 topic-mix segments, 4 tracks. **A bar is also an empty element whose width was chosen; it is the exact opposite of a reservation, because its width IS the datum.** What separates them is **paint**: a reservation is invisible by definition. The predicate now requires no paint of its own — **`/members` 359 → 0** — and the fixture carries an identically-sized **painted** segment as the control it should have had first. This is the same distinction `data-viz-row` draws for M1, drawn here by a property of the element rather than by an attribute someone has to remember to add.

**Then the crawl falsified v4 itself, which is why the bridge run is mandatory rather than optional** (`62c25e4`). `/bill/[id]`'s raw-JSON block classified correctly, but `/`'s markets marquee — **worked example #1 in the very backlog entry this column implements** — read M5s 0 and left all 86–88 in bucket (b). Two causes, both measured before either was touched. **(1) The instrument was suppressing the property it keyed on.** The audit runs its context with `reducedMotion: "reduce"`, deliberately, because a moving marquee would perturb the geometry every other measurement depends on — and `globals.css` carries `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .markets-tape-track { animation: none } }`. Measured both ways: no-preference reads `markets-marquee`, reduce reads `none`. **So the computed animation-name of the site's one marquee is `none` under the only conditions this audit ever looks at it.** Animation is now read from the **stylesheet** — an element is animated *by authorship* if any rule matching it declares a non-`none` animation-name, whatever the active media query says about playing it. **(2) `overflow-x: clip`, not `hidden`** — the tape computes to `clip` and the predicate checked `hidden` only; they are one family and both are accepted. **The fixture could not have caught (1), and that is the lesson rather than the excuse:** its marquee case has no reduced-motion override, so it passed under both the broken and the fixed predicate and **proved nothing about either**. Case **v4-C2** closes the gap — the same geometry plus the same `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { animation: none }` the app carries — and it fails on the first draft and passes now. **A fixture that does not model the conditions the instrument runs under is not a trap: a preference-conditional style is part of the measured system.** That sentence is now a clause on SKILL's falsification-constraints paragraph. The fix was **sequenced after the closeout commit rather than amended into the v4 commit**, because the crawl is what surfaced it and collapsing that into "v4 shipped correct" would erase the reason the bridge run exists.

**The closeout pair, neither left for the maintenance baseline to inherit** (`25493bd`). **`/bill/[id]`'s three metabox chips are now plain** — HO 619 had left them bordered on the argument that a metabox *field* is not a repeated row and C3's noise target is per-row multiplication, which is true and **is not what the rule asks: the rule asks what the chip DOES.** Grepped, they are the sole remaining non-plain `TopicChips` consumer, a bare render with no href and no handler, i.e. labels. Same rule, no special case, and the last chip divergence on the site closes; the `plain` prop now has no false consumer, **which is not dead code — it is the mechanism the rule needs the day a chip becomes a control.** **`/welcome`'s `landing_readout` wraps** — at 900 the 44% pitch column leaves ~292px and the three stats need 331px, **the last real narrow-width item on the site and, of all places, on the front door.** Fixed by wrapping, not by shrinking the 18px stat padding to buy 39px: *a tuned number goes stale the next time a stat's digits grow, and "wrap, don't pin" is the kit's standing narrow guard.* It **predates P6b** — every changed selector in the HO 619 landing diff was `.winbar` or inside `.row`, and `.readout` is in the other column — so this was never that HO's regression; it was simply the first time `/welcome`'s narrow ladder had been run.

**The fixture reads 18 of 18 exact, and the standard it now holds is the PAIRED CONTROL.** The ten pre-existing readings are unchanged (POS-1..4, NEG-1/1b/2/3/4, CAL M1x 3), and the **eight** new cases ship as *pairs* rather than as bare "the new column is non-zero" assertions: **M4f 6 / M4-true 0** with an *empty*-box control that must stay M4-true · **M4w exactly 3** with the painted-bar control at 0 · **M5s exactly 1** (the outermost frame, inner level absorbed) with an un-animated twin that must stay in bucket (b) · **M5s 1 under a reduced-motion override** (v4-C2, the fixup's own case) · **M3 sampling 2-child rows** at a non-zero mean. A new column asserted only in the direction it was built to fire is satisfied by an instrument that fires on everything, which is legs A and B's both-direction problem in a smaller frame.

**The terminal baseline, v4 at `62c25e4`.** Site **M1 17** (M1a **0** · M1b 17) · **M1x 332** · **M2 3** · **M4-true 14 / 853px** · **M4f 587 / 56,605px** · **M4w 0** — the live width-reservation is fixed, so the fixture carries the only positive. **22 of 26 routes read M1 0**; the four that don't are `/members` 13, `/vote` 2, `/lobbying` 1, `/trades` 1, **which is the residue register exactly.** M3 spans 2.21 (`/trends`) to 11.23 (`/changes`) under the new sampler. Narrow: the marquee now lands in **M5s** at every width on `/`, `/`'s bucket (b) reads 0 / 0 / 8 at 1024 / 900 / 720 against v3's 88 / 78 / 86, `/members`' 721 / 4 / 472 is still **100% M5x authored truncation**, and `/bill/[id]`'s single 720 item is M5s. The gap distribution's 500+px bucket is 0, and **every route was measured — none skipped.**

**The two identities, checked as arithmetic and not as prose, because they are what "done" means here.** **Site M1 17 == the permanent-residue register, entry by entry:** `/members` **13** = 11 knee-marginal `a.mc-row` at 121–131px (priced at HO 612; reopening costs 16 more truncated names) + 2 `.mc-row-hdr` at 230px, a short column label over a wide data column · `/vote` **2**, the plateau residual, no column width reaches zero · `/lobbying` **1**, a column header priced at HO 615 · `/trades` **1**, the 196px amount-column header, irreducible without narrowing a column sized to stop the money wrapping. **Site M1x 332 == the `data-viz-row` registry, marking by marking:** `StageFunnel` 13 + 13 (two routes, one component, 6 visual rows each) + `/lobbying` 125 (50 rows) + `/amendments` 94 (30) + `/nominations` 65 (15) + `MemberIdeology` 4 (2) + `/patterns` `PatternBars` 18 (6) = **332 candidates across seven markings on six routes, 115 visual rows.** Both sums balanced on the first check. **The arc's one-line identity: site M1 equals named residue; site M1x equals the registry; nothing unexplained.** **Also notes now run through HO 620.**

**Also (HO 621–623), Absence Watch shipped, plus the dashboard fit-finish that followed it — no theme-% change (a new front-page surface over an existing measure, not a new data domain; 623 is arrangement only).** **HO 621 re-ran a probe written four months earlier, and the reconcile is the reusable half.** Three of its four verbatim-copied facts were unchanged (the delegate carve byte-for-byte, `PARTICIPATION_FLOOR = 50`, the HO 566/540 lag query, line moved by one). The fourth — the population predicate — had **changed mechanism at HO 595 without changing semantics**: `getParticipationStrip` stopped aggregating `member_votes` live and now reads the materialized `member_participation`, whose writer computes exactly the two integers the probe had been computing inline. Two things follow that a re-run would otherwise have assumed. **The floor did NOT migrate into the materializer** — it is still applied at read time by each consumer, so the table holds all ~554 bioguides and "floored population" is a property of the query, not of the row set. And **`member_participation` is cumulative-only** (four columns, no `vote_id`, no date, no window), so a recency rule remains a genuinely new read path that nothing materializes. The probe's M1/M1.4 moved onto the table, because the ruling turns on the probe's population being the *strip's* population; **a new M1.0 measures the one axis on which they can now diverge — staleness — and it read clean: stamp 0.44d old, uniform across rows, 0 members differing, 0 missing.** That instrument exists because a stale materialized table renders correct-looking numbers with no error and no visual tell. **The four reads.** M1's sweep: House `>=10%` names 18 members, `>=15%` seven, `>=20%` three; **the Senate is a different distribution entirely** — `>=10%` names exactly three and nothing at all sits above 15%, so no single cumulative cut serves both chambers. M2's divergence sets were the whole point and both failure modes were **real, and lopsided 6:1**: six House members carried `>=15%` cumulative while missing 0–2 of the last 30 rolls (five voted on the final pre-recess roll call), against one — Biggs, 26 of 30 missed at 9.0% — invisible to a rate. Senate divergence was 0 both ways. M3b measured the window path at **306ms / 16,028 rows**. M1.4 found **exactly one** structurally invisible member (Darline Graham, SC, 14 votes); no delegate is floor-excluded — the six are in the population and carved by rule. **Two things the output showed that nobody asked for, and both changed the build.** The **House was in recess**, so its "last 30 roll calls" was a two-day pre-recess burst and its last vote was 15.2 days old — which is *why* those six sticky members all read `window=0/30`, and why a present-tense strip would have been false about an entire chamber. And **streak and window disagree on the two shapes**: Wilson and McConnell were 30/30 with streak 30, while Biggs was 26/30 with **streak 0** — only a streak distinguishes *gone* from *spotty*. **The ruling: the trigger is the consecutive-miss streak, R1 is dead, R2's rate rides inside the row as evidence rather than as the trigger, the copy is dated, and the acute/chronic division of labour with the participation surfaces is recorded so it is not later "fixed".** **HO 622 built it in two commits.** `abd8768` is `getAbsenceWatch`: **candidate filter first, walk second** — Phase A is the probe's own window query aggregated DB-side, a *necessary* condition (a >=30-streak member necessarily has every explicit position in the last 30 rolls equal to `not_voting`) that bounds the expensive walk. Its predicate is **`nv = n AND nv > 0`, deliberately NOT `nv >= 30`**: a genuine streak member with NO-DATA rows inside the window has fewer than 30 rows there, so the tighter-looking filter would drop exactly the member the band exists to name — the loose one still returned 4 candidates across both chambers, of which 2 survived the walk. Phase B walks to a 120-roll bound in one query per chamber. `lastCastDate` is **sliced from `vote_date`, never parsed**, because the House stores a `-04:00` offset and the Senate a `Z`, and a `Date` round-trip would shift a late-evening House vote onto the next calendar day. **The degradation guard sits OUTSIDE the `unstable_cache` boundary** (HO 598): an empty array here is the good-news state that renders nothing, so caching a failure as "nobody is absent" would be a silent wrong answer with no tell — and this was not hypothetical, the guard caught the real `Invariant: incrementalCache missing` throw when the first oracle tried to call the function from `tsx`. `12b3e57` is the band, in the HO 608 slot the mock draws it in, with **two recorded deviations**: the mock drew the rule 621 ruled OUT (missed >= 20 in 21 days, six members, a sparkline and a CHRONIC chip), so the row anatomy is the ruled one and only the mock's shell carries over; and its two-column `.abw2` grid does not, because that grid existed to fit six rows and a permanently half-empty column is a reserved box under another name. **The mock's footer ships as designed and is load-bearing** — the delegate carve is population-correctness, so Radewagen at 82.4% missed is absent *by rule*, and without the disclosure that absence is mysterious rather than legible. **The probe was the oracle, and the oracle's own first cut was wrong in the instructive way.** It reads the rendered band off a running server (the cached function cannot be called outside a Next request context) and reported FAIL on all six assertions against a render that was **correct** — because it whitespace-split the row and read `R | - | KY` as three cells, not knowing React emits `<!-- -->` separators between interpolations. **The same class bit the narrow-width probe harder**: its first version bucketed rects by rounded `top`, which under the six-token `--fs` ladder splits one baseline-aligned row into three and measures a gap *across* a cell sitting invisibly between them — a phantom **221px** interior gap, precisely the layout audit's own v1 bug that instrument v2 fixed with vertical-interval clustering. Corrected, the interior gap is **8px at every width — exactly the flex `column-gap`** — and that number, not the audit's, is what proves C1 here, because **the audit's M1 does not score this band's rows at all**: two sibling rows fall between M1a's `>=3 siblings` gate and M1b's `no siblings` gate, so its 0 is true but *under-determined* on the new element. The other 622 expectation that missed: **M4-true is 1 / 42px before AND after** — that residue is the HEARINGS tab label HO 610 closed on, and the band contributed zero, which is the honest claim rather than the predicted 0. **The C4 branch was fired, not narrated** — threshold bumped to 9999 in the working tree only, rebuilt, `/` rendered **zero `abw` nodes** with nav and hearings intact as controls; reverted, tree clean, nothing committed. **HO 623 is fit-finish, and one of its two items did not exist.** `25629d9` widened the feed column **`minmax(400px,27%)` -> `minmax(480px,31%)`** (an owner ruling and a recorded mock deviation, the HO 610 masthead precedent): measured **678 -> 779px at 2560** and **400 -> 480 at the 1440 floor**, both under the ~850px C8 ceiling. **The `bxp` container query moved with it, 440 -> 520, and the handoff's stated reason for that did not survive measurement** — at 1440 the panel's inner width is **420px**, already below the old 440, so 1440 would have stayed stacked either way; the boundary that actually moves is **1680**, inner **446px**, which under 440 the *widened* column would have flipped to wide mode at a cramped 446. The transferable rule is **a container-query threshold is derived from the container it was tuned against, so widening the container obliges you to re-derive the threshold** — otherwise a width change silently changes which viewports collapse. `/bills` was checked rather than assumed: 1140px at 1440, far past either threshold. `d754db3` dimmed **past ribbon days** to the same 0.42 the schedule rows already use, so one opacity means one thing in that panel: in NEXT mode the bar reads `NEXT · THU SEP 17` while the ribbon still showed `TUE 7 · WED 9 · THU 4` at full strength, which reads as three pending days the bar is ignoring. **The counts were correct — the ribbon is a week total, not a forecast — so this was a DISPLAY contradiction, not a data one**, and that is the find worth keeping: *a ribbon of counts beside a NEXT jump is data-coherent and display-contradictory until the past dims.* It needed one JSX branch, flagged rather than taken silently, because no selector can know which day is past and the shown day can sit outside the week entirely. **The other item — the NEXT-state spacing jump — DOES NOT REPRODUCE, and was measured rather than eyeballed away:** `bar.bottom -> row.top` is **0px at 2560, 1700 and 1440**, `.hsch-wrap`'s height equals the single row's exactly, and no element occupies the band; all three of the handoff's candidate diagnoses are ruled out (`.hcal-agenda-day` is not in this component at all — that is `/hearings`' calendar, this is `.hsch-*`; there is no agenda-head to suppress; `.hsch-wrap` declares no row-gap so the empty second column at >=1700 contributes nothing). **Nothing was changed for it.** The other hearings states were **fired, not narrated** — a scratch env-read clock in `HearingsTab`, reverted and uncommitted: TODAY mode at Wed Aug 5 renders MON/TUE dim, WED amber-active, THU/FRI full; the empty week at Oct 5 renders `NOTHING SCHEDULED` + five dashes + one dim line with no `.hsch-wrap`. **The audit did not move across 623** — `--route home` at 2560 reads **123 candidates · M1 0 (M1a 0 · M1b 0) · M1x 13 · M2 0 · M4-true 1 / 42px · M4w 0**, identical either side, falsification PASS. **Narrow was confirmed rather than assumed**, and the confirmation needed a rebuilt control: at 720 the post-623 build shows a non-tape overflow (`.hsch-row` / `.hsch-title`, `docScrollW` 739) and **the pre-623 build shows the byte-same set** — bucket (b) unchanged, pre-existing, not introduced here. **Live at ship, and the expectation was the instrument: the band names exactly two — McConnell (51 consecutive, since Jun 11, 14.1%) and Wilson (34, since Jul 16, 29.3%).** **Prod-verified converged twice** — `12b3e57` for the band (four sequential reads, byte-identical content, which is also the HO 533 serialization check: plain arrays, no Map) and `d754db3` for the fit-finish (five reads on one stylesheet hash carrying `minmax(480px,31%)`, `@container bxp (max-width:520px)` and `.hsch-cell.is-past{opacity:.42}`, with the HTML's 4 dimmed cells and 2 absence rows agreeing — no stale-HTML-new-CSS cycle). Cost as shipped: **1,064 rows returned / ~516ms cold**, one regen a day on the `votes` tag. Docs: HO 623 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 623.**

**Also (HO 624–627), the ledger ghost and the dashboard density pass — no theme-% change (624–626 are read-cost and a one-line index hint; 627 is arrangement and depth over existing measures, not a new data domain).** **HO 624 was a probe against the backlog's largest read line, and the line was a ghost.** The entry read `bills_agg` **110M rows / 26% of the 30-day budget / 317 calls at ~347k each**, and the doubt was specific: **~347k is not a `bills`-shaped number** (the corpus is ~17k) but `member_votes` was ~366k, and until HO 595 `participationAggCte()` full-scanned it **inside the very same statement text** — the per-query ledger attributes by statement, and the HO 602 window (read 2026-08-05) straddles a July fix. **The probe changed instrument, and that is what made M3 a measurement instead of an argument.** `@libsql/client` v0.14.0 does not surface per-statement `rows_read`, so it reads Turso's hrana `/v2/pipeline` directly and meters exactly what the ledger meters. **M0 calibrates it because the instrument has a trap: a bare `COUNT(*)` is answered from B-tree interior pages and under-reports by 227–416×** (`member_votes` 366,496 rows → `rows_read: 1617`; `bills` 17,463 → 42), so nothing may price a scan by bare count and every figure below uses a predicated form. **The verdict: 20,752 rows per call**, identical on both sorts, **16.7× under the ledger** — component sum 18,617 (`bills_agg` 17,463 + `part_agg` 554 + `members` 553 + `palestine` 47) plus ~2.1k of ≈4 index seeks per member row across the three LEFT JOINs. **The coincidence test is decisive and was stated as arithmetic: `|347k − member_votes 365,996| = 18,996` against `|347k − measured| = 326,248`** — the ledger figure sits within 5% of the thing HO 595 killed and 16× from the query it was billed to. **Two levers died on measurement.** The proposed `congress = 119` narrowing excludes **zero** rows (all 17,463 sponsored bills are 119, a single-congress table), and `includeCeremonial` is not one either — **both CTE variants read 17,463**, because the predicate filters rows RETURNED inside a covering scan, not rows read. Materialization was priced and declined: ~1,092 written rows to save a 17k covering scan that reads in single-digit ms. **The variant-surface inventory is the part a future ledger read needs**, because the ledger groups by statement text and there are **three**: `getMembersRanked` (filters × sort × page × pageSize — 72 combinations before state/q/page, × 56 states) · `getCommitteeRoster` (per committee, 236 × 3 × 2 = 1,416) · `searchMembers` (per query string at 600s, unbounded by construction). **And the plan check found a live defect nobody was looking for:** `searchMembers`' lean copy carried **no `INDEXED BY`** and planned onto `idx_bills_is_ceremonial` as a **non-covering MULTI-INDEX OR** — the HO 277/329 misplan class, on a request path — measured **35,129 rows / 594.6ms, 2.01× the hinted shape**, the row-fetch doubling a non-covering OR predicts. **HO 625 filed it in three hunks and one of them was a correction nobody asked for.** The QUEUED entry resolved; the **reusable rule** recorded as *date every per-query ledger read against the fixes that landed inside its window — a killed statement stays on the leaderboard for the length of the window*, which is the measured-number rule one level up (a ledger line is `f(query, corpus, window)` read as a property of the query); a **new dated OPEN LOOP** for a single dashboard glance after ~Sep 1, the first window clean of both the pre-595 statement and the HO 606–615 layout-arc crawls, carrying the standing rule **nothing gets optimized off the current ledger — its top lines are ghosts and burn events**; and a flagged ride-along correcting the HO 602 tombstone, which credited HO 595 with closing **42%** of the budget. **HO 624 makes that false: all three of the top three lines were the same ghost, so 595 actually closed ≈(110.0 + 91.3 + 85.4)/418 = 68%.** The per-call figures in that tombstone are left intact as a faithful record of the read and re-labelled as pre-fix cost inside a trailing window. **HO 626 took the hint, in one line.** Plan flipped to `SEARCH bills USING COVERING INDEX idx_bills_sponsor_agg` and the `GROUP BY` temp b-tree disappeared with it (the covering index is already ordered by `sponsor_bioguide_id`). Full-statement `rows_read` across five terms: `smith` 35,129→18,580 · `texas` 35,228→18,679 · `a` 36,594→20,045 · `johnson` 35,126→18,577 · `california` 35,268→18,719, with the hinted CTE standalone at **17,463 — exactly `billsAggCte`'s figure**, so it now lands in the hinted family. **Equivalence was proven, not assumed:** all five terms returned **identical rows, position for position**, old vs new against the same live state — a hint changes the plan and must not change the answer, and because the values are byte-identical nothing behind the 600s cache had to converge. **The currencies discipline held under temptation:** timings improved sharply (277ms → 8.8ms) and were deliberately **kept out of the commit's argument**, because the Aug 7 platform read established a dev-box timing as a known non-signal — citing it would have been the exact error the 624/625 arc was about, one HO after writing it down. **HO 627 was density, and it opened with an owner-reported defect the arc's own instruments had never been pointed at.** **Commit 0: the row is the click target, everywhere in it.** C3 had made stage the sole carrier of colour, so the amber bill id read as *the thing to click* — and clicking it left the dashboard while clicking two pixels to its right expanded. One advertised affordance, two outcomes. The id keeps its `href` (new-tab, copy-link, a11y) and its `onClick` became modifier-aware; the sponsor link had the same swallow and got the same treatment; a **leading** disclosure glyph returned (leading, because a right-anchored caret is the C1 defect HO 609 removed from this row); and the drill moved **one level in, not away** — a packed-left `→ FULL PAGE` now leads the expand panel, because the pre-627 button sat at the bottom of `.bxp-metabox` and the `@container (max-width:520px)` rule stacks the dashboard's narrow panel, pushing it below the whole summary/hearing/news/odds run. **The gate is the durable part and it amends the §1 reproduction permanently: the prior reproductions passed while the owner failed — they clicked pixels users don't. The gate now clicks the pixels users do**, taking each target's own client rect and issuing a real mouse click at its centre, from a genuinely scrolled feed, at 1440 and 2560. **It caught a real defect on its first honest run**: letting the default happen on a modified click was not enough, because the event still bubbled — ctrl-click opened the bill in a background tab **and** expanded the row behind it, the same defect mirrored (`open=true · tabs 1→2`). 16/16 after. **Two handoff premises did not survive measurement, and both were the same shape.** The handoff names the feed column `.bills` with `.feed { overflow-y: auto }`; **neither class exists** — the column is `.dash-bills` and the scroller is `.v2f` itself — and the first gate run scrolled the missing selector, scrolled nothing, and reported *"feed too short to scroll"*, which is the same-as-success shape; the harness now finds the scroller by walking up to the first genuinely-overflowing ancestor rather than trusting a class name. And a **variant-split depth looked right and was refused**: `/dashboard-classic` was removed at HO 608–610 (the route 404s) and `app/page.tsx` is the only caller of all three feed components, always `variant="v2"`, so a variant-keyed constant would have been an unreachable branch carrying a comment about a page that no longer exists. **Commits 1–3 were depth, not breadth — nothing the arc cut came back.** MOVERS and NEW 5→30, STALLS 5→60 (a **ceiling, not a target** — the stale set is 5 today, so it renders everything there is), BREAKING 5→10. **Cost was measured because the ledger discipline applies even to 25 extra feed rows:** MOVERS `rows_read` **1,018→1,094** and NEW **1,027→1,103**, +76 each — the ~1,000 baseline is the mention subquery's `news_mentions` GROUP BY, not the LIMIT, since both walks are pre-ordered index ranges that short-circuit at LIMIT either way. **Commit 3 un-tabbed the DISTRIBUTION panel to the mock**, BY STAGE over BY TOPIC, co-visible; BY TOPIC ships as a **ranked bar list** per the mock rather than the treemap, because two halves of one panel have to read as one panel and a squarified treemap beside a bar run reads as two widgets. `DashboardTopicTreemap` is unchanged and still exported with no dashboard caller (noted, not swept); `DistributionsTabs` was deleted in the commit that orphaned it; the fixed 230px `.distributions-panel-body` went with the tabs, since it existed to stop a swap jumping and would now **cap the panel at half what it renders**. Click-to-filter was verified live on **both** halves rather than assumed (`?stage=floor` → 1 selected + 5 dimmed; `?topics=healthcare` → 1 selected + 7 dimmed; `ActiveFilterStrip` in both), cross-rebase intact. **A NEW `data-viz-row` marking shipped WITH ITS CURVE, and leg B refused the crawl until it did** (live M1x 13→30). Measured void, bar-end → value: **2560 TOPIC 8–395px (median 353) against STAGE 8–488px (median 454); 1440 TOPIC 8–155px against STAGE 8–166px.** **No cap reaches it, and this is the strongest form of the exemption rather than the `FirmsLeaderboard` kind: the bar is scaled to the track, so the void is PROPORTIONAL TO THE VALUE ENCODED** — a topic at 1/8 of the max must leave 7/8 of its track empty, and capping shrinks track and bar together, preserving the proportion while making the small topics unreadable. Registry form **M1x 30 / 14 rows** (candidates, not visual rows). **The audit held where it had to:** `--route home` at 2560, falsification PASS, **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1 / 42px · M4f 0** — the P3 exit numbers unchanged; **only M1x moved, and it is decomposed above.** **The remaining left-column shortness at 2560 is recess plus C4 doing their jobs** — empty states collapsed, panels content-sized — and is explicitly not to be fixed by resurrecting anything the arc cut. **Prod-verified converged at `3d0d6d1`**: 6 stage rows, 8 topic rows, 30 feed rows, 30 disclosure glyphs, and `.bxp-head-drill` present in the served stylesheet (the HO 212 stale-`.next` trap ruled out by reading the CSS, not the HTML). Docs: HO 625 (backlog), HO 627 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 627.**

**Also (HO 628), the feed row hairline the 609 rebuild dropped — no theme-% change (one declaration restored from the committed mock; arrangement only).** The owner reported the right-rail bill rows reading cramped, and the diagnosis was **one missing declaration, not a spacing rework**. The mock's `.mv` is three things — `padding:5px 10px`, a hover background, and **`border-top:.5px solid var(--border-soft)`** (`dashboard-layout-target.html:142`, identical in `dashboard-2col.html`); the HO 609 rebuild adopted the first two and dropped the third **without a comment recording the drop as a decision**, which is how it survived three HOs. At depth 5 the omission never read. **HO 627's raise to 30 is what exposed it**: thirty borderless two-line rows at 5px padding are one undifferentiated block. Mock wins — the standing precedent, cited at 627 for this same page's distributions panel. `04f7523` puts it on the **group wrapper** via the sibling combinator, not on the row, and three things follow from that placement. The first group carries no line against the tab strip with **no `:first-child` carve** (measured: `border-top-width` 0px on group 1, 1px `rgb(17,24,32)` on group 2). `.v2f-group.open`'s amber frame overrides the grey top edge rather than fighting it — **but only by source order**, since `.v2f-group + .v2f-group` and `.v2f-group.open` are both specificity (0,2,0), so the hairline is declared ABOVE `.open` and a tidying reorder would put a grey line on the open row's top edge; the comment says so. **The open-frame interaction was verified, not assumed, because the handoff named it as the one thing that could go wrong.** The open group's `margin:-1px 0` pulls the next sibling's new hairline into the same 1px band as the amber bottom border — **measured `gapPx = -1`, exact overlap** — and `position:relative; z-index:1` against the neighbours' `auto` is what makes amber paint over it. **No sliver at 1440 or 2560, top or bottom**, so the handoff's contingency (`.v2f-group.open + .v2f-group { border-top-color: transparent }`) was not needed. **One reading worth keeping: `getComputedStyle` returns `1px`, not `0.5px`** — Chrome rounds a sub-pixel border up to one device pixel at DPR 1, which is what all ~279 fractional borders in the file already do, so it renders as the house hairline renders; but a future diff taken against the *computed* value will not find the number the declaration contains. **Commit 2 was NOT taken.** The contingency was `.v2f-row` vertical padding 5px → 7px as a recorded mock deviation if the rows still read cramped; no such ruling was given, so padding stays `5px 12px` — the mock's density is 5px WITH the hairline, and that pairing is the approved design. Gates: the pinned HO 627 click gate re-ran **16/16** at both widths from a genuinely scrolled feed (no JSX changed, but it is the pinned requirement and it is cheap); `--route home` at 2560 read **M1 0 · M1x 30 · M4-true 1/42px**, falsification PASS — a border-top opens no gap. **Prod-verified by stylesheet on the deployed hash** (the HO 212 trap, read from the CSS and not the HTML): `.v2f-group+.v2f-group{border-top:.5px solid var(--border-soft)}` present at `04f7523`. Docs: HO 628 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 628.**

**Also (HO 629), the bills column boundary moves left, and the instrument's live anchor moved with it — no theme-% change (arrangement only).** Owner ruling: **31% → 45%**, chosen off an interactive mock with a live fraction knob (candidate set 31/36/40/42/45) rather than estimated from stills. Not a side flip — the J-revision arrangement stands. `78f5c78`, measured on the local production build, **every figure net of a 15px scrollbar, which is why each lands slightly under the handoff's estimate**: column **779 → 1130px at 2560** (the boundary moves 351px left), 729 → 1058 at 2400, **480 → 626 at 1440** (45% now binds; the 480 floor goes dormant above ~1067 viewport and stays as-is). Left region 1372px at 2560 with two-up panels 816/544, and 756px at 1440 with 446/297 — the compression the ruling accepted. **The gain is real and partial, and it was measured on ONE build and ONE data state** by injecting the old track so the fraction is the only variable: truncated movers titles **18/30 → 14/30 at 2560**, 18/30 → 15/30 at 2400, 20/30 → 18/30 at 1440; what still truncates is genuinely long titles, not a width failure. **Breakage was checked rather than assumed** — real horizontal overflow in the left region is **0 at both widths** (authored ellipsis and scroll excluded) and `documentElement.scrollWidth` equals the viewport at both, so the handoff's HALT-if-a-panel-breaks condition did not fire. **The ~850px C8 ceiling is RETIRED, not re-justified.** C8's scope is rows carrying a right-hand element — its stated mechanism is that extra width pushes that element further away, which is C1 again. This row has none: HO 609 removed the pinned metric and caret, HO 627's disclosure glyph is leading. The title is packed-left `nowrap` + `ellipsis`, so added width converts directly into visible characters and a short title ends mid-row with nothing anchored past it. The ceiling was **never tested at 623** (both values sat under it) and is false for a truncating row. **The derived bxp check reached the handoff's conclusion and falsified its reasoning.** The threshold stays **520**, but not because "wide down to ~1290, stacked in a narrow band below that". Measured: `.bxp` carries 16px horizontal padding and an `inline-size` query resolves against the **content box**, so 1440 reads outer 598 / **content 566** (chrome **60px, re-measured rather than reused from 623**), and the flip sits at **viewport 604 STACKED / 605 WIDE** — content 520 vs 521, exactly the threshold. **The band the handoff predicted is empty**: the two-column grid only exists at `min-width:1400`, and 1400 already yields content **548 > 520**, while at 1399 the grid has collapsed and the panel is 1291 wide. **So the dashboard panel is WIDE at every viewport ≥605 and the stacked branch is now mobile-only** — a behaviour change at 1440, which used to stack. `/bills` confirmed with one measurement rather than re-derived: **1140px, WIDE**. One consequence recorded so it is not re-filed as a defect: HO 627 moved `→ FULL PAGE` into the panel head partly *because* the container query stacked the dashboard's narrow panel, and that justification is now historical at desktop widths; the change stands on its own. **The layout audit halted, the instrument was RIGHT, and the ANCHOR was wrong — `83a29fa` re-anchors it, riding the commit that invalidated it (the HO 612 discipline).** Leg B asserted `live M1x === 30`; M1x counts **candidates**, and candidacy is width-gated (`m1bMinWidth` 600), so narrowing the left region dropped the widest node under `[data-viz-row]` from **659px to 518px** and M1x fell **30 → 0 with the marking untouched**. **Checked before touching the assertion** (HO 615's rule): at 2560 on one build the attribute reaches the same **2 nodes** (`ul.stage-funnel`, `ul.topic-dist`) and the same **86 visible nodes** under them at 45% and at an injected 31%. Nothing rotted. **The sharper reason to replace it rather than retune it: the old gate had stopped discriminating** — at 45% it read FAIL whether the marking was intact or deleted, so it could no longer separate either of its two failure modes from a legitimate resize, which is the same-as-success shape arriving through a threshold. Leg B's live half now asserts what the attribute **reaches** (marked-node count and identities, width-invariant); M1x is still printed beside it as the registry figure, but as an observation, not a gate. **Both directions were fired before it shipped**, scratch only, reverted, components clean: marking stripped from `StageFunnel` → reaches `ul.topic-dist` → FAIL; marking added to `div.v2f` → reaches 3 nodes, M1x **157** → FAIL; PASS restored on the reverted build. **Both dashboard arms MEASURED, not derived** (the HO 627 precedent): `--route home` and `--route home-stage-committee` each read **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1/42px · M1x 0**, falsification PASS. The exemption registry is restated in the backlog — **site M1x 366 → 306** — with the visual-row count unchanged at 131, which is the whole point: the marking is **dormant at this width, not gone**. The click gate re-ran **16/16** at both widths (row geometry changed). **Prod-verified by stylesheet on the deployed hash**: `minmax(0,1fr) minmax(480px,45%)` and `@container bxp (max-width:520px)` in the served CSS at `83a29fa`, HO 628's hairline still present alongside. **The mock is committed here, and the trace of its absence is kept rather than tidied away: `docs/design/mock-629-bills-boundary.html` was ABSENT FROM DISK at close time on 2026-08-07** — not in `docs/design/`, not in `~/Downloads`, and named as absent rather than assumed lost — and the owner dropped it in the amendment pass before this block landed, so it ships in this commit. It is the interactive one: a `--bills-frac` knob over the 31/36/40/42/45 candidate set with a live viewport/column/inner/mode readout, which is what makes "chosen off the mock, not estimated from stills" a checkable claim rather than a recollection. **The mock is the ruling's record; this block is its reading.** Docs: HO 629 (this block) + the exemption-registry restatement (backlog). **Also notes now run through HO 629.**

**Also (HO 630), Absence Watch expands the member card in place and navigation becomes opt-in — no theme-% change (an existing card reused on an existing band; arrangement plus a prefetch).** Owner ruling, the same product logic as HO 627's: **the drill moves one level in, not away.** The band wrapped each whole row in a `<Link>`, so any click left the dashboard; now a plain click expands the member's card under the row and the member page is opt-in — modifier-click on the name, or the card's own drill. `SponsorExpandedPanel` is reused **verbatim**, no absence-specific variant. **The /members data path does not transfer, and that is the design:** that surface expands by URL state and server-renders the selected member, which IS navigation — the defect. `abd3b63` prefetches instead. `getMemberCardExpansion` is extracted from an inlined IIFE in `app/members/page.tsx` to `lib/queries` (this HO makes it two-consumer, the promotion bar) and **deliberately carries no cache of its own** — each of its five parts keeps its own key/TTL/tag, so a `bills` flush still reaches stats/topics/recent-bills and a `committees` flush still reaches the roster; a sixth entry would have an invalidation contract that is the intersection of five different tags. Assembly rides inside the cached `getAbsenceWatch`; the degradation guard stays outside the cache boundary, untouched. **The degradation rule is PER MEMBER and its failure mode is named:** a card-assembly failure degrades THAT member to identity-only (`card: null`) and the row still renders from the base query, without an expand affordance — a row that advertises an affordance it cannot honor is the 627 defect again. One try/catch around the loop would let one bad member cost every card; hoisting it further would let it cost the band, and "card data missing" collapsing into "nobody is absent" is the HO 622 silent-wrong-answer one level up, on a surface where empty is the good-news state that renders nothing. **Two structural constraints decided the shape of `e1e49e0` and both are load-bearing.** `SponsorExpandedPanel` is a SERVER component and a client island cannot import one, so the server band renders each panel and passes it into the island as a `ReactNode` — the `ActivityTabs` idiom, and what keeps `lib/queries` (and with it `next/cache`) out of the client bundle. And because the band keeps `formatSince`, **the island holds no clock at all**: the HO 574/589 constraint is satisfied by construction rather than by discipline, and the grep over `AbsenceWatchRows.tsx` returns zero lines. Row: `<Link>` → `div role=button tabIndex=0 aria-expanded` with Enter/Space; the name stays a real `<a href>` with a modifier-aware `onClick` whose `stopPropagation` is **not optional** — without it a ctrl-click opens the tab AND expands the row behind it, the HO 627 mirrored defect. Leading disclosure glyph, never far-right. **The head-drill was MEASURED into existence rather than added on principle** — the handoff made it conditional on where the card's own buttons land, and `View detail →` sits **447px down at 2560 and 402px at 1440**, ~85% of a 482–536px card, before the stack pushes it further; `→ Full page` now leads at **3px / 7px**. **Commit 3's threshold is measured, and the handoff's stated failure mode was the WRONG INSTRUMENT.** It named "committee names wrapping unreadably"; measured, they **never wrap at any width** (0/14 from 1370px down to 560px) because they truncate instead. So the curve reads truncation, anchored against the pane the card was designed for rather than a bare pixel count: **/members gives it 1073px, columns 210/518/345, 12/14 committees truncated, worst name 30% visible.** The band's wrapper: **1370 → 9/14 / 41%** (the band at 2560 — *better than the reference*) · 1200 → 11/14 / 33% · **1050 → 13/14 / 26%** (parity with the reference) · 950 → 14/14 / 21% (every name now cut) · **754 → 14/14 / 12%** (the 1440 floor). **1050px** is therefore where the band's card reaches the readability of the surface it was built for — above it the band is no worse than /members already is, below it the card degrades past any existing surface. A **container** query, not a media query, deliberately: the band's width is a function of the dashboard grid's fraction, and HO 629 moved that fraction one commit earlier, so a media threshold would have silently changed meaning with it. Result: three columns at 2560 (card 577px tall), one 704px column at 1440 (522 → 1245px tall). Scoped to the band's mount per the HO 507 rule, and `/members` **verified byte-identical rather than asserted** — card width, all three column widths, truncation count and worst-visible percentage, at both widths either side of the change. **Gates.** The click gate reads **30/30**: the 16 HO 627 feed legs pass **untouched** and 14 absence legs join them (7 per width). **No scroll leg, dropped deliberately and said so in the harness** — the band sits at the top of the page, so the client-rect-vs-page-position divergence the feed legs exist to catch cannot arise. **The ctrl-click leg was proven to fire before it shipped** (stopPropagation removed in scratch reproduced `open=true · tabs 1→2` at both widths, the exact HO 627 signature; restored, 30/30) — and it **caught a weakness in its own first draft**: it asserted only that *a* tab opened and printed "no /members tab" while passing, a leg that proved less than it printed, so it now waits for the destination URL. Audit `--route home` at 2560: **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1/42px**, falsification PASS, element count 1755 → 1780. **The handoff's guard-2 expectation of M1x 30 was already superseded by HO 629 one commit earlier** — it reads 0 and 629's block records why; the crawl does not open panels, so the card's interior is inherited from /members' P4-worked hygiene and is not re-scored here. **Cost, priced in ROWS per the ledger discipline: 236 rows per `getAbsenceWatch` regeneration** for today's 2-member band (M000355 106, W000808 128, plus one batched palestine read of 2) — paid on regeneration, not per request, and **never per expand**, which is a client toggle over prefetched props. Linear in band membership, which `ABSENCE_STREAK_MIN = 30` bounds to a handful. The real cost is PAYLOAD: both panels serialize into the RSC flight data on every dashboard render (650,209-byte document at 2 members), which is exactly what buys the zero-fetch toggle; neither is in the DOM until opened. **Reconciling the probe's verbatim SQL against the shipped helpers corrected two paraphrases that would have UNDERSTATED it** — `getSponsorRecentBills` has NO LIMIT (the 7-row cap is render-only in the panel) and `getSponsorTopTopics` counts in JS over the full raw row set rather than aggregating in SQL. **Prod-verified converged at `dc79474`**: four sequential reads with byte-identical band markup (2 rows / 2 glyphs / 2 panels) and **zero `class="abw-row" href`**, which is the positive proof the whole-row link is gone rather than an absence read as success; the served stylesheet carries `@container abwcard (max-width:1050px)`, `container:abwcard/inline-size` and `.abw-drill`. Docs: HO 630 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 630.**

**Also (HO 631), the absence card gets a compact interior, stops reshaping the page, and its bound moves to its bottom edge — no theme-% change (an existing card re-densified on an existing band; arrangement plus a payload bound).** **Owner rulings, both made off `docs/design/mock-631-absence-card-overlay.html` — the live one, with a variant knob and a working overlay, not stills: COMPACT interior, OVERLAY over the page, click-outside-closes.** The mock rides this commit as the ruling's record (the HO 629 precedent); **its Wilson dataset is illustrative, and that mattered — see below.** **Commit 1 (`78f12ee`) made the density OPT-IN rather than hoping overrides do not leak**: `SponsorExpandedPanel` grows `density?: "full" | "compact"` defaulting to `"full"`, `/members` passes nothing, and the HO 507 rule is satisfied by the explicit prop. Compact is photo 56px inline with the name block, bills and committees capped 10, committees two-column collapsing under HO 630's container threshold (which does not move — the container width is unchanged by this HO), both columns closing with the house `[ + N MORE → ]`. **`/members` was RENDER-DIFFED, not asserted, and the diff earned its keep on a detail no reasoning would have reached:** the full branch keeps its original three-child JSX shape rather than one interpolated string, because **React emits text-segment markers between adjacent children**, so collapsing them into a template literal changes the served HTML — visually identical, no longer byte-identical, which is the gate. **The count-source fix landed HERE, one commit before the slice that needs it.** The bills see-all gated on `recentBills.length` while printing `stats.total` in the same label — two different predicates (the array is `summary IS NOT NULL`-gated, the total is not), so the panel was already incoherent; more to the point, gating on array length means commit 3's slice would have silently killed `[ + N MORE → ]` **exactly when it applies.** **Commit 2 (`56daf37`) is the overlay, and its stacking claim was reasoned and then verified rather than asserted:** the pop can only overlap the band's LATER SIBLINGS in the left region — all `z-index: auto` — so a modest z-index beats paint order, and it never reaches the sticky bills rail **by geometry, not by z-order** (`left:0; right:0` of a band that ends at the column gap gives it no width in that column to paint into). Esc closes and returns focus to the row; an outside click closes AND the clicked target's own behaviour proceeds — one click, not two. **The zero-reflow instrument discriminates, and its falsification came FREE:** rect-top of the element after the band, closed vs open, reads **0px at both 1440 and 2560**, where the same measurement read **~523px at 2560** before the change — so no scratch edit was needed to prove the leg can fail, the arc's own prior state supplied the nonzero reading. A leg that only asserted "open" was equally green either side of the change and proved nothing. **Commit 3 (`fa87cdf`) closes the QUEUED line, and its saving lands somewhere other than where the handoff looked.** The handoff asked for a served-document re-measure against HO 630's 650,209-byte baseline; **measured, the document does not move, and the reason is structural rather than a failed slice** — the card is a SERVER component, so only its RENDERED output serializes, and commit 1 already caps the render at 10. **Confirmed by ID, not by byte count** (`119-s-476`, `119-hr-5868` absent from the pre-slice document), because a byte total can coincide. What the slice actually removes is the tail of the value `getAbsenceWatch` **caches**: **16 rows, 15,481 bytes, 44% of the bills payload** — and because the Data Cache survives a deploy, that saving lands on the next regeneration, not at promotion. **The handoff's own figure for this was wrong, and the correction is the durable half.** It predicted "12→10 and 9→9 at the current band" and called the saving "small … the point is the bound, not the bytes." Live: 12→10 (cut 2) and Wilson **24**→10 (cut 14). **The 9→9 was the MOCK's illustrative Wilson dataset read as an expectation about real data** — the owner named it his error and the **second of its class**, and extended the policy accordingly: **illustrative mock data never sets a numeric expectation for a build.** The saving is not small; it is 44%. **Commit 4 (`707b94a`) is the HALT-ruled fold bound, and it exists because a viewport FRACTION is not a viewport BOUND.** `min(72vh, 760px)` bounded the card's HEIGHT while knowing nothing about where the card STARTS; the pop hangs at `top:100%` of a band sitting below the whole header stack, so 72vh of 900 put the bottom at **992.5 against a 900px fold — 92.5px below it**, reachable only by scrolling the page, which is the page-growth commit 2 removed re-entered through the bottom edge. `calc(100vh - 360px)` subtracts the origin first, so the bound is on the BOTTOM. **The constant is measured, and the first measurement was wrong in a way worth recording:** written against 1440 alone it read "345 + buffer, width-invariant". Measured across three viewports the band-bottom offset is **344.5 (1440×900) · 352 (1920×1080) · 346.5 (2560×1400)** — a 7.5px spread, because the header stack's nav wraps differently at 1920, so **the widest viewport is not the worst case; 1920 is.** 360 is sized against that worst origin (8px clearance there, 15.5px at 1440), and the comment now carries the range and the coupling rather than the one number, because a constant derived from one width silently mis-sizes at another. All three terms earn their place: the fold term wins at 1440 (540px) and 1920 (720px), the 760px cap still wins at 2560×1400 where the fold term is **idle and this commit changes nothing** — which the probe reports as N/A rather than counting as a pass, since **a green reading must distinguish "the fix works" from "the defect never reproduced here"** (it recomputes the OLD bound's bottom from the same measured origin to do so). **A stale server nearly measured the wrong build, and the catch generalises the HO 212 trap one layer.** A `next start` from the previous evening was still listening on :3000 and answered 200; its stylesheet carried `abw-card-wrap` and `abw-row` but **zero `abw-card-pop`** — a pre-commit-2 build serving the state the arc had already left, and every figure taken from it would have read plausibly. Not a 404'd stylesheet but a **200 stylesheet from a stale build**, caught by asserting the new rule's presence in the SERVED CSS before trusting a single measurement. **Gates.** Click gate **36/36** — the 16 feed legs untouched, 20 absence legs (10 per width), the three new ones being Esc-closes-with-focus-return, outside-click-closes-**and**-the-target-still-acts (both halves asserted; a leg that only checked the close would prove less than it prints), and open-A-click-B-switches. Audit `--route home` at 2560, **closed state**: **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1/42px · M4f 0 · M1x 0**, falsification PASS, elements 1780 → 1791. **Scoped honestly: the crawl does not open the pop, so the overlay's open state is not audited** — the card's interior is inherited from `/members`' P4-worked hygiene. **Prod-verified converged at `707b94a`**: the served stylesheet carries `max-height:min(72vh,760px,100vh - 360px)` — the minifier drops the redundant `calc()` inside `min()`, which is valid and is byte-identical to the build the probe measured (same CSS hash locally and on prod) — and the fold probe re-run **against prod** returns the identical origins, computed max-heights and above-fold verdicts at all three viewports. Docs: HO 631 (this block) + the QUEUED tombstone (backlog). **Also notes now run through HO 631.**

**Also (HO 632), MIA, feed day-groups, and the cards go quiet — no theme-% change (display copy, arrangement, and subtraction; no new data domain).** **Three rulings off `docs/design/mock-632-quiet-cards-and-groups.html`, revised twice in review (group headers punched to amber-bright with the tape's 2px left edge; the band renamed), and a FOURTH ruled item that turned out to need no code.** The mock rides this commit as the ruling's record. **Its arrival is itself the durable half: after the browser-to-disk hop failed three times, the mock shipped THROUGH THE HANDOFF CHANNEL as a fenced block in `632-mock-delivery.md` carrying a declared md5 and byte size, to be extracted, verified, and the wrapper deleted.** Extraction is by exact bytes between the fences and the checksum discriminates: the trailing-newline candidate read `2a49e8d8…`, the correct one `7989929c17a09b48b14c1a4c6cbcac56` at **27377 B**, confirmed independently with `md5sum` before a line of code was written. **That is now the standing mechanism for mock transport** — a mock too large or too fragile for the browser hop travels as a checksummed `.md` delivery, is verified before use, and the wrapper is deleted as transport rather than kept as record. **C1 — the band is MIA on screen, and the handoff's own caution about its grep was right: there are TWO sites, not one.** The visible title AND `aria-label="Absence watch"` on the section; renaming only the first leaves a screen reader announcing the old name over a band that reads MIA. They now differ ON PURPOSE — visible `▲ MIA` (terse chrome; the ▲ carries the alarm and C3 wants one bright element, not a restated one), accessible `MIA: absence watch` (keeping the context a sighted reader gets free from the band's position). "MIA" alone would announce an initialism stripped of what makes it legible, a downgrade aimed at the reader who can least afford one; the file says so, so a later consistency pass does not collapse the two.
