The FEATURE keeps its name everywhere else — roadmap, backlog, helpers, filenames. **C2 — TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK on MOVERS and NEW, and it REVERSES HO 609's refusal with the provenance kept rather than overwritten.** 609 refused feed groups on a sound rule — the committed mock had none (its TODAY/YESTERDAY strings belonged to the hearings day bar) and the mock wins. **What changed is the mock, not the rule**, so the same precedent now requires them; the comment asserting the refusal is replaced, because a comment stating the opposite of the shipped state is its own defect. **The bucket field is PARAMETERIZED BY MODE, because the rule "group and age from the same timestamp" has no single-field answer**: MOVERS reads `stage_changed_at`, NEW `introduced_date`, STALLS `latest_action_date` — and STALLS is ungrouped by design, since it is ordered by stall age and a TODAY header over it is a category error. **The two grouped fields do not store the same SHAPE, and this is the HO 622 trap in a new place**: `stage_changed_at` is an ISO instant in UTC (convert to ET), `introduced_date` is DATE-ONLY (slice, never parse — `new Date('2026-08-06')` is UTC midnight, i.e. 2026-08-05 20:00 ET, backdating every row of that feed by one). One rule over both silently breaks whichever loses. **ET is measurable rather than principled: 5 of 30 MOVERS rows (17%) land on a different calendar day under UTC bucketing.** ET-today is derived ONCE from the drilled `nowMs` and shared by both paths, so the two feeds cannot disagree about when today started; nothing reads a clock (every `Date` is built from an argument), so SSR and hydration bucket identically and 574/589 holds by construction. **Measured distribution at close: MOVERS TODAY 12 · YESTERDAY 18 · EARLIER 0 · OLDER 0; NEW TODAY 0 · YESTERDAY 3 · EARLIER 27 · OLDER 0.** **OLDER renders nothing today and is NOT vestigial — its population is a function of the WEEKDAY, not corpus depth**, because both feeds are 7-day rolling windows while the ISO week is Monday-anchored: late in the week nearly the whole window sits inside the current week, while on a Monday up to six of seven days fall before the week start and OLDER carries most of the feed. **A Monday screenshot showing a full OLDER group is correct behaviour, not a regression** — recorded here so it is not filed as one. Empty buckets render nothing (a header over no rows is C4's reserved box wearing a label). **The seam needed no override, verified rather than reasoned**: the 628 hairline is `.v2f-group + .v2f-group`, a header sits between two groups as their sibling, so the adjacency does not match across it and the header's own border-top takes over — measured `border-top: 0px` on every post-header group at both widths. **The mock ships a defensive `.grp + .v2f-group{border-top:none}` and it was deliberately NOT carried**: it is genuinely redundant here, and a no-op override is indistinguishable from a load-bearing one to the next reader. **C3 — the metabox and the compact card's buttons go quiet, by SUBTRACTION.** `.bxp-metabox` loses background, border, radius and padding and nothing replaces them, because `.bxp-mrow` already carried `border-bottom: 1px solid var(--border-strong)` — the hairline-separated rows the mock's quiet variant draws are what the panel had all along, wearing a box. Row padding and separator weight were out of the ruling and are untouched. **Global, not dashboard-scoped, and that is the ruling**: the panel is shared with `/bills`, so a dashboard-only fork would put two treatments on one panel showing the same rows on two surfaces — the pre-316 chip problem in reverse; `/bills` rides it deliberately and was verified, not assumed. The last boxed button becomes a plain amber `→ Congress.gov ↗` link (`.bxp-btn--secondary` had exactly one consumer since 627 moved the primary to `.bxp-head`), the two glyphs kept because they mean different things — one marks the link, the other that it leaves the site. The compact card's stacked buttons become plain amber links under `density="compact"` ONLY; `/members` renders full density, where no ruling was made, and keeps its boxes. **The arrow MOVES rather than doubling** — the boxed labels already end in a trailing →, so the compact label drops it; writing both renders "→ View detail →". **Topic chips are unchanged in both variants** (they are links; HO 619 keeps their borders). **THE FOURTH ITEM WAS RULED YES AND SHIPPED NO CODE, and the error was the review's, not the handoff's.** Stage colour on `.v2f-stage` was ruled YES — and was **already live**, landed at `938f0ca` (HO 609, the same rebuild whose comment refused feed groups): the `STAGE_COLOR` map already carried dim INTRO/CMTE, `--stage-floor`, `--stage-enacted`, **and** the `other_chamber`/`president` extension the handoff flagged as its own new generalization and offered to revert. **The verification that missed it looked at the wrong LAYER** — it searched CSS for stage-keyed classes and found none, while the shipped rule is an inline `style` off a map in JSX. **Now policy, in three clauses: a style-presence check covers CSS and inline JSX BOTH; select by FUNCTION, not by an assumed class name; and an EMPTY SELECTION FAILS rather than reporting absence.** The commit was dropped; a pure inline-to-class refactor with zero visual delta was declined as not what the ruling asked for. **The same policy earned itself twice more inside this HO's own verification, which is why it is stated as policy rather than as an anecdote.** **First:** the groups probe located tabs with an unscoped `button` locator, and `.first()` matched the WeeklyBand's "NEW BILLS" metric earlier in the DOM — the NEW tab never switched, every NEW assertion re-measured MOVERS, and it reported PASS. It was visible ONLY because the two distributions happen to differ. The probe now scopes to the tablist AND asserts `aria-current` landed before trusting a reading; NEW then read YESTERDAY:3 · EARLIER:27, matching the independent DB-side recon exactly. **Second:** the chip check selected `.topic-chip`, matched nothing, and printed `n/a` **while passing** — those chips carry no class at all, their border being an inline `border: 1px solid ${topicColor}`, the identical blind spot that hid the stage colouring. Re-selected by href it reads 3 chips at 1px, so the HO 619 constant is verified rather than eyeballed. **`/members` byte-identity held, and the diff had to be READ rather than glanced at**: the whole page is NOT identical across the change — the committee rail's activity-bar widths and member counts moved (236 rows either side, live `recent_count` drift picked up when the rebuild reset the data cache) and the stylesheet hash changed because globals.css did. Both are attributable and neither is the panel. Isolated, the sponsor panel is identical on both sampled members across a 40k-char window from `.sponsor-card-grid`, the button block character-for-character, still carrying `sponsor-card-btn--amber` and its trailing arrow with no compact marker — so the conditional label introduced none of the text-segment markers that broke byte-identity at HO 631. **Gates:** click gate **36/36** at both widths across all three commits (headers are inert — no handler, no role, no tabIndex — and row discovery was unaffected); groups probe 14/14; quiet probe 6/6, asserting COMPUTED style rather than class names, since a renamed class with the box still applied would pass a markup check and fail the eye. Audit `--route home` at 2560: **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1/42px · M4f 0 · M1x 0**, falsification PASS, **M3 unchanged at 5.18** — the handoff anticipated M3 movement from the stage-colour commit, and with that commit dropped there is none — and els 1791 → **1793**, exactly the two headers on the default tab. **Prod-verified converged at `f077a4f`**: `▲ MIA` and `aria-label="MIA: absence watch"` in the served HTML with zero occurrences of the old visible string; TODAY/YESTERDAY headers stable across three sequential reads; and in the served stylesheet `.bxp-metabox{align-self:start}` (the box gone, the two surviving `.bxp-metabox` rules being pre-existing responsive margins), `.bxp-mrow` still carrying its separators, both `.bxp-golink` and `.sponsor-card-link`, `.v2f-grp` verbatim from the mock — **weight included, and that clause is true of `f077a4f` and stops being true one commit later; C4 below moves it** — and **zero** occurrences of the seam no-op. **THE VERDICT CAME BACK AS A HALT TWICE BEFORE IT CAME BACK AS A VERDICT, and it is recorded verbatim with its provenance rather than paraphrased.** Given in the DESIGN CHAT, against the LIVE PROD SCREENSHOT of `f077a4f`: *"looks good but can be scale back the bold text."* That reads as approval of the three shipped rulings plus one adjustment, and the element it names is unambiguous — the only thing this arc made bold is `.v2f-grp`, which the mock sets to 600. **C4 (`805b2aa`) is that adjustment: 600 → 400, one declaration, nothing else touched.** **The mock is NOT edited and stays at 600.** It is the record of what was *ruled*; the adjustment was made afterwards off the *rendered surface*, which is the HO 623 precedent (the feed column widened 400→480 by an owner ruling with the mock deviation recorded rather than back-fitted). **A mock is a record of a ruling, not a claim about pixels** — so when the two disagree after the owner has seen the render, the render wins and the mock keeps the history, and the deviation lives in the CSS rule's own comment where the next reader of that rule meets it rather than in an artifact they may never open. **Amber-bright, the .18em tracking, the 8px 12px 5px padding and the 2px left edge all stay verbatim from the mock, deliberately**: one variable moved, so the next screenshot attributes what it shows to the weight and to nothing else. **Verified COMPUTED rather than authored** — a rule can be present in the stylesheet and lose the cascade — **16/16 at both widths**, the weight asserted AND each of the four invariants pinned separately, because a leg that checks only the weight is equally green on a build that also nudged the colour; an empty selection FAILS rather than printing absence, this HO's own policy applied to its own last commit. Harnesses re-run **whole**, against a fresh build on a killed-and-restarted server (the HO 631 stale-server trap, and one was in fact listening): groups **14/14**, quiet **6/6**, click gate **36/36**. Audit `--route home` at 2560, closed state, matches the main baselines exactly — **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1/42px · M4f 0 · M1x 0 · M3 5.18**, falsification PASS — while **els reads 1795 against 1793, reported rather than smoothed**: a font-weight cannot add a node, and the feed's buckets have rolled a day (MOVERS now YESTERDAY 12 · EARLIER 18), so that is live-data drift of exactly the kind this block already names for `/members`. **Prod-verified converged at `805b2aa`**: the served stylesheet is the same hash and the same md5 as the build the probe measured, carries `.v2f-grp{…font-weight:400}`, and contains **zero** `.v2f-grp` rules at 600. **THE ARIA STRING STANDS AT `MIA: absence watch` BY PRE-RULED DEFAULT, and it is filed as a default rather than as a ruling.** It was put to the owner twice and came back silent both times; the relay carried a pre-ruled fallback for exactly that case, so C1's deliberate split survives untouched — visible `▲ MIA`, accessible `MIA: absence watch`. Recorded so a later reader does not mistake an unexercised default for a positive decision, and so the divergence is not collapsed by a consistency pass on the strength of a silence. Docs: HO 632 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 632.**

**Also (HO 633), the sans face is IBM Plex Sans, self-hosted, and the HO 257 reversal is finally on the record — no theme-% change (one token, zero consumer edits, no new data domain).** **Owner ruling off `docs/design/mock-633-sans-specimen.html`, a four-candidate face knob driven on the REAL surfaces rather than on stills**, which is why no per-route ruling was needed: the specimen showed the representative surfaces and `--sans` is the seam, so every consumer moves together by construction. The mock rides this commit as the ruling's record. **It is the SECOND use of the HO 632 checksummed-delivery mechanism and it worked first time** — extracted from `633-mock-delivery.md`, verified independently at `c9b081e06acbde420cb103e2ca363d6d` / **12,571 B** against the declared pair before a line of code was written, wrapper deleted as transport. A mechanism that survives its second use on a different mock is a mechanism rather than an anecdote. **THE CHECKSUM IS THE BLOB'S, NOT THE WORKING COPY'S, AND THAT DISTINCTION IS ALREADY LIVE ON 632's MOCK — re-verify with `git cat-file -p <ref>:<path> | md5sum`, never `md5sum` on the file.** `core.autocrlf` is `true` here and the repo carries no `.gitattributes`, so an LF blob checks out as CRLF: `mock-632-quiet-cards-and-groups.html` reads `7989929c…` as a blob — matching the checksum its own block declares — and `d2bc16c7…` on disk. **A future reader re-checksumming either mock the obvious way gets a mismatch and would reasonably conclude the record was tampered with**, which is a self-inflicted false alarm on a mechanism whose whole purpose is to make tampering detectable. Named here rather than in 632's block, which is append-only and was correct when written. **THE RULING SUPERSEDES HO 257's "CBT has no `--sans`", and the interesting part is that the rule had already been reversed SILENTLY by the 609-era title work** — so for several arcs the codebase shipped a sans while two live `globals.css` comments asserted it did not, each using the false rule as the stated justification for a mono choice that was itself correct. **Commit 2 (`2c344b5`) corrected both comments; commit 3 (`32cf791`) corrects the SKILL.** The wiring keeps the token as the seam: `variable` on `<html>`, `globals.css:106` becomes `--sans: var(--font-plex-sans), system-ui, …` with **the old system stack DEMOTED TO FALLBACK rather than deleted**, so a face that fails to load degrades to what shipped yesterday instead of to a serif. **WEIGHTS ARE 400 / 500 / 600, MEASURED — and the handoff's expectation of 400 + 700 was wrong in a way that matters more than the number.** **A static grep could not have answered it either**: 10 of the 12 rules that set `font-family: var(--sans)` set **no weight at all**, so their weight arrives by inheritance or off a Tailwind class on the element, and the authoritative reading is therefore the **COMPUTED weight of every element the browser actually paints in the sans stack, across the routes that use it** — 400 (titles, prose, sponsor names), 500 (`/bills` and `/patterns` row titles), 600 (sponsor brackets, absence-card names), with **no 700 consumer anywhere**. The asymmetry is why it was worth measuring rather than guessing: loading a weight nothing uses is payload on every page, but **missing one something uses is worse, because the browser synthesises it — and a faux-bold is precisely what a specimen sign-off cannot catch.** **THIS IS THE THIRD INSTANCE OF ITS CLASS AND THE RULE SHARPENS, AS PRE-DIRECTED.** The owner named the second at HO 631 — the mock's illustrative Wilson dataset read as an expectation about real data — and set the policy *illustrative mock data never sets a numeric expectation for a build*. HO 633's source is one step further out: the expectation came from **the specimen's own class declarations**, not its data. So the sharpened form: **a mock or specimen is evidence about ITSELF — its data and its own style declarations alike — never about the product**; and where the product's own source cannot answer either, as here, the authority is the computed value on the rendered surfaces rather than anything readable statically. **PAYLOAD, AND A FINDING THE LEDGER HABIT SURFACED: the payload does not scale with the weight count, because Google now serves Plex Sans as a VARIABLE font.** The three weight blocks share ONE identical six-file set (`font-stretch:100%`, the same woff2 across all three), so the on-disk cost is **137,108 B across 6 unicode-range subsets regardless of whether one weight is loaded or three** — of which English text fetches **exactly one, the 40,240 B latin file**, confirmed on the network log as a single same-origin request. The built CSS carries **19 `@font-face` blocks: 18 real (3 × 6 subsets) plus a metric-adjusted `IBM Plex Sans Fallback`** (local Arial, `ascent-override: 101.32%`, `size-adjust: 101.17%`) ahead of the system stack, so the `display: swap` flash is metric-matched rather than a reflow. **C1b RESTORED NOTHING, AND THE CORRECTED STORY IS THE DURABLE HALF.** C1b was scoped to restore a preload that next/font "emitted no preload" for, with a candidate cause of variable-class-only wiring skipping the injection. **The candidate dies on the first artifact** — the loader emits the latin subset as `*.p.woff2` at 40,240 B regardless of `variable` vs `className`, so the request was honoured and the break is downstream of it. **Prod carried the preload all along.** The break is in Next, is **WINDOWS-ONLY**, and is mechanical: `NextFontManifestPlugin` finds the font module with `mod.request.includes('/next-font-loader/index.js?')` — a forward-slash literal — while the loader path is assembled with `path.join`, which is backslash-separated on Windows; the predicate cannot match, `next-font-manifest.json` ships as `{"pages":{},"app":{}}`, `getPreloadableFonts` returns null, nothing preloads. Vercel builds on Linux. **Both fallback numbers, 400 kbps / 150 ms, 3 runs each, identical source both sides:** local build — woff2 starts **3240 / 3278 / 4070 ms**, *after* the CSS that discovers it, swap window **+2798 / +2783 / +2776 ms**; prod — woff2 starts **360 / 422 / 387 ms**, ~1.9 s *before* the CSS finishes, swap window **+477 / +543 / +700 ms**. **So the expectation that the 2,797 ms would shrink toward the unthrottled case was already true, and prod was never the slow case: 2,797 ms was a local-build artifact.** Unthrottled there is no flash either way — the font completes before FCP (local −11 / −13 / −15 ms, prod −74 / −76 / −142 ms). **The confound reads the right way round**, which is what makes the comparison usable across two different targets: prod's FCP is *later* than local's while its font starts ~2.9 s *earlier*, so the gain is the preload and not a faster link. **Reproducing the recorded 2,797 to within a millisecond is what separated instrument-sound from target-wrong** — had the re-measurement disagreed with the record, the instrument would have been the suspect and the real finding would have stayed buried. Landed as `18f6e68`: a comment in `app/layout.tsx` carrying the mechanism, the numbers with their date, and an explicit **fence against hand-rolling a `<link rel="preload">`**, which would double the preload on prod to paper over a build that is not the product. **THE LAYER-CHECK POLICY GAINS A FOURTH CLAUSE, from this arc rather than from 632's:** *presence of a runtime-injected element is a RENDERED-DOM question* — React streaming inserts the font preload from a flight `HL` hint, so **the served HTML reads identical whether the build emitted the preload or cannot emit it at all**, and a `curl` of prod would have confirmed the wrong conclusion on both sides. Only reading the `<link>` out of the rendered DOM distinguishes them. It sits alongside the three from HO 632 (CSS and inline JSX both; select by function; an empty selection fails) as the same failure in a fourth layer: the check looked where the thing is *authored* rather than where it *lands*. **Zero-external re-checked in both directions after C1b**, local and prod: exactly one font request, same-origin `/_next/static/media`, **zero off-origin requests of any kind and zero font-CDN requests** — the mock's CDN load was transport only. **RESIDUE MOVED, AND THE HANDOFF PRE-NAMED THAT IT COULD.** `/members` **13 → 9**, restated in the permanent-residue register at `ba65d5a` with the decomposition measured off `m1.rows` rather than the summary count: **7 knee-marginal `a.mc-row` at 121 / 123 / 123 / 124 / 124 / 126 / 129px + the 2 `.mc-row-hdr` at 230 / 230px** — four of the eleven knee rows crossed under threshold, the band narrowed 121–131 → 121–129, and **the header pair HELD, unmoved**. That split is the finding: the register's own claim was that the knee and the header-axis class differ in kind, and a text-metric change moving the first while unable to touch the second is that claim **arriving as a prediction that held**. **The face swap is named as CANDIDATE, not established cause** — no single-build before/after isolated it — and **the four crossed rows buy nothing forward**, having been priced as accepted residue at HO 612; whatever carried them under can carry them back over on the next face, weight or cap change. Site M1 restated **17 → 13**. **`/lobbying` is filed as a WATCH and deliberately NOT restated**, an incidental find of this arc rather than a product of it: the register carries 1, a crawl reads **3 at 215 / 179 / 129px**, and **the divergence was verified PRE-Plex, so it predates HO 633** — which is the only thing this arc is in a position to say about it. **The class attribution is the substantive half**: 215px is the `mc-row-hdr` the register already prices, while **179px and 129px are `lob-filing-row` DATA rows — a class the register never priced**, so this is not the accepted line growing and must not be folded into it. **The reviewer's candidate window (HO 597–598) is recorded as the reviewer's and as WRONG**: the 1 was priced at HO 615, re-affirmed at HO 619, and read 1 again in the terminal v4 crawl at HO 620, which **bounds the change to after 620** and outside that window. Site M1 is therefore stated as **13 with up to 2 unexplained — 15 if the WATCH attributes** — **cited, never subtracted**, so the closing identity is open on exactly one named line rather than balanced by dropping the rows that would unbalance it. **Gates:** audit `--route home` at 2560, closed state, matching the HO 632 close baselines exactly — **M1 0 · M2 0 · M4-true 1 / 42px · M4f 0 · M1x 0 · M3 5.18 · els 1795**, falsification **PASS**, so a whole-app face swap moved nothing on `/`; `--route members` run per the handoff's pre-naming, **fixture legs green FIRST at 18 of 18 exact** before any zero or any moved number was trusted; typecheck clean per commit; code, docs and SKILL never mixed. **Prod-verified live at `18f6e68`** by served SHA. **One upstream item is left as the owner's call with zero repo impact**: the Windows manifest bug is a clean `vercel/next.js` issue — mechanism, predicate line, repro all in hand — and **the layout comment protects CBT whether or not it is ever filed**. **HALT 1 ANSWERED, AND THE PRE-RULED DEFAULT WENT UNEXERCISED.** Recorded verbatim with both halves of its provenance, per the mechanism the previous arc established: given in the **DESIGN CHAT**, against a **live prod screenshot of `ba65d5a`** posted alongside — ***"good"***. **That is a live confirmation of the specimen ruling on the rendered surface, which is the one thing a specimen sign-off cannot supply**: the four-candidate knob ruled the face in the abstract, and this reads the shipped face on the real dashboard. **The relay carried a pre-ruled default for the no-verdict case** — that HALT 1 produced none, the specimen ruling (*"ibm"*) standing as the arc's only owner ruling and live confirmation declined — **and it is filed as UNEXERCISED rather than dropped**, so a later reader mistakes neither the presence of a fallback for its use nor its absence from the record for its never having existed. **The channel took one halt to get here, and that is the rule from `73d1273` working in both of its sentences**: the relay came back carrying a verdict SLOT rather than a verdict, the session halted instead of reading silence as assent, and the verdict then arrived as a quotation with named provenance. **The provenance also departed from the template it was requested under** — the relay anticipated *"typed into the Code session"* and the verdict was in fact given in the design chat against a screenshot — **so what is recorded is where it actually happened, not where it was expected to**; a provenance line back-fitted to its own template is worth nothing to the reader who later wants to check the reading rather than inherit it. Docs: HO 633 (this block). **Also notes now run through HO 633.**

**Also (HO 634), the `/lobbying` M1 divergence is attributed, priced and closed — and the closing identity balances again at site M1 15, with nothing unexplained.** The register carried `/lobbying` **1** while a crawl read **3**; the two extra rows are `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row` **data** rows, a different class from the column header the register already held. **The attribution is not a commit: the data crossed under a constant.** Neither candidate window contains the change, and the evidence is **byte identity rather than commit absence**: the `.lob-filing-row` block hashes to **md5 `bc49313d103615a3f976173dd7791770` at `62c25e4` and at `1e6435a`** — the same bytes across the whole window — with `git log 62c25e4..HEAD -- app/lobbying components/FilingRow.tsx` returning **ZERO** commits as corroboration, and the governing grid untouched since HO 614 `13203eb`. So **both windows (HO 597–598, and after-620) are retired AS RETIRED**, which is the reconciliation the WATCH asked for rather than an assumption that one of them applied. The mechanism is HO 614's cap: 340px, fitted to a then-current **minimum** rc ink of 239, with `gap ≈ 340 − rendered_ink + 12` — and a minimum over a growing corpus can only move one way, so every filing shorter than anything in that sample pays the difference straight into the gap. **Disposition (a), accepted residue. `(c′)` — per-row content sizing bounded at today's 340, `fit-content(340px)` — was MEASURED and DECLINED, and it is priced on the register so reopening is a decision rather than a rediscovery:** it buys over 5 → 1, header 215 → 12 and **zero vertical cost** (feed +0px, wrapped 63 → 63), and it spends the ISSUES column — x-origin spread **0 → 167px at 2560 and 0 → 201px at 1440**, worst where the surface is tightest — leaving a **partial** column, 78% of rows holding the old x at the bound while 22% pull left. **`.lob-filing-row` ships unchanged; no code, no CSS, no component was touched by this HO.** **FILED AS ADOPTED-UNVIEWED, and that is recorded rather than glossed: the eight paired renders were never opened, and the ISSUES-column loss — the thing the pass HALTed for — was ruled on the written recommendation, not by eye.** The shots stay on disk as the **unexercised record**, so a later reader knows which kind of decision this was. **Three things ship with it.** (1) The **header-axis class closes EXACT on all three routes** against a corrected **four-term** form, `gap = track_A − ink_A + column_gap + inset_B`, **valid only where `inset_A = 0`** — and the precondition is written into the register line, not a footnote, because `.mc-r-num` and AMOUNT are live counterexamples where the form gives 52 against a surface reading 32. `/trades` is the exhibit: `inset_B` **139px**, so the old three-term form predicts 57 against a measured **196**, off by more than the threshold itself. (2) **Two of the four terms are text metrics**, which corrects — append-only, HO 633's block untouched — its claim that the class "cannot be moved by type at all": the prediction held, the stated reason was over-strong, and it held because those metrics are short fixed labels rather than corpus data. (3) The **admission standard is amended twice**: a register line prices the row's **worst** gap, not the row (`/members` carries 4 over-threshold gaps across 2 header rows and the register carries 2), and a line may be **class + bound + statistic instead of a px constant** where the class genuinely has no constant — `/lobbying` M1 varies **1–3 per crawl**, measured twice on different days, 7 of 81 rows pooled at 121–179px. **Site M1 restated, cited and not subtracted: 9 (`/members`) + 2 (`/vote`) + 3 (`/lobbying`) + 1 (`/trades`) = 15**, where `/lobbying`'s 3 is 1 header row + 2 data rows and is itself a sample under that bound. **Two items found off the shots ride as backlog lines only because they verified**, and neither is 634 scope: the dashboard renders **243** and **138** twice each from **one predicate** each (`getStageChangesCount`, `getNewBillsThisWeekCount` — same cached call at both sites, diverging only under an active filter, week strip recommended to survive since it carries the deltas), and **`bills.stage_changed_at` records when the SYNC OBSERVED the advance, not when the stage changed** (`new Date().toISOString()` at both write sites; lag positive on 40 of 40 recent movers, median 3.76d, 40 rows across 2 minute-stamps) — which matters only because HO 632's day-grouping reads that field, so **"group and age from the same timestamp" holds with both off the observation clock**: internally consistent, externally wrong. **Also notes now run through HO 634.**

**Also (HO 635–636), the stage clock renamed and ENACTED re-sourced — a schema rename that fixes a CLASS of defect and exactly one instance of it; no theme-% change (one column renamed, one reader re-sourced, no new data domain).** The HO 634 block filed `bills.stage_changed_at` as a WATCH: both write sites stamp `new Date().toISOString()`, so the field records **when the sync OBSERVED an advance, not when the stage changed**, and HO 632's day-grouping reads it. **The census is what made the size legible — fifteen consumers read that name as occurrence, NINE of them windowing COUNTS on it** — and the disposition follows from that split: **rename, so every reader confronts what it is getting, plus re-source the one reader whose replacement anchor is exact.** `stage_changed_at` → **`stage_observed_at`** (`stage_transitions.changed_at` → `observed_at` folded in), across both writers, every production reader, all 14 `INDEXED BY` hints and the 17 diagnostics under `scripts/`. **THE RENAME FIXES NO INSTANCE, AND THAT IS THE POINT** — it converts every remaining class-(C) reader from an unexamined default into a deliberate choice. **ENACTED is the one instance, and it moved first and alone** (`9e95b21`), because for an enacted bill the latest action IS the enactment, so `latest_action_date` is exact rather than a proxy; **both halves of its WoW delta moved together**, since subtracting an occurrence count from an observation count is the same defect one level up. It changed a live number — the strip read `● 0 enacted ▼3` and now reads `±0`, because the shipped ▼3 was three enactments we *noticed* last week that had happened a month earlier. **THE FIGURES, WITH THEIR GRANULARITIES, BECAUSE THE NUMBERS ALONE MISREAD** (n=40 throughout, coverage **100%** — 40 resolved, 0 unresolved): the shipped clock bins a row into the right **week** **60.0% (24/40)**; `latest_action_date` bins it into the right **week** **85.0% (34/40)**; and `latest_action_date` **equals** the true advancing-action date exactly, at **DAY** granularity, **82.5% (33/40)**. **The last two are ONE PROXY AT TWO GRANULARITIES, not a proxy scored against a truth** — the ground-truth date carries no score, it is the yardstick — and **34 ⊇ 33 by construction**, since an exact date match is necessarily a same-week match. Disposition (4) therefore rests on **85.0 against 60.0**, both at the granularity the counting readers actually window on. A later reader who sees 85 beside 82.5 and reaches to reopen this should read that sentence first; the failure mode this paragraph exists to prevent is a closed decision reopened on a granularity mismatch. **WHAT DID NOT SHIP, STATED SO IT READS AS A DECISION AND NOT AN OVERSIGHT: eight class-(C) counting readers still window on the observation clock** — `getStageChanges` / `getStageChangesCount` (the `243 ▲15` strip, read at three sites), `getWeeklyBandPriorWeek`, `getWeeklyBandTransitionLadder`, `gatherLeadData`'s two transition reads, and `gatherReportData`'s stage-movements + first-tracked reads — carried forward as a QUEUED backlog entry with disposition (4) already priced. The six class-(B) display-age readers and three class-(A) ordering readers are **deliberately unmoved**: nothing in the probe says they must move, and observation time is arguably the right clock for ordering a terminal. **The census classes OVERLAP** (`getStageChanges` both windows and orders), so those class counts do not partition the fifteen. **Disposition (3) — a new occurrence column — is DISQUALIFIED ON POPULATION, not on effort:** a ~29h API backfill reaching **1,758 of 17,520** rows, so the corrected counts would be a partial-population figure with no honest way to signal that to a reader. **The measured stakes for whoever picks (4) up:** `TRANSITIONS` reads **243** on the shipped clock against **163** on occurrence — 49% high — and the four-week shape **INVERTS**, 112→230→228→243 versus 180→288→114→163, so the *delta* diverges threefold rather than the level merely shifting; **zero missing cron days across 28**, so this is structural to polling cadence and not an outage artifact. **THE MIGRATION SHAPE IS THE REUSABLE PART: expand → ONE cutover commit → an idempotent top-up run AFTER the deploy is confirmed live → contract**, at zero exposure and the cost of one extra `migrate` run. The expand adds the column, backfills it, and creates the **correctly-named** index before any hint can name it — a missing named index is a hard SQL error, not a silent fallback. **The contract is the FALSIFICATION LEG, not cleanup:** while the old column still exists a missed reference is *silent*, reading a column nobody writes and going stale invisibly; dropping it is what makes a missed reference fail loudly, so a clean run after it IS the proof that the cutover was complete. Verified either side — expand read gap 0 / mismatch 0 on both tables, and the pre-drop gate re-run against prod *after* the cutover was confirmed live read gap 0 on both, so no in-flight lambda from the old build beat the top-up. **`stage_transitions` DUAL-WROTE for exactly one release** because its `changed_at` is `NOT NULL` with no default — which is precisely why its zero readers did NOT license the bare `RENAME COLUMN` a reader-only analysis suggests. **A stale verbatim mirror was reconciled as part of the cutover:** `cold-start-audit-332.ts` pins copies of production SQL, and its `queryEnactedThisWeek` copy still windowed on the old clock after `9e95b21` moved it — so re-running unreconciled would have EXPLAINed a statement that no longer exists in the product and passed it green. Second citation of the HO 554→557 rule, this time inside the standing instrument built to catch mis-planned queries. **TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE HO 634 BLOCK ABOVE, appended rather than retro-edited:** `getStaleBills` windows on `latest_action_date` and was **never exposed** to this field (the 634 write-up misattributed it), and the legacy-NULL cohort is **15,631**, not the `~14k` `components/StagePillStrip.tsx` still documents (comment fix filed as QUEUED; HO 636 was docs-only). **And one correction to my own reading of the observation, worth more than either:** I read the three percentages as accuracy scores against a shared yardstick and concluded 82.5% must therefore be a coverage figure. Coverage was 100%. **A trio of percentages measured against one ground truth invites exactly that misreading unless each one carries its granularity** — which is why the figures above carry theirs. A second inference stated as if measured is corrected in the same spirit: the observed stale-value flip was seen against a **local `next start` and its `.next/cache`**, not against prod — no stale value was ever observed on prod, and the first prod read after `bb54c22` already served the corrected one. **Docs (HO 636):** this block · the WATCH closed with its remainder carried forward as a priced QUEUED entry · the 32-reference rename sweep · two new oddities entries and two ride-along clauses · the SKILL schema/index/query contract plus three durable clauses (the migration pattern, the `ensureColumn` resurrection trap, the index-name lie). **The sweep left every CLOSED roadmap, backlog and oddities block alone by rule** — an entry naming `stage_changed_at` was correct when it was written, and a sweep that silently rewrites history is worse than one that leaves a stale name in a 2026-07 block; where a reader would be misled, this block carries the correction. **Also notes now run through HO 636.**

**Also (HO 637), disposition (4) survives falsification and is HELD anyway — the anchor is right and the maturity is not; no theme-% change (a read-only probe and a ruling, no code).** The HO 636 QUEUED entry recorded (4) — re-source the eight class-(C) counting readers from `stage_observed_at` onto `latest_action_date` — as **priced and proven viable** off an 85.0%-vs-60.0% week-binning figure. **That figure measured BINNING ACCURACY FOR ROWS ALREADY IN THE SET; it never measured SET MEMBERSHIP**, and moving the window changes which rows are in the set at all. So this HO measured membership, and then measured the thing neither the handoff nor the entry had thought to ask. **On membership, (4) SURVIVES:** across the current week and three prior (cohort **1,556** stamped rows, 2026-08-11), the rows (4) admits that the shipped clock does not are **overwhelmingly late notice, not staleness** — 0/57/144/157 per window observed *after* the window closed, against only 3/2/6/12 observed before it opened, and the share of set (2) stale beyond the p90 observation lag (16.01d) is **0.6% · 0.0% · 0.9% · 5.1%**. Corpus-wide only **52 of 1,556** rows (3.3%) have any action postdating their observed advance, **28** by more than a week. The feared membership error is real and small. **Its measured size is a FLOOR, not a steady state** — both sets require the stamp, which begins 2026-05-11, so the staleness ceiling is 92.1 days and the rate can only grow as the stamped population ages.

**WHAT HELD IT IS A DEFECT THE ARC HAD NOT LOOKED FOR: MATURATION.** Under (4) the predicate still requires `stage_observed_at IS NOT NULL`, so a week's count **keeps growing as late observations arrive** — 57 of w1's eventual 106 rows landed after w1 closed. The trailing-7d window is therefore always missing its unobserved tail while the comparison week has matured, and **the WoW delta is biased negative by construction**. The settling curve, pooled over seven closed windows: **f(0)=32.7% · f(3)=63.2% · f(7)=79.7% · f(14)=92.1% · f(21)=95.0%**. The bias floor is `f(N+7) − f(N)` — **at N=0 it is 47.0pp, meaning a perfectly flat week renders −47% WoW.** Live confirmation of direction: w0 reads 155 against w1's 106 for a naive **+46.2%**, but at equal maturity (both at m=0) it is 155 against **49**, **+216%** — understated ~4.7x. **This is the ENACTED lesson one level over: same clock on both halves, different MATURITIES.** The shipped clock has no maturation term at all — its window and its stamp are the same event, so f(0)=100% by construction, which is the property being given up.

**Three ways out were priced and all three were refused, so the entry records refusals rather than an open question.** **(a) A SETTLING LAG of N=14** brings the bias to 3.0pp with 92.1% in hand — and buys a 3pp residual on a strip then labelled for a week that ended a fortnight ago. **The dashboard's claim is that it answers what is happening NOW; a 14-day-stale live signal is a different product, not a tuned one — owner ruling, and it is not a parameter.** N=21 reaches −0.7pp for another week of staleness. **(b) (4d) DISCLOSE-DON'T-DELAY** — keep the window current and mark the week as still settling, the way a tape marks an unsettled close — **PRICED BADLY AND DECLINED ON THE MEASUREMENT.** Completeness at close ranges **3.9% to 70.3%, an 18.0x spread, median 34.2%**, so *one* caveat cannot describe the number: the same mark would sit over a reading 70% complete one week and 4% the next, and w4 would have rendered **3** against an eventual **77**. The cause is that observation is **batched, not continuous** — over 56 days, 45 days carry any observation and 11 carry none, per-observing-day median 12 against a max of 137 — so the close-day reading is a function of whether a batch landed before or after the boundary, i.e. of sync cadence rather than of Congress. That is not an incomplete number, it is a differently wrong one, and the trade this project keeps choosing is wrong-for-incomplete. **The scheme's "settled prior-week delta" is not settled either:** at any week's close its two halves sit at f(7) and f(14) by construction, a −12.4pp floor, and simulated across w0–w5 the shown delta sign-flips against the settled truth twice (−4% shown vs +2% true; −15% vs +89%). A genuinely settled delta needs both halves at m≥14 — the 14-day staleness relocated into the delta, not removed. **(c) So GROUP A IS HELD**, pending an owner ruling on the label, not on a parameter.

**The eight are ruled per reader, and the handoff's own groupings were corrected twice by the code.** **Group A — four readers that move together or not at all:** `getStageChangesCount` · `getStageChanges` · `getWeeklyBandTransitionLadder` · `getWeeklyBandPriorWeek`. **The handoff's "`getStageChanges` is separable, a (C)+(A) hybrid" was WRONG**: the feed and its count are co-rendered on three surfaces (`app/changes/page.tsx:57`, `app/welcome/page.tsx:138`, `components/ActivityTicker.tsx:56`) and are **arithmetically coupled** — `ActivityTicker.tsx:63`'s `remaining = counts.total - bills.length` drives the `[ + N more → ]` footer, so moving the count alone makes that number silently wrong, clamped at 0 when the count drops below the rendered rows. The ladder coupling was verified rather than inherited (same `buildChangesWhere({},7)` + `GROUP BY stage`; w0 sums 220+23+3 = 246 = the headline). **APPROVED IN PRINCIPLE, HELD on maturity.** When it moves: hint to `idx_bills_latest_action` **in the same edit**, the forced-hint justification comment at **all four sites** (`queries.ts:6793`, `queries.ts:6835`, `dashboard-lead.ts:51-57`, `report-generation.ts:669-676`) — not the one the handoff named — and `cold-start-audit-332.ts:205,207`'s pinned copies reconciled in the same edit. **Group B — `gatherLeadData`'s two reads: DECLINED**, recorded as declined rather than left open; the dashboard lead has been orphaned since HO 244 and moving a clock inside an unrendered generator is churn. **Group C — the handoff's "two reads" ARE NOT A PAIR, and the split is the arc's best catch:** `report-generation.ts:676` is a count over a named calendar week, already `date(stage_observed_at) BETWEEN ? AND ?`, while `:720`'s `MIN(date(stage_observed_at))` is `stageTrackingStart`, the provenance guard that lets a report say "this week predates stage tracking" instead of "Congress was idle" — **a statement ABOUT the observation clock, which must never move.** Moving `:676` alone would also make that guard insufficient: **118 cohort rows (7.6%), action dates spanning 2025-01-23..2026-05-07**, would count as transitions in weeks that predate tracking while the guard still said the week predates tracking. **`:676` NEVERTHELESS STAYS, and the reason is maturation, not the guard** — the report generates Monday 09:30 UTC for a week that closed ~9.5 hours earlier, maturity ≈0.4d, so under (4) it would **freeze ~33–43% of the week's movements into `reports.content_md` and never revisit them**, where a live reader at least self-corrects as observations arrive. Under the shipped clock the report's count is complete-as-defined at week close. **The split stands as analysis; the move does not happen.**

**THE LANDED HO 635–636 FIGURES NEED THIS CORRECTION, AND THE DECOMPOSITION SHOWS BOTH PRIOR FRAMINGS WERE WRONG.** The four-week occurrence column `163 / 114 / 288 / 180` and the claims built on it — **"243 against 163, 49% high"** and a four-week shape that **INVERTS** — read the observation count as an inflation of a true occurrence count. **"49% high" is not supported.** The two anchors do not differ by spurious membership (0.6% this week); they differ by **BINNING**, and **neither figure is the true count**: 243 is *moves we noticed this week*, 155/163 is *bills we ever saw advance whose latest action of any kind fell this week*, and advances that actually **occurred** this week remain unmeasured — disposition (3), a new occurrence column, stays disqualified on population (~1,758 of 17,520 reachable). **The shape inversion is real but is a RE-BINNING, not the correction of an error.** **Do not write the gap as 111** — that is a net (806 in (1) over the 28-day span against 695 in (2)) and it describes neither population. The gross is **126 rows (1) counts that (4) does not, against 15 (4) counts that (1) does not**, and 126 − 15 = 111. **Of the 126, both mechanisms are present and the earlier one-sided readings were each half right:** **34 sit at >30d lag** — genuine phantoms that (1) counts as recent transitions and (4) correctly rebins away, the exhibit being **`119-s-3674`, latest action 2026-01-26, first observed advancing 2026-08-07**, counted by the shipped clock as a transition in the last four weeks — while the remaining ~92 sit at ordinary observation lag straddling the span boundary, which (4) pushes backward out of view rather than removing. Median lag 16d, p90 58d, max 193d.

**Docs (HO 637):** this block · the QUEUED entry rewritten to carry the settling curve and the per-reader rulings, so a later reader inherits the refusals instead of rediscovering the bias · a new backlog line for the orphaned lead-generation path. **Finding: the shipped clock's own window compares a 24-char ISO instant against `datetime('now', …)`'s space-separated form, and `'T'` (0x54) > `' '` (0x20), so the boundary calendar day is admitted whole at the lower bound and excluded whole at `getWeeklyBandPriorWeek`'s upper bound — measured at −5..+6 rows/week against a true-instant compare.** It **self-resolves for Group A** the moment both bounds sit on a date-only column, so it is not fixed twice; its only residue in product code is `lib/dashboard-lead.ts:64` and `:103`, the orphaned generator — **retiring that path closes this finding outright**, which is why the backlog line carries it. Older probes pinning the same shape (`cold-start-246`, `filtered-view-500-382`, `sync-trailing-cost-479`, `stage-clock-635`) are a sweep line, not a gate. **Also notes now run through HO 637.**

**Also (HO 638–639), the ME Senate card named a field the seat no longer had, and `nominee` enters the `race_candidates` vocabulary as a distinct status handled identically to `won_primary` — no theme-% change (one value added to an existing vocabulary plus a three-row roster correction; no new data domain).** `/race/S-ME-2026` and the dashboard race card showed **Mills and Platner** as the active field while the seat's actual general-election candidate was **Troy Jackson**, nominated at a **2026-07-25 party convention** after Platner won the June 9 primary and **withdrew on July 10**. **Three mechanisms independently guaranteed it would never self-correct, and the third is not a variant of the second.** (1) The `races-seed.json` entry was frozen — a hand-curated roster nothing re-derives. (2) `backfill:race-challengers`' `source_url='harvest:primary_winner'` **sentinel guard structurally skips any race that already carries a non-sentinel row**, which is exactly what a hand-seeded strip race is — so the harvest could never reach S-ME by construction, not by accident. (3) Found in Phase 1 and independent of both: ME's `primary_candidates` carry **no `status='winner'` row at all**, so even with the guard lifted the harvest is **inert** for that seat — it derives challengers from primary winners and ME has none recorded. Removing any one bar leaves the other two standing.

**Two corrections to the handoff, recorded rather than retro-fitted.** Its §1 claimed the seed was frozen at HO 171 with `last_verified` 2026-06-01. **Wrong: `seed:races` RAN on 2026-07-03 and it touched ME.** The JSON *entry* was frozen, not the run — the write happened, the content didn't change, and the stamp moved anyway. That is the finding, not a footnote: it is the entire mechanism behind the `last_verified` oddity filed this arc. Second, the **"NJ-07 shape precedent"** cited when the seed diff was approved is **cosmetic**. JSON array order feeds nothing (the loader upserts row by row) and display order is the SQL `CASE` ladder alone — so a correct NJ-07 render is **not evidence** that the file's candidate ordering did anything at all, and it must not be cited as one again.

**The vocabulary, and why the expedient thing was refused.** `nominee` ships as a **distinct status handled identically to `won_primary` everywhere** — a different HOW of becoming the party's general-election candidate, not a different WHAT. Seeding Jackson as `won_primary` would have rendered correctly today, which is precisely the trap: it writes a **false claim into a status column that nothing downstream re-reads** (he won no primary; Platner did), and a false-but-correctly-rendering value is the HO 577 defect class carrying the HO 420 cost. **Five hard sites, not the four the handoff counted.** The missed one is **`buildRoster`'s `wonPrimary` flag**, which feeds `favoredMember`'s party-market tiebreak — a nominee who didn't set that flag would lose the tiebreak to a `withdrew` row. `RosterMember.wonPrimary` was **renamed `isNominee` in the same commit**: once `nominee` sets that flag, the old field name is the same status-column lie relocated one layer inward.

**The falsification, and the two sites ME cannot reach.** With Mills and Platner both `withdrew`, `active.length === 1` and `ofParty.length === 1` **short-circuit before either flag is read** — so `buildRoster` and `deriveMatchup` are **unreachable from ME's own data** and are proven by the scratch third-candidate fixture **alone**. Measured, not asserted: without the fixture, `nominee` and a garbage status are **indistinguishable** on those two paths. A green ME card is **not** a fire of them, and the provenance is stated here so a later reader doesn't inherit the wrong one.

**Two soft sites got comments, not code.** `RaceMapCard` and `RaceDistrictCard` render the raw status generically, so `nominee` degrades correctly with **no edit** — that is **luck, not design**, and it is the opposite failure mode from `statusLabel`'s `default:` rung, which silently swallows an unknown. The comments mark the generic path as **load-bearing** so a later tidy toward an explicit switch doesn't manufacture a third silent-default site.

**The ordering fire, and why it could not run from `tsx`.** Both ladders are `unstable_cache`-wrapped, and calling one outside a Next request context throws `Invariant: incrementalCache missing` — this codebase's own HO 622. So `getRaceCandidates` was fired through **`GET /api/race/S-ME-2026/hub`** and `getRaceCandidatesForCycle` through a **rendered `/electoral`**, its only consumer. Both returned **Jackson 0, Platner 1, Mills 2**; the failing signature would have been **Jackson at 3+**, sorted below the withdrawn rows. The raw status column was read back as the literal string `nominee` **first**, before either ordering was trusted — because `seed:races` does not validate status and a typo would have produced a **byte-identical failure signature** at the seed step.

**Two things the fire turned up that nobody asked for.** `buildRacesCartogram` had to be **read** before `/electoral`'s DOM order could count as a SQL fire — it is a pure grouping pass with no sort, so the array order is the query's; had it sorted, the reading would have been **JS measured and called SQL**. And the locator **failed loudly on its first run** rather than returning an empty selection: `kalshiOdds` carries a nested `"raceId":"S-ME-2026"` that tripped the boundary guard, so the rewrite selects by **roster content** and then back-tracks to the enclosing `raceId` to verify provenance (57 challenger arrays in the payload, **exactly one** matched).

**One thing that looks like evidence and isn't.** The live ME card renders `Jackson  D · nominee`. That string is **`RaceCard:206`, a hardcoded label for the `challenger.kind === "nominee"` SHAPE** (HO 305) — unrelated to the status column, and it would read identically if Jackson were `won_primary`. The status-column evidence is the **two orderings** plus `/race/S-ME-2026`'s title-case **`Nominee`**. This is the arc's own instance of the layer-check policy: the check has to look where the value **lands**, not where a similar-looking string is **authored**. Filed to oddities.

**The instrument that already knew, and it is the sharpest finding of the arc.** **Kalshi (65%) and Polymarket (69%) both named Troy Jackson as favorite** while the roster printed beside them on the same card named Platner and Mills. `favoredMember`'s name-market branch does `roster.find(m => nameKey(m.name) === nameKey(fav.favoriteLabel)) ?? null` — so it **computed "this market names someone who is not on this roster" on every render for roughly two and a half weeks** and **discarded it as a null**, falling back to a party label. Two independent feeds disagreed with the curated data beside them and **nothing surfaced it**. Meanwhile `races.last_verified` read **green on the defect**: `/race/S-ME-2026` printed "last verified 2026-07-03" beneath a roster **two candidate-states** behind, because the stamp records when `seed:races` last ran and stamps whether or not the entry's content changed — the **same-as-success shape** (HO 503/506/637) reaching a **live user-facing surface**, with a live exhibit. Both are filed: the market-vs-roster disagreement as a queued detector, the stamp as an oddity.

**What the fix did NOT do, stated as a decision.** Mills and Platner **stay as rows**: `seed:races` is insert/upsert with **no delete pass**, so removing them from the JSON is a no-op and both would render as `running` forever — they had to be **transitioned by status**, and `withdrew` is accurate for both. And ride-along B left the **39-race `last_verified` re-stamp unpatched** (2026-07-03 → 2026-08-11, of which exactly one was actually verified): a hand-restore of the other 38 is **reversed by the next run with nobody watching**, which is worse than a uniformly meaningless stamp. The stamp is not trustworthy either way; the honest move is to record why, not to launder 38 dates into looking deliberate.

**Docs (HO 639):** this block · three backlog QUEUED lines (the name-market stale-roster detector, the 11 shares-without-a-winner contests split into their two classes, the absent convention-nominee ingestion path) and two WATCH lines (the twice-transcribed ordering ladder, `seed:races` validating ratings but not status) · two oddities entries (the `RaceCard:206` shape label, the `last_verified` run-stamp) · SKILL's three stale status enumerations corrected with a vocabulary note, the harvest passage's `won_primary` deliberately left alone. **Also notes now run through HO 639.**

**Also (HO 640–641), the two-class split was a story and the measurement killed it — and underneath it sat a settled-guard that freezes a contest the instant it is scored; no theme-% change (a read-only probe and its docs).** HO 640 was scoped to characterize the 2 contests HO 639 left uncharacterized inside the 11 shares-without-a-winner contests. **It started by reproducing the whole partition rather than starting from the 11, and that is what licensed everything after it:** on the predicate copied verbatim from the HO 638 probe (`primary_date IS NOT NULL AND primary_date < today AND election_round = 'primary'`), it read **718 / 506 / 212 → 11 / 123 / 78, every delta zero**. A drifting denominator would have made every number below it a measurement of something else; it didn't drift, so the characterization stands on the same ground the original claim did.

**The category-error half of the split is FALSIFIED, with numbers.** HO 639's backlog line asserted the 8 top-two/jungle contests were a **model** error — two candidates advance by design, so a single `status='winner'` row is a category error. Measured across completed contest-party `open` contests: **CA 52 total / 48 with winners / 48 carrying exactly TWO winner rows. WA 10 / 3 / 3. Zero contests anywhere carry exactly one.** Fifty-one top-two contests are marked correctly with two advancers each — the schema, the ingester and HO 207's two-★ rule all handle top-two properly. So the 8 are not a category error; **they failed the same way ME and NJ did**, and the 11 collapse to **one class, not two**.

**The count was wrong in its inputs too, and the correction names where the phantom came from.** **ME is two contests, not one** — `senate-ME-2026-D` and `senate-ME-2026-R` are separate `primaries` rows — so the real split is **8 / 2 / 1**, and the single genuinely unclassified row is **`house-NJ-09-2026-R`**: an ordinary partisan primary, two candidates, Pino at 51.5%, a derivable winner, no top-two or RCV excuse. Noticing that 8 + ME didn't reach 11 was right; **inferring "2 uncharacterized contests" from the gap was not** — one of the two was an undercount, not a contest. A fourth shape rides inside ME: **`senate-ME-2026-R` carries one candidate, Collins at 100.0%**, uncontested, so there is no votebox contest for anyone to mark — which is exactly why the obvious guard fix is refused below.

**THE FINDING THAT OUTRANKS THE ONE THE PROBE WAS SENT FOR.** `isSettled` (`primaries-sync.ts:551-565`) is **past-dated AND carries a recorded share — with no winner term** — evaluated at all three sync write paths (`:646`, `:905`, `:1311`) **before the incoming roster is built**, while `status` and `vote_pct` are written in the **same INSERT** (`:685`/`:948`/`:1380`). A contest scraped in the window after Ballotpedia posts results and before it marks a winner therefore takes shares + `running` and **settles in that same instant**; every later tick refuses it. It is a race against Ballotpedia's call, and it keeps catching contests every cycle. **516 of 718 completed contests are settled — and most froze CORRECTLY, having been marked before they settled. The damage is the 11 that froze mid-write. The mechanism is the defect, not the count.** The arithmetic closes exactly rather than being cited as a residue: 506 with-winner + 11 = 517 against 516 with-shares, the one-row difference **measured, not inferred**, as `senate-MI-2026-R` — one candidate, winner marked, no share recorded.

**Two dispositions, and the second one already ships.** **Stopping new losses is a guard fix and NOT a one-liner:** adding a winner term un-freezes every contest that legitimately has no winner — the uncontested seats — and those re-scrape forever, so a correct fix needs a bounded re-check (eligible N days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless) or an attempt counter, with **N measured against how long Ballotpedia actually takes to call**. Probe, then build, under its own number. **Recovering the existing 11 needs no build at all:** `isSettled` is module-private and guards only the three sync paths, so **`npm run backfill:primary-results`** (and `reingest:primary-slate`) bypass it and write both columns — gated on one measurement, whether Ballotpedia has marked those voteboxes since, which is a live fetch and therefore not a docs-HO action. **"Permanent" was the review's word and it is struck**: the freeze is **unreachable by any automatic path, not irreversible**, and calling it permanent would have justified building a repair that already exists. What is true is the reporting — the cron reports success every tick while never touching these rows, the same-as-success shape reaching the ingestion side.

**The WA verdict is `structurally missing`, and the evidence is a same-tick contrast rather than a timestamp argument.** All ten completed WA contests carry `pc.updated_at` at or after their own 2026-08-04 date, so "un-swept" is retired outright. In the **same 2026-08-06 tick**, WA-04/06/10 each took **two** winner rows while WA-05/07/08/09 took none — same date, same run, same code path. `isWinner` originates at `primary-candidates-scrape.ts:212` off Ballotpedia's votebox `winner` CSS class, so **no mark means no winner**, and the discriminator was entirely whether that particular race had been called.

**A self-correction inside the probe, kept because the wrong number was the plausible one.** Reachability was first read off `primaries.race_id`, found NULL on all eleven, and reported **all 11 unreachable** — a finding that would have quietly closed the product question. That column is the **runoff** join key (4 of 876 rows, 0 of the 718 completed; `getRunoffsForRace` matches on it and is correct), **not** the primary→race link, which is the shape join `state` + `chamber` + `CAST(district AS INTEGER)` gated by `EXISTS(race_ratings)`. Re-measured: **product impact is 5, not 11** — NJ-09, WA-05, WA-08, ME-D, ME-R sit on rated seats; the other 6 are unrated and the harvest never reaches them regardless. Filed to oddities, with the wrong first pass as the exhibit. Ride-along noted there: the density comment at `queries.ts:1684` still reads **3/907** against a measured **4/876**.

**`primary_type` is NULL on all four WA rows, and the handoff's proposed attribution was wrong.** This is **not** an instance of the HO 584 fallback-default WATCH: `primaryTypeFor` (`primary-calendar-scrape.ts:62-67`) falls back to `return "open"` and **cannot emit NULL**, so citing 584 would file a live gap under a cause that reads as understood. It takes its own WATCH line with **mechanism unknown stated in those words** — **91 NULL of 876, 75 of the 718 completed**, across 12 groups, with **WA the only special-system state left NULL** (CA `top_two` 52, AK `top_four` 4, ME `ranked_choice` 6, LA `jungle` 6). The consequence is the reason it is carried: **a branch on `primary_type = 'top_two'` catches 52 CA and 0 WA**, and the miss looks like absent data rather than a bug.

**SKILL was checked and NOT edited, which is a result.** Three anchored greps — the `primary_candidates.status` enumeration (`'running' | 'winner'`), the `/primaries` ★-advancer passage, and the `race_id` schema comment ("loose link to races.id") — all read correct. The ★ passage's *"runoffs advance two, so two ★s is valid and expected"* is now **corroborated** by 48/48 CA and 3/3 WA rather than merely un-contradicted. **There is no Commit B**; manufacturing an edit to make the sweep look complete was refused.

**THE PATTERN, NAMED BECAUSE IT IS THREE FOR THREE AND ALL THREE ARE THE OWNER'S.** §1 of the HO 638 handoff said the seed was frozen at HO 171 with `last_verified` 2026-06-01 — the run had happened, on 2026-07-03, and touched ME. The **NJ-07 "shape precedent"** cited when the seed diff was approved was cosmetic — JSON array order feeds nothing. And now the **category-error split**, reasoned from what top-two *ought* to imply rather than from what the corpus *does*. Each was tidier than the truth, each survived until something measured it, and each was specified by the owner and implemented faithfully by Code. **A framing that makes the backlog tidier is a hypothesis, not a finding.** The counter-discipline is the one this probe ran by accident of instruction: reproduce the whole before characterizing the part, and re-derive a split from data rather than restating it from the report that proposed it.

**Cost:** 11 statements, **38,864 `rows_read`**, both table counts predicated (`WHERE id IS NOT NULL`) so no figure is quoted off a bare `COUNT(*)` — the HO 624 M0 trap, where a bare count is answered from B-tree interior pages and under-reports by 227–416×. `primaries` 876 rows, `primary_candidates` 2,556.

**Docs (HO 641):** this block · backlog QUEUED line 2 **rewritten** (its two-class premise is dead, so it was replaced rather than amended — an amendment would leave the falsified sentence sitting above its own correction) · a new QUEUED **freeze parent** carrying both dispositions · a new WATCH line for the NULL `primary_type` · two oddities entries (the subset-predicate guard, the `race_id` name) · no SKILL edit. Probe `ffde734`, read-only, pushed separately. **Also notes now run through HO 641.**
**Also (HO 642–643), a dashboard chrome batch, a chamber selector, and four rulings taken on measurement rather than on the exhibit that prompted them — no theme-% change (presentational edits, one filter on an existing panel, and three read-only probes; no new data domain).** **The build first, because it is the smallest part.** The HO 608 polarization chip is **cut from `/`** on an owner ruling that ideology belongs to `/members` only — `getPolarizationBand` stays (the members page reads it and `PolarizationOverTime` documents the registration-vs-caucus seam against it), but `/` no longer calls it at all, and the five `.weekly-band-polchip` rules went with it because `WeeklyBand.tsx` was their only consumer. **The breaking-row hover lost both of the things that put a shade over the bills:** the `:has()` cross-column dim that dropped the whole feed to 40%, and the overlay's drop shadow. The opaque `--bg-row-hover` fill plus a 1px `--border-strong` carry the overlay alone — verified by eye on the furthest-overrunning row, **351px past the panel edge and over the distributions**, which is the property that had to hold once the dim it leaned on was gone. **`.v2f-title` was never bold.** Measured before editing: computed weight **400**, IBM Plex Sans, 14px, `rgb(229,231,235)`. The perceived heaviness was **contrast, not weight**, so the lever was `--text-primary` → `--text-secondary`; the weight is now written explicitly so the next reader need not walk the chain to learn it was never set. C3 still holds — line 2 is `--text-dim` and the id chip amber, so the title stays the row's brightest element.

**`.abw-name` is the one whose MECHANISM IS AN UNFALSIFIED HYPOTHESIS, and it is recorded that way deliberately.** The MIA member names carried an unfamilied `font-weight: 600`, so they inherited `--font-mono` and asked **Consolas** — the Windows resolution of that stack — for a weight it does not ship; the browser synthesized a faux-bold, and synthesized Consolas bold is what reads as Comic Sans. That chain is **unfalsified, not proven**: it depends on which mono resolves on the owner's machine, and a rendered-font read here measures this box. The fix (`var(--sans)` at **500**, a real loaded Plex face since HO 633) is correct on every platform regardless, because it removes the synthesis rather than the symptom — which is why it shipped on an unproven mechanism. **The falsification is the owner's eye;** if it still reads wrong the next step is `CSS.getPlatformFontsForNode` on the owner's browser, not another guess. `.abw-title`/`-claim`/`-ev`/`-party` stay mono — chrome, and HO 297's split is exactly that line.

**`PER_CHAMBER` 2 → 3, and the handoff's stated mechanism was a reading of one viewport presented as the general case.** `.race-grid` is `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(400px,100%), 1fr))`, so the column count is whatever the container buys. Measured on the shipped dashboard: `.dash-left` **756px → 1 column** (viewport 1440), **1020px → 2** (1920), **1372px → 3** (2560). The handoff put "`.dash-left` of ~1370px → 3 columns" at the default; **1372px is the 2560 reading alone**, and at 1440 the grid is a single column. So the orphan the ask was pointed at is a **≥~2350px phenomenon** — four cards wrapping 3+1 — while at 1440 and 1920 four cards already filled their rows. The fix is unchanged and now covers more: **6 divides by 1, 2 and 3; 4 divides none of them**, so every column count this panel resolves to fills completely (measured row shapes `[1×6]` / `[2,2,2]` / `[3,3]`). It is the card count and not the grid: widening to a flat 2×2 would trade the hole for four ~680px cards, C8's failure mode. **The unpriced cost, stated because nobody priced it:** at 1440 the grid is one column, so six cards add roughly **500px of panel height at exactly the width that never had an orphan** — the fix is free only at the width that had the defect.

**The chamber selector was mostly already built, and the one real correction was in a count nobody would have checked.** `FeedFilters.chamber` and `buildFeedWhere`'s `bill_type IN (…)` already existed, and `buildChangesWhere`/`buildStaleWhere` compose on it, so MOVERS and TOP STALLS needed only a populated first argument; only `getNewBillsThisWeek` / `getNewBillsThisWeekCount` took signatures, and they moved in the same edit because that helper's own comment says its predicate is exactly the count's so the tab label cannot drift from the rows. **The load-bearing correction: `getStageChangesCount` computes `total` from `buildChangesWhere({}, days, dashboard)` — it DROPS its own `filters` argument by construction**, so a chamber passed in could never have reached `.total` and the badge would have stayed corpus-wide while the rows narrowed. Both call sites read **`.filtered`**, the arm that reads the filters; with no chamber the two arms are byte-identical, so the unfiltered default is unchanged. Verified by exact partition: MOVERS **335 → 116 / 219**, NEW THIS WEEK **173 → 44 / 129**, both summing back. `chamber` is feed-panel scope only — not in `DashboardFilters`, not touching `WeeklyBand`, BREAKING, the distributions or the masthead — and that is written at the derivation site, because a URL param reshaping exactly one panel is surprising and the next reader will otherwise "fix" it.

**The count coupling was PINNED, not merely imprecise, and that is the reason it got its own commit.** `ActivityTicker`'s `[ + N more → ]` took the chamber but not the dashboard stage/topic filters. Measured against prod running the pre-fix code as a control: under `?topics=healthcare` the footer read **305** — the corpus value — while the badge directly above it read **57** and thirty rows were rendered. Not drift; the filter reached the rows and not the count at all. Passing `filters` to the count makes it exact and costs nothing: it is the identical call `app/page.tsx` already makes for the badge, so it shares that `unstable_cache` entry — no new query, no new key. The zero branch is exercised and correct (`[ VIEW ALL CHANGES → ]`, never `+ 0`).

**R1 — `challenger.kind === "empty"` renders a finding it has never once earned, and the fix is AUTHORISED.** The S-MI exhibit (`no challenger filed` printed beside a Kalshi favourite, a Polymarket favourite and an AIPAC PAC line all naming people) turned out to be a class: across **186 rated seats**, **129 reach `kind === "empty"` and 0 of them are true-empty**. Every instance is roster-**absent**. That is sharper than a conflation — it is not a mixed population needing disambiguation, so a split sized for a mix would have been sized wrong. **`/electoral` already renders the same underlying state correctly**, as `RaceMapCard`'s *"Challenger field not yet available"* — an absence. Two surfaces, one state, two contradicting claims, and **the hedged one is the one nobody questioned**. The fix takes the number this sweep establishes and its shape is recorded here: the caller can distinguish on `hubs[i].candidates.length`, so `empty` splits into *roster absent → hedge* and *roster present, uncontested → the finding*. **The second branch has ZERO members in the live corpus**, so it must be **hand-fired on HEAD before it is ever recorded as working** — an unexercised guard is unproven, not protection (HO 552). Scratch only, never a checkout of pre-fix files, confirm it fires, revert, `git status` clean, commit nothing.

**R2 — the harvest is scoped separately, and the render fix does NOT wait for it.** `backfill:race-challengers`' reach stops at `primary_date` **2026-06-02** while winners exist through **2026-08-06**, and its sentinel rows sit at **82 across 53 seats — matching SKILL's recorded first-run figure exactly**, so it has effectively never advanced; the rated set growing **137 → 186** is a second, independent contributor to the 0-row count. Of the 129, only **62 are derivable** — running the harvest closes **under half** the class and leaves the false claim standing on the other **67** (the HO 638 ME shape, where no winner is recorded and the harvest is inert). **The HO 642 report's "hasn't been re-run since 2026-08-04" is CORRECTED here:** that was an inference, the measurement is roughly two months, and it matters because the guess made the staleness look like a week's lag. `race_candidates` carries **no `updated_at` at all**, so the bracket is behavioural — newest primary date reached versus newest reachable — which is **stronger than a timestamp column would have been**, not a workaround for its absence.

**R3 — the HO 639 market-vs-roster detector is NOT built, and the trigger is a re-measurement rather than a date.** M3 counted **88 of 186** seats where a card element names a person while the roster names nobody. An alarm firing on **47% of the rated set** reads the harvest's staleness, not the defect it was conceived for, and calibrating a threshold against a stale harvest bakes that staleness into the baseline permanently. **Trigger: re-measure M3 after the harvest is current.** Build only if it collapses to the ME-shape handful it was designed for.

**R4 — the at-risk lower bound is W = 8, and the PLATEAU is the finding rather than the rung.** Chosen by the rule fixed before the numbers existed: smallest W in [6,20] with pooled max ≤ 6 and median ≥ 1. Pooled max falls 7, 7, **5** and then **sits at 5 for every W from 8 through 20** while distinct members drain 24 → 7 — so raising the bound past 8 buys **no reduction in the peak at all**, only thinner transient traffic. The ceiling is set by roughly **five persistent long House absences that no bound in range excludes**; 5 of them are inside the band at W=20 exactly as at W=8. The band at 8 still spans 22 roll calls before promotion, well past the 10-roll product floor. **WATCH:** selected on a window whose House newest roll is **20 days old** and which lies entirely pre-recess, so it measures voting days rather than the gap but cannot see the return. Re-read after the House comes back, alongside the still-open S=30 review — which stays **unswept**, because moving both boundaries in one pass would make neither attributable. The tier itself remains **unbuilt** and belongs to the deferred MIA redesign.

**R5 — scope, stated so a later reader cannot over-read the 129.** It is **population-at-risk, not cards on screen**. The string lives in `components/RaceCard.tsx`, rendered only by `CompetitiveRacesStrip variant="v2"` — the dashboard's featured seats — so **1 of the 6 featured cards prints it today** (S-MI). PA-10, the other seat commit B newly featured and which nobody had looked at, is **clean**: one hand-curated Ballotpedia row (Janelle Stelson, `won_primary`) whose non-sentinel `source_url` makes the harvest's `NOT EXISTS` guard skip the seat entirely — the HO 213 seed-safe guard working as designed.

**What this arc REFUSED, each naming what would have closed a question prematurely.** The at-risk tier was **not declined off the one-cursor snapshot** — the snapshot read 0 and generalised to "any WARN in 5..29 selects zero", and the 60-cursor replay refuted it outright (median 4, 31 distinct members). The **ceiling of 6 was not moved to fit a W**, and the sweep was not extended past 20 to rescue the tier, because below that the band spans fewer than 10 rolls and warns nobody about anything. And the **harvest was not run to make the S-MI exhibit go green before the class was measured** — which would have turned a 129-seat render defect into a one-seat data chore and closed the wrong question.

**Cost:** three read-only probes. `absence-atrisk-642.ts` 21 statements / **148,545 `rows_read`** (the 60-cursor replay and the 15-rung W-sweep add **zero** queries — both are further passes over the control walk's in-memory matrix, and the statement count is byte-identical before and after them). `roster-coverage-642.ts` 8 statements / **851,974 `rows_read`**, the bulk being M5's could-reach join across `primaries × primary_candidates`. Every figure is a real per-statement `rows_read` via hrana `/v2/pipeline`; nothing is sized by a bare `COUNT(*)` (the HO 624 M0 trap).

**Docs (HO 643):** this block · four oddities entries filed as **one named family plus one outlier** (an index is not a clock; an element rect is not rendered ink; `grep -c '^-[^-]'` is not a deletion count; and separately a verdict string that does not read its own numbers) plus the transient-sampling lesson · four backlog lines (the `--numstat` diff instrument owed from 639–641, harvest currency, the M3 re-measure as R3's build trigger, and the `.home-grid` display orphan filed as a claim) · SKILL's rated-set figure corrected in both places it appears. **Also notes now run through HO 643.**

**Also (HO 644–646), a false negative that had never once been true, a band that became cards and grew a second tier, and the sweep that closed a three-hop pointer drift — no theme-% change (one render fork, one presentational rework plus a widened window on an existing query, and their docs).**

**HO 644 — the card stopped rendering an unknown as a finding.** `challenger.kind === "empty"` printed *"no challenger filed"* on **129 of 186 rated seats, 0 of them true-empty** — the branch had never once been true. `/electoral`'s `RaceMapCard` already rendered the same state correctly as an absence, so the fix was a **copy, not a design decision**: `deriveMatchup` now discriminates on `candidates.length` (roster absent → `unknown` → the hedge) rather than `active.length`, and the string is verbatim from `RaceMapCard`'s placeholder with an instruction to shorten both sites together if it ever has to move. **The sub-case named and NOT built for:** `activeChallengers` also filters withdrawn, so a roster whose challengers have all withdrawn lands on `empty` and renders the negative finding — arguably wrong for a field that *was* filed and then vacated. Zero members today, and a third variant on zero evidence is the mistake the change existed to fix.

**The arc's instrument lesson is HO 644's `/electoral` control, and it is the sharper of the two this arc produced.** The obvious check read **0 vs 0** on both strings — equality by absence, indistinguishable from *"neither surface renders it"*, and it would have passed while proving nothing. It was rescued by working out that `RaceMapCard` only mounts on `pinnedCell`, that `onStatePick` routes to the district modal instead, and that `onSearchSubmit` still pins — then pinning Michigan by search to force the state into existence. Same discipline on the payload: slice before the first `self.__next_f` push rather than accepting a raw `hedge=2`.

**HO 645 — the absence band becomes cards, and gains a second tier.** The band's rows are member cards now, ruled off `docs/design/mock-645-absence-cards-v6.html` (committed before the build, `0e4ab80`). Anatomy: a **mat** around the photo — the Topps border, retoned; it is what makes the thing read as a card rather than a tile, and collapsing it into a thick border loses the face's inner frame with it — the streak as a **corner disc** with its 6px mitre, a position chip, a name plate on a scrim over the photo's foot, and a two-cell stat line.
