The back is a new **330px `AbsenceCardBack`** with ruled stat rows. **`SponsorExpandedPanel` is NOT modified** — `/members` is its other consumer — and with it went the container query, both caps and the measured `calc(100vh - 360px)` fold bound that existed only to fit a ~1000px three-column card into a band-width overlay.

**Positioning: vertical in CSS, horizontal measured.** `top: calc(100% - 12px + 8px)` off the card `<ul>`, where `12px` is the rack's bottom padding and **`8px` is exactly how far a 12px square rotated 45° pokes above its box**, so the notch's tip meets the card rather than nearly meeting it — measured live at **−0.49px** tip-to-card-edge at both 1440 and 2560. Horizontal is read off the open card's `offsetLeft`/`offsetWidth` and clamped into the rack. It anchors to the **lowest** card, not the open one: with one row they are the same card and the notch touches, and if the list ever wraps the panel drops below both rows instead of covering the second — the HO 631 ruling surviving the rework. **Accepted cost, stated so it is not rediscovered as a defect:** the open panel covers the band's footnote and its delegate-carve disclosure.

**The at-risk tier is W = 8**, from HO 642 P2's pre-fixed criterion. Phase A's window widens from 30 to 8 and Phase B's walk is byte-identical; the bucket is `>= ABSENCE_STREAK_MIN` (30) → MIA, `[8, 30)` → at-risk. `ABSENCE_WARN_MIN` and the Phase A window are now **one constant**, because they are the same number by construction rather than coincidence — Phase A's `nv = n` over the last W rolls is a *necessary* condition for a streak of W, so a window wider than the smallest reported streak filters that member out before Phase B ever sees them. **`ABSENCE_STREAK_MIN` was NOT swept:** the S=30 review is a separate loop still gated on the House returning, and moving both boundaries in one pass makes neither attributable.

**Re-measured, not inherited** (`scripts/diagnostic/absence-payload-645.ts`, prod, 2026-08-11): Phase A **house 26,120 → 6,922** `rows_read`, **senate 6,060 → 1,616**, Phase B unchanged at **240 per chamber**. Narrowing W makes Phase A *cheaper* because the window IS the scan — an `IN (…)` over vote ids, so a wider one reads more rows to return fewer candidates. HO 642 read 8,674 for the house leg; today's corpus reads 6,922, which is why it was re-read rather than carried.

**Two branches fired rather than argued.** The amber tier is **empty in the live corpus** (MIA 2 / AT RISK 0) exactly as the occupancy replay predicted, so it was falsified by temporarily raising `ABSENCE_STREAK_MIN` to 40 — moving Wilson at streak 34 through the amber path on real HEAD with real data. The footnote rendered `MIA = 40+`, **which is itself the proof that the disclosure is interpolated and not hardcoded**. And deviation 5 retired HO 630's identity-only degrade on a structural argument; that argument was then **run**: `card === null` forced at the assembly site gives a complete back (three ruled rows of base-query data, head, sub-line, footnote), **zero** block elements rather than empty shells, and a live affordance (`role="button"`, `aria-expanded` `false→true`, one panel). **The control leg is what makes it evidence** — 0 blocks on a nulled member is trivially true, so the discriminating reading is the control's **2 blocks / 0 empty-shelled in the same page load**. Both reverted, nothing committed. **Named and NOT covered by the PASS:** the *cause* was not exercised — the forced null sits downstream of `queryAbsenceWatch`'s per-member `try/catch`, so that catch's `console.error` and its `Promise.all` isolation are still unfired, and they are carried as a WATCH rather than folded into the green.

**Eleven deviations from the handoff, all disclosed. The three load-bearing ones:** the card is `div[role="button"]` rather than the mock's `<button>`, because §1a keeps the name an `<a>` and an anchor inside a button is invalid HTML; the disc reads `60+` at the walk bound, because there the streak is a **floor, not a count**; and the front stat cell says **MISSED**, not the handoff's `119TH` — `missedPct` is a missed rate and **the mock's label named its complement**. That last one is the HO 631 rule earning its keep on a new axis: illustrative mock data set no *numeric* expectation there, and here it nearly set a **semantic** one, on the surface whose entire claim is a member's voting record. **The rule is extended: an illustrative mock sets neither a figure nor a field's meaning.**

**Also recorded, because a substituted proof should read as a substitution:** §5.7's byte diff on `/members` was **not run** — a stash-and-rebuild is unsafe on a shared working tree — and was replaced by a structural proof that names each way the page could have changed and closes it (`SponsorExpandedPanel` and `app/members/page.tsx` untouched; the `globals.css` diff confined to the `.abw` block; zero `.abw-*` classes served), ending at the served string `class="aspect-[3/4] shrink-0 object-cover" style="width:96px;…"`, preserved character for character by reordering the class interpolation. **`SponsorPhoto` *was* modified; its emitted output was not.** Its fallback branch has no instance on that page, so that leg is proven by inspection, not live.

**HO 646 — this sweep, and the drift it exists to prevent.** The pointer had gone **three hops**: 643's block authorised 644, 645 was derived from 644, 646 from 645, with nothing writing a block in between. **The numbering convention reads from the roadmap**, so a doc sweep is not optional bookkeeping — three hops of derivation is exactly the drift it exists to prevent, and each hop was a number nobody could check against the record.

**Two premises in the HO 646 handoff were wrong on inspection, and correcting them here is what keeps this block from asserting them.** (1) §4 instructs *"read SKILL's Absence Watch passage first"* and the Docs line calls for it **rewritten**. **There is no such passage.** Measured at `8852478`: zero occurrences of `MIA`, `streak`, `ABSENCE_`, `getAbsenceWatch`, `AbsenceWatch`, or HO 622/630/631 anywhere in `SKILL.md`; the single `Absence` hit is *"Absence of badge is the correct signal"* on the member hub. So commit 4 **ADDS** the passage — the band shipped at HO 622 and was reworked twice without ever reaching SKILL, which is a three-HO documentation gap this sweep closes rather than a rewrite it performs. (2) The `/` page entry's **body order is stale beyond the band**: it lists a three-item body (`CompetitiveRacesBlock` → `WeeklyBand` → two-column) that predates HO 608's region split and HO 270's `RacesBoxTabs`, so the band could not simply be slotted into a correct list. The band's slot is named in that entry; **the rest of its staleness is left standing and recorded here rather than silently half-corrected** — a partial rewrite of a stale enumeration reads as a current one.

**One process note, from HO 645's own commits, because the pattern is worth a clause rather than three.** Each of its three commits mixed kinds. `0e4ab80` is labelled `docs(...)` and carries the `AbsenceWatchRows → AbsenceWatchCards` rename — splitting a pure rename from its content change is the **right** technique, and it is what let git detect the rename and keep the file's history, but the vehicle was the docs commit. `6a92bbe` carries a diagnostic edit inside a build. And `8852478` carries the backlog re-price alongside code — **that one is the handoff's fault, not the build's**: HO 645 §4 mandated the re-price as a condition of shipping B, which contradicted the standing rule that docs never ride with code. **Recorded as an instruction defect: when a handoff mandates a doc edit as a condition of shipping, it must name it as its own commit in the commit table.**

**Docs (HO 646):** this block · three oddities entries (a threshold expressed in the wrong variable; `__name is not defined` as a build artifact; a lesson encoded in one instrument is not inherited by the next) plus two live confirmations noted against the existing same-as-success rule rather than re-filed · four backlog lines (the payload entry amended with its by-id breakdown, and three new: the unfired `card === null` cause, the 119TH denominator at zero query cost, the `SponsorPhoto` fallback residue) · **SKILL's absence-band passage ADDED, not rewritten** — see the premise correction above. **Also notes now run through HO 646.**
**Banked / unbuilt:** **strong VIA-markup report attribution** (blocked, not deferred — `stage_observed_at` history (renamed from `stage_changed_at` at HO 635) began 2026-05-11, 52 committee→floor moves all-time, no markup-date alignment → 0 clean joins; HO 268 Gate A; revisit once the history matures) · **MARKUPS column on the reports index stat strip** (queued from 268 — needs a new persisted column + migration + backfill) · **MOVERS from→to display + hop-count sort** (gated on `stage_transitions` accrual, planted 2026-06-11) · ~~metro-zoom panels (spec-3 Phase 2)~~ (**SHIPPED HO 236/237** — dense-state CA/TX metro insets + leader lines; see backlog DONE. Extending the per-state config to more dense states is a fresh scoped item, not this banked one.) · ~~**race→news / member→news surface**~~ (**both halves SHIPPED** — race→news hub HO 398, member→news hub HO 414, off the HO 394 observation layer's `observation_entities` entity join; residual drawer/challengers + the dark HO 272 `NEW` badge tracked in backlog QUEUED) · **observation inert fields** (grading / cluster / lineage — activate when a second collector or a real consumer arrives) · **the_hill budget-starvation soak** (watch a few news ticks that politico runs long — confirm the_hill isn't systematically skipped; fix is per-feed budget carving, not a timeout raise) · primaries-map results-coloring (recency-only today) · rating-history sparkline (logging live since HO 220; chart awaits weeks of data).

**Owed eyeball:** **hearings LIVE state** — the ● LIVE badge (v2 band callout + `/hearings` list/calendar glyph) was never exercised against a real streaming meeting (0 live in every build sample); confirm it renders correctly the next time a committee meeting is actually live.

**Also (HO 654), a count stopped claiming to be about Congress, and the delta beside it stopped being rendered at all.** `getStageChangesCount` takes **disposition (4c)** — decline to re-source, disclose instead, stay on the observation clock — and with it **Group A closes as a set** (`getStageChanges` · `getWeeklyBandTransitionLadder` · `getWeeklyBandPriorWeek`). The group's own rule was *move together or not at all*, which governs PREDICATE moves; this HO moves no predicate, so the coupling is honoured rather than broken, and there was no case for disposing one reader out of a coupled four. The HO 636 entry's stated close criterion was an owner ruling on the strip's LABEL, and that ruling is this one.

**The strip reads `TRANSITIONS OBSERVED` and carries no week-over-week delta.** The reason is NOT that observation is fine — it is that **neither available clock yields a correct delta and no third anchor exists in the database.** On the observation clock a delta partly measures sync cadence: `stage_observed_at` is stamped with the wall clock of the run, so an outage dips and the catch-up spikes, and both read on the dashboard as activity in Congress. On occurrence it is biased negative by construction, since 54% of a week's final count arrives after the week closes. HO 635 measured **243 observed against 163 occurrence** with the four-week shape inverting; HO 637 then disqualified the 163 as well, because windowing on `latest_action_date` admits a May referral that picked up a cosponsor yesterday. The LEVEL survives all of that — it is a real count of a real thing — so the count stays and the label now names it.

**Removed in both renderings, which is the part that took a ruling.** The inline `▲N` and the hover card's `▲N vs last wk` + `last wk N · MON DD` are both gone. Disclosure does not rescue a delta: the lag copy explains why the LEVEL is inflated and says nothing about whether the delta's SIGN is trustworthy, and copy that did say so would be a number rendered beside an instruction not to believe it. The hover reader is the one who asked for detail, so serving them a dated prior-week figure is worse than serving them nothing. `prior`/`priorDate` are now optional on `WeeklyBandMetricCard` and their **absence is the claim**. The disclosure sits in that card rather than a Tooltip — the band has none, and the card records why (the primitive does label+body only; the dashboard's stacking would occlude an in-flow card) — so the copy is hover-only and **the label carries the honesty at rest.**

**Figures recorded, not characterised.** At the edit the strip read **361**, prior **176**, delta **▲185**, priorDate 2026-08-05. The ladder's rungs were **committee 335 + floor 24 + other_chamber 2 = 361**, summing to the headline exactly — recorded BEFORE the edit so that a pre-existing gap could not later be misread as this HO breaking the sum; the `introduced`/NULL case that could open one is absent from this window. The delta figure is left as a figure: **▲15 at HO 653's writing and ▲185 here** is consistent with the volatility this ruling assumes and equally consistent with a genuinely busy fortnight, and the data does not separate those.

**Two surfacing facts worth having on record.** The count is read at **five** sites, not the three HO 635 named — `WeeklyBand`, `app/page.tsx`, `ActivityTicker` on `/`, plus `app/changes/page.tsx` and `app/welcome/page.tsx`. Only `WeeklyBand` renders a label or a delta, so the relabel touched one component; but **`/welcome` renders the number as `MOVERS(361)` with no hover surface, so no disclosure reaches it.** `MOVERS` makes no timing claim, so no relabel is owed there — it is simply a surface where the figure appears unqualified. And the ENACTED cross-clock defect HO 654 was told to look for **does not exist**: `queryEnactedThisWeek` and `queryEnactedPriorWeekCount` both window on `latest_action_date` (`lib/enacted-this-week.ts:67-71` and `:97-101`), moved in lockstep by HO 635.

**Docs (HO 654):** this block · the HO 636 class-(C) entry struck (all eight readers dispositioned: Group A closed here, Group B declined at 637, Group C ruled to stay) · one new backlog entry for a stale e2e fixture found in passing · SKILL's strip and `getStageChangesCount` entries state that the count is of observations and that the delta was deliberately removed. **Notes now run through HO 654.**

**Also (HO 656), four open loops closed or advanced by RUNNING things that already shipped — and the one that needed new code needed two fields.** No migration, no cron wiring, no `isSettled` edit. **§A — the freeze recovery is PROVEN on real data.** The gate was one live Ballotpedia fetch of `house-NJ-09-2026-R`: **Pino is now marked `isWinner=true` at 51.3%**, so the freeze is what the entry said it was — **unreachable by the automatic paths, NOT irreversible**. `npm run backfill:primary-results` (which bypasses `isSettled` entirely) then ran off-tick: **398 seats, 370 ok / 28 empty-or-failed, 1,926 candidate rows updated, `vote_pct` 1,663 → 2,077 of 2,527**. **The frozen set went 11 → 6, with 7 of the original 11 recovered** (CA-04/14/16/43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09) and **4 residual** (WA-05, WA-08, ME-D, ME-R). The residuals were live-checked rather than counted: **all three pages parse `ok` and mark ZERO winners**, so the residue is **upstream-unmarked, not a parse failure** — `senate-ME-2026-R` (Collins, uncontested, 100.0%) being the expected-and-correct case the entry already named.

**Two things the run showed that no amount of reading would have.** First, **the population is a MOVING SET, not a fixed 11** — two rows entered the frozen class *during* the run (`house-AZ-03-2026-R`, 1 candidate, uncontested; `house-VA-10-2026-R`, 2 candidates, Aug 4) because writing shares ahead of Ballotpedia's call is exactly what settles a row. The freeze is **ongoing and recoverable**, which is a different and better fact than “11 rows are stuck.” Second, **N cannot be measured from our data and the honest answer is to say so.** We know only that Ballotpedia *had* marked the recovered 7 by today, never *when* — upper bounds only. The live residuals give the other end: **WA unmarked at 9 days, ME unmarked at 65**. So the recommendation is a **bounded re-check window with a GENEROUSLY CHOSEN N (30 days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless)**, not a fitted one; the cost is N wasted fetches on an uncontested seat, and at N=30 the recent contests stay eligible while ME settles. The attempt-counter shape needs a column, so it is a migration and out of scope by construction. **Filed QUEUED; the build takes its own number.**

**§B — the harvest had never advanced, and running it moved 94 seats.** Before-state re-derived rather than trusted: **sentinel 82 rows / 53 seats — byte-identical to SKILL's FIRST-RUN figure**, which is the evidence it had never re-run. After: **206 rows / 147 seats**, and **zero-roster rated seats 130 → 36**. Those 36 are classified into buckets that SUM, with no unnamed remainder: **28 future primary** (not a defect) · **2 winners exist but are all the incumbent**, correctly excluded (NC-07, S-SD) · **2 the §A freeze class** (WA-05, WA-08) · **3 past-dated with an EMPTY ROSTER** (ME-02, NY-02, NY-22 — `cands=0`, the HO 564 territory) · **1 too recent to be called** (WI-06, voted Aug 11, two days before the run). The rated set itself re-measured **187** against the entry's 186, and completed contests **768** against 718 — both drift, both re-derived.

**§C — the absence card's denominator, two fields and no new statement.** `queryAbsenceWatch`'s population pass was already SELECTing `member_participation.total` and `.not_voting` and throwing both away after dividing, so the row now reads **`14.9% · 133/890`**. **Zero new SQL was the premise AND the gate** (added lines matching `db.execute|sql:` = 0). It stays **missed/total, not the mock's cast/total**: the band's claim is absence, so the numerator must be the same quantity the percentage is a rate of. The overflow stop-condition was **measured, not eyeballed** — 83px on one line live, 93px at the mock's `29.3% · 370/1,262` scale, inside a 330px back.

**§D — the `pageErr` rate has NOT step-changed, and that is the finding.** Eleven scheduled prod crawls, 2026-08-02 (HO 590's deploy) → 08-12; **one day's log is simply gone** (`gh`: `log not found`, retention), so the observable span is 10 days and is reported as such rather than extrapolated. **Fires on 7 of 10 days**, events per day **2 · 1 · 4 · 1 · — · 0 · 5 · 10 · 0 · 0 · 1**. The three clean days are scattered, not a step: **08-07 clean is followed by the two heaviest days (5, then 10)**, and the 08-10/08-11 pair is broken by a fire on 08-12. **No deploy in the 604–655 train moved the rate in either direction.** Affected routes are the age-rendering feed set throughout (`home`, `home-stage-*`, `dashboard-v2`, `bills`, `stale`, `committees-redirect`, `member-detail`) — recorded as an observation, not a hypothesis, per the entry's exhausted-families rule. **The counts come from the LOGS, not from run conclusions:** `expect.soft` fails the test, `--retries=2` then reports it flaky, and the workflow still concludes `success` — so conclusions under-report fires and a green run list would have read as a fixed bug.

**Docs (HO 656):** this block · backlog — the 119TH-denominator and harvest-currency entries struck with their numbers, the `isSettled` freeze parent and its 11-contests symptom updated with the recovery and the residue, the `pageErr` entry given §D's table and conclusion, and three new QUEUED entries (the bounded-re-check guard build, the harvest's schedule, `race_candidates.updated_at`). **No SKILL commit:** §B.5 checked the 82/53 line and it presents as **“First run:”** — a dated record, which the instruction says to leave alone. **Notes now run through HO 656.**

**Also (HO 657), the races panel merged to one body — and both of the handoff's load-bearing premises turned out to be false on measurement.** The ruling was variant B of `mock-657-races-merge.html`: the COMPETITIVE|PRIMARIES sub-tab row, the 6-month tick timeline, the 2×2 date grid and the primaries count badge all die; the competitive body survives byte-identical under a new **NEXT PRIMARIES** line with per-date click-open detail. `RacesPanelTabs` (62 lines) and `DashboardPrimaries` (158) are **deleted**, and the shell is server-rendered — the island existed only to hold the toggle. **`getDashboardPrimaries` (the query) is untouched and re-pointed at the line**, consuming the same `cards`/`strip` payload the grid did: no SQL moved for a display merge.

**PREMISE 1, corrected: the hover-popover confinement check could not be run at all.** The handoff called it “the number-one regression risk in the whole HO.” The popover renders only on the **`default` variant**, and **`/dashboard-classic` no longer exists in the tree** — `CompetitiveRacesBlock` has exactly ONE consumer, `app/page.tsx` at `variant="v2"`. So the HO 230 containment box **currently confines nothing on any live route**. The check was **demoted on measurement, not skipped**: replaced by the structural assertion (same `<section>` tag, same `dashboard-pane home-races-pane` classes, one `.home-races-pane` in the DOM) plus a filing. The classes stay regardless, because `.dv2-racesbox .home-races-pane` is live styling on this route.

**PREMISE 2, corrected: `.races-panel-body`'s min-height does not steady the HEARINGS↔RACES flip.** §2.3 asked to “keep whatever prevents the box from jumping.” Nothing does: measured, the box goes **980px on RACES to 102px on HEARINGS**, and the body renders **879px against a 240px floor**, so the rule is **inert at current content**. It was SUB-tab parity, and the sub-tabs are gone. **The handoff's framing had already been written into two comments before the measurement**; both now state what is true, and the min-height is kept only as a floor for a shrunken body. The lesson is HO 654's, one turn later: a comment that overstates its scope is the next defect's nursery, and inheriting one from a handoff is the easiest way to mint another.

**What the payload check bought, which the sub-tab structure had been hiding.** Both tab bodies were `ReactNode` props, so **both serialized on every request even though one rendered** — `dash-prim*` appeared **44 times in the payload** of a page that showed none of it. Deleting the file rather than un-toggling a branch takes it to **0**, and the `/` response drops **555,593 → 549,687 bytes**. Recorded as a figure, uncharacterised. The same property is why “sub-tab row absent” is asserted **in the rendered DOM** and never as a payload string count: a string count conflates DOM with RSC payload, and on this page it did.

**Empty-window behaviour is proven, not assumed.** Forcing `cards: []` renders **nothing at all** — no line, and deliberately **no “no upcoming primaries” notice** — while the competitive body holds. In the merged panel the battlefield IS the body, so an empty notice is noise; the seasonal death is commented so nobody adds an empty state back. Perturbation reverted before the commit.

**Docs (HO 657):** this block · backlog — a QUEUED entry for the now-unreachable `default` variant (its deletion drags the popover, `RaceHubBody`'s preview mode, the `pacByRace` gating and their CSS, plus a SKILL phantom sweep, so it is its own enumerate-first HO per the HO 650 precedent), the parallel-glob flake WATCH gaining its second route, and the prose-that-outlived-its-code entry gaining two diagnostics · SKILL, alone behind the self-mod guard, re-pointing `getDashboardPrimaries` and turning the PRIMARIES-tab and `RacesPanelTabs` passages into RECORD. **Notes now run through HO 657.**

**Also (HO 658), the `default` variant of the races strip is deleted — and the insurance three comments called load-bearing was measured to hold nothing up.** The enumerate-first HO the HO 657 entry filed: `CompetitiveRacesStrip`'s 2×2 branch, HO 178's hover popover, the HO 230 `[data-col]`/`[data-row]` position keys, `RaceHubBody`'s `preview` mode and its preview-only IN THE PRESS block, the Block's three `variant === "v2"` ternaries, and the `.competitive-race-*` CSS all go. **Figures: 578 deletions against 234 insertions across 7 files; `competitive-race` occurrences in the SERVED CSS bundles 34 → 0** (33 in `globals.css` source over 23 lines), **bundle 172,518 → 169,887 bytes local, and 0 on prod against live neighbours still present** (`race-grid` 2, `dv2-racesbox` 9, `home-races-pane` 1, `rc-stat` 4, `hsch-row` 5 — the neighbours are what make the zero discriminating rather than a fetch that missed).

**Two prefetches died with the surface they fed, and they are the reason this was enumerate-first.** `getMember` and `getRunoffsForRace` ran per seat in the Block's loop for `RaceHubBody`'s preview only: **12 reads per dashboard render (6 seats × 2)** for a surface no route rendered. `incumbent`/`runoffs` leave `RaceHubData` with them. **`getRaceCandidates` and `getRaceNews` STAY** — the roster resolves candidate-named markets to party lean (HO 274) and `news[0].observedAt` feeds the NEW badge (HO 432) — and both were confirmed against **pre-fixed baselines**, because the roster's failure mode is same-as-success: kill the threading and the K cell renders a **neutral surname with no error**. Baseline and post-deletion read identically: **6 cards on the same 6 `data-seat` values**, **`New 4` + 4 `.rc-new` pips** and `Moves 3` under a `2000-01-01` last-view stamp, and the five discriminating KALSHI cells **all still party-colored** — `S-ME` Jackson 67%, `S-MI` El-Sayed 59%, `IA-03` Garriott 74%, `OH-07` Poindexter 77%, `PA-08` Cognetti 63%, every one `rgb(59,130,246)` = `--party-democrat`. **S-GA and the three POLYMARKET cells are named false-pass cells and prove nothing**, so they are not cited as evidence.

**All three HO 230 containment sites delete: leg 1 fired on every one.** `.home-races-pane`'s `position:relative`+`overflow:hidden`, its `overflow:visible` override in the 1200px block, and `.dv2-racesbox-panels`'s `position:relative`. The third was gated on one more measurement, because the earlier instrument had exercised **only** the RACES pane while that declaration wraps BOTH. Run with **HEARINGS active**: the pane holds **26 nodes in 66px**, has **zero** absolute/fixed visible descendants, and — the premise correction — **has no hover popovers at all**. The gate's own wording assumed them; `.hcal-stat-tip` belongs to `HearingsCalendar`, which **HO 610 replaced on `/` with the day schedule**, so hovering all 8 hoverable elements produced no popover and no DOM growth. Dropping `position:relative` moved **0 of 22 visible nodes**; the padding-injection control moved **22 of 22**. The RACES leg was then **re-run rather than inherited from the approved table**: **79 visible positioned descendants, 0 whose `offsetParent` is `.dv2-racesbox-panels`** (they anchor to `.ctl` and `.cm-agg`), **0 of 338 moved** on removal against **317 of 338** on the control. Deleting a positioning context is exactly where a remembered pass is worth the two minutes to redo.

**A third instrument trap, on top of the two the approved table already carried** (a perturbation aimed at a `display:none` pane reads "nothing moved" vacuously; a hover aimed at a non-hit-target reads the same). **The first RACES run reported 338 of 338 nodes moved — a total dependency that did not exist.** `getBoundingClientRect()` is viewport-relative and Playwright's `hover()` scrolls its target into view, so re-hovering between snapshots shifted every rect by one uniform delta. **The tell was the uniformity**, and the fix is document-relative coordinates (`+ window.scrollX/Y`). A perturbation instrument that moves the CAMERA between readings reports the whole scene as moving; both a 0 and a 100% deserve a control leg before either is believed.

**Deviations and things left standing, named rather than absorbed.** (1) **The absence figure is 34, not the 37 the approved instrument recorded** — same direction, different count method; both sides here are counted identically (substring occurrences across the served bundles), which is what makes the delta an instrument rather than a number. (2) **The prod dashboard DOM is not reachable to verify the two live-consumer comparators**: `/` 307s to the landing for a signed-out client (the HO 355 auth gate), and the comparators are client-computed from a signed-in render. Prod verification is therefore **SHA `c1b08cf` + the discriminating CSS absence + the public `/race/[id]` payload** (Incumbent · Candidates · News · last-verified all present, `In the press` **0**, `competitive-race` **0**, stable across two reads); **the badge and party-color comparisons were run locally on the identical commit and are reported as local.** (3) The approved ride-along named `useSingleOpenPanel.ts:10-11`; **`:21` carried the same phantom** (it counted CompetitiveRacesStrip as one of "four consumers" of the Esc handler — it stopped using the hook at HO 178) and both lines are corrected. (4) `RaceCard.tsx:35` named the `default` variant as a live sibling and is corrected under the phantom sweep. (5) **Left standing, recorded not rewritten:** the two dated diagnostic comments (`roster-coverage-642`, `sc-special-premise-600`) are artifacts of their probe time; `app/page.tsx:164` still describes "COMPETITIVE|PRIMARIES sub-tabs" and `RaceCard.tsx:102` still says "all four cards" — both are HO 657/642 residue, neither claims a deleted thing is reachable, and widening a deletion into someone else's stale prose is how a scoped commit stops being reviewable.

**`GET /api/race/[id]/hub` is confirmed orphaned and FILED, not deleted.** Zero callers in `app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, `e2e/`, `scripts/` — every `/race/` hit is the page route. SKILL has recorded it unused since HO 178, so it is **pre-existing, not dragged here**; deleting it would have widened this commit past its own boundary. Its header comment still describes the drawer that this HO deleted, and that staleness rides with the filing.

**Docs (HO 658):** this block · backlog — the QUEUED default-variant entry **struck with its figures**, plus one new QUEUED line for the `/api/race/[id]/hub` orphan · oddities — one entry, that a perturbation instrument reports a vacuous "nothing moved" against a collapsed pane **and a spurious "everything moved" when the camera moves between readings**, so falsify the instrument in both directions before believing either · SKILL, alone behind the self-mod guard: the HO 178 popover and HO 230 containment passages become RECORD, the `getRace`/`getRaceCandidates`/`getMostCompetitiveRaces` entries stop describing the 2×2 hover-popover grid, the `/` entry's "4 competitive seats" is corrected to 6 (HO 642), and any `nowMs`/#418 consumer list naming the Strip or Block is corrected. **Notes now run through HO 658.**

**Also (HO 659), `race_candidates` gained one TEXT column and stopped being a table you can only ask questions about by diffing runs.** `updated_at`, added by `ensureColumn` in `main()` — never the CREATE array, per the `activity_count` (HO 597) rule the `migrate.ts:840`/`:918` comments state. **The semantics were pinned before any code, because the helpful deviation is the defect here.** It is **last write (reach)**, not last content change: the harvest DELETEs and re-derives, so an unchanged roster still re-stamps, and *what a run touched* is exactly the quantity HO 642 and HO 656 were approximating behaviourally. One `runStamp` per invocation is bound into every write of that run, so "which run touched this row" is a **GROUP BY, not a range guess**. **NULL means pre-instrumentation and is never backfilled with a fabricated time** — the 11 curated HO 171/174/182 rows read NULL today, and the behavioural bracket stays the record for pre-column history.

**Writers re-derived rather than trusted, and the count held: exactly two.** `backfill-race-challengers.ts`'s `INSERT OR IGNORE` and `seed-races.ts`'s upsert — the latter's `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` sets the stamp too, since a re-seed IS a write under the reach semantic. Every other `race_candidates` reference across `scripts/` and `lib/` is a SELECT; `backfill-races.ts` names the table only in a comment about what it does not touch. **No reads were added anywhere** — `lib/queries.ts` is untouched, and the first consumer is HO 660's staleness check.

**Both falsification legs ran, with figures.** Before: **217 rows / 151 seats**, sentinel **206 / 147**, curated **11**, sentinel content checksum `127aeac…`. **Post-migration non-NULL = 0** — the migration stamps nothing, proven rather than assumed. **Post-harvest: non-NULL 206, exactly the sentinel row count; ONE distinct stamp** across 206 rows / 147 seats; **checksum byte-identical** to the pre-migration capture with zero row-count drift, so nothing needed explaining as real-world movement. **Negative leg: the 11 curated rows are still NULL, count unchanged** — one reading that proves two things, that the migration did not stamp and that the harvest's skip-curated guard still holds with the new column in the statement. `POST /api/revalidate?tag=races` returned `{"ok":true}`; nothing reads the column yet and no app surface changed, so no further prod verification is owed.

**An incidental proof of the reach semantic, recorded because it was luck rather than design.** The harvest was run **twice** (the second time to capture its summary lines), and the pair is the semantic stated as data: **the stamp advanced to the second run's ISO while the content checksum did not move at all**. That is precisely "an idempotent rewrite stamps" — reach measured, content unchanged — and it also means the stamp now in the DB is the second run's, not the first's. The instrument is `scripts/diagnostic/race-candidates-stamp-659.ts`, left untracked beside its siblings; it prints the before-census, the checksum and the stamp census in one pass, so the three legs are diffs of one instrument rather than three hand-written queries.

**The cadence ruling goes onto the record here: the challenger harvest gets slot (b), its own daily cron slot.** HO 656 priced both shapes and recommended (b); this HO adopts it unchanged and for its stated reasons — the harvest is pure DB-to-DB with no Ballotpedia fetch, it is idempotent under its sentinel, and it therefore buys freshness without spending the primaries cron's 50s deadline or the per-unit cursor that makes a killed tick resumable. **The wiring is HO 660 and the backlog entry stays OPEN until it lands**, because that entry's close criterion is *chosen AND wired*: a ruling with no cron line is still a harvest that runs when a human remembers.

**Docs (HO 659):** this block · backlog — the `updated_at` entry **struck with its figures**, the harvest-schedule entry gaining the slot-(b) ruling while staying open, and the M3 re-measure entry **restated** (the 642-era 88/186 and 129/62/67 figures are superseded by HO 656's post-run 36, bucketed and summing; the trigger now waits for HO 660's first *scheduled* fire, and the detector is built only if the remainder past those buckets holds the ME shape) · **no oddities entry — nothing odd emerged**; the column behaved exactly as specified on both legs · SKILL, alone behind the self-mod guard: the `race_candidates` schema block gains the column with its semantic, and the `backfill:race-challengers` entry gains the per-run shared-stamp sentence. **Notes now run through HO 659.**

**Also (HO 660), the challenger harvest got a clock — and the one thing that would actually prove it cannot be forced.** `/api/cron/race-challengers`, daily at `30 12 * * *`, wiring the slot (b) ruled at HO 659. **The slot is derived, not picked:** the harvest's ENTIRE input is what `/api/cron/primaries` writes, that cron fires `0 0,12 * * *`, and US primary results post in the 00:00–06:00 UTC band — so the 12:00 tick is the one that reads them settled, and 12:30 harvests what it just wrote. **A winner marked by the 00:00 tick waits at most 12.5h**, which is the stated price of holding the ruling at ONE daily fire rather than quietly over-provisioning to two. 12:30 is clear of the weekday fmp half-hourlies (`0,30 13-21`) and collides with nothing in a table that went **16 crons → 17** (functions 15 → 16). **No cron-lock, stated as a decision so it doesn't read as a gap:** the primaries tick is deadline-bounded at 50s inside a 60s ceiling so it is finished by ~12:01, and nothing else writes `race_candidates` on a schedule — the seed is manual.

**The refactor shipped alone and its equivalence was MEASURED, not read.** A route cannot import a script's `main()`, so the harvest moved to `lib/harvest-challengers.ts` — the `runStamp` (inside the function, so one stamp per invocation regardless of caller), the sentinel DELETE, the `INSERT OR IGNORE … SELECT DISTINCT`, and the fill census, returning figures instead of printing them. **The SQL moved byte-identical, and the HO 659 stamp instrument is what checked that claim**: after the refactored run the sentinel content checksum was **`127aeac…`, identical to the pre-refactor reading**, with exactly **one fresh distinct stamp across all 206 sentinel rows** and the **11 curated rows still NULL**. That is the second HO in a row where the instrument built for one purpose paid for itself in another — a refactor that passes a content checksum plus a stamp census touched behavior not at all. The npm script survives as the manual path with what a scheduled fire has no use for (before-census, sample eyeball, flush reminder); wiring a clock did not retire the hand-run.

**Verified on prod at `5b08bdd`, with the guard fired rather than assumed.** Two negative controls first — **no header → 401** and **wrong secret → 401** — then the authorized fire: **200 in 643ms**, payload `cleared 206 · inserted 206 · rows 206 · races 147 · of 187 rated`. Read back three ways: `cron_runs` carries **exactly one row for the path, `success`, 643ms**, with the figures in its payload; the 659 instrument shows **one distinct stamp `2026-08-14T02:36:51.750Z` across 206 rows / 147 seats** — the route's own stamp, which is how we know the write went through the shared path and not some other — and curated **11 still NULL**; `/api/health` lists the route with `maxStaleMs 93600000` (26h, mirrored from the existing daily entries rather than invented), `lastStatus success`, `healthy true`, and the whole health payload green.

**Two tails, both open on purpose.** (1) **The `schedule` trigger is UNPROVEN and is not claimed.** The HO 504 precedent governs: a manual POST and a deploy-adjacent invocation prove the ROUTE, never the schedule. **WATCH: confirm the ~12:30 UTC `cron_runs` row tomorrow.** (2) **That row — not today's curl — arms the M3 re-measure**, exactly as the HO 659 restatement specifies, because the detector must calibrate against the maintained regime and a hand-fired route is still a harvest that runs when a human remembers. The two tails are one dependency: until the schedule proves itself, M3 stays unarmed.

**One drift observed and deliberately not fixed:** `CRON_ROUTES`'s primaries entry records `schedule: "0 12 * * *"` while `vercel.json` has `0 0,12 * * *`. The field is **display-only** — it is echoed into the health payload and plays no part in evaluation, which keys on `maxStaleMs` — so this is a cosmetic staleness in a neighbouring line, not a health bug, and correcting someone else's row inside a wiring commit is how a scoped diff stops being reviewable. Recorded here; it belongs to whoever next touches that table.

**Docs (HO 660):** this block · backlog — the harvest-schedule entry **struck**, both halves of its *chosen AND wired* criterion now true, carrying the slot, the route path, the verification figures and **one WATCH line for the first scheduled fire** (and the note that this fire is what arms M3) · the M3 restated entry **untouched**, its trigger text already naming HO 660's first scheduled fire · **no oddities entry — nothing odd emerged** · SKILL, alone behind the self-mod guard: the cron topology gains the slot, and the `backfill:race-challengers` entry records that both callers now share `lib/harvest-challengers.ts`. **Notes now run through HO 660.**
